Some grep hits for `ext.` are false positives, because the variables happen to be named `*ext`: - `ext.encode`/`ext.endswith`/`ext.lower` in `generator/job.py` are methods of `held_text`; - `ext.device`/`ext.shape`/`ext.index_*` in `modules/moe_batch_recon.py` are on a `*ext` tensor variable; - `ext.get` in `modules/module.py` and `model/model_tp_fn.py`. **Performance shape of this configuration** - Decode is correct but slow: - about 1270 EXL3 linears per token; - one host sync per MoE layer (`expert_count.tolist()`, `block_sparse_mlp.py:1104`); - about 360 small HC launches. - The obvious next steps, in order: - route `BC_LinearEXL3.run` to `exl3xpu_C.linear`; - replace the MoE decode loop with trellis-serve `moe_forward` (repack each expert into its blob; same tile bytes; the clamp convention differs by ≤ 4.5e-4, Part B); - fuse HC (`k_hcfuse.py` is the template, Part E); - optionally capture the decode step with `torch.xpu` graphs. --- ## 5. Per-op detail Each part keeps its own op table (call sites, conditions, recommended path), per-op signatures and math, a step-by-step forward, and its CUDA-dependency table. The recommended XPU configuration in section 3 takes precedence where a part discusses alternatives. --- ## Part A. EXL3 / FP16 linear layers and the quant kernels Paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3`. Python lives in `exllamav3/`; CUDA in `exllamav3/exllamav3_ext/`. ### 1.0 Checkpoint facts (verified against the HF safetensors headers of rev 332ab457, all 15 shards) These were read straight from the safetensors headers of `turboderp/GLM-5.3-Flash-exl3` (HTTP range requests; nothing downloaded under `/mnt/sandisk`). - **Codebook: every EXL3 tensor is `mul1`.** Each quantized linear ships `<key>.mul1` (I32 scalar, value `0x83DCD12D`), and the checkpoint has **zero `.mcg` tensors**. The value is only a flag: the multiplier is hard-coded in the kernels. - **Every EXL3 tensor uses an integer bitrate**, and none use the half-integer `frac` format. The model was converted with exllamav3 1.4.4 (`quantization_config.version`). Fractional trellis support only arrived in commit 07b8a2e (after v1.5.0). So `frac.cu`, `dq8_half`, and the `HALF=true` instances are never reached. - **No bias** on any linear. **No `su`/`sv` packed sign tensors**: only `suh`/`svh` (F16) are stored. - The bitrate is `K = trellis.shape[-1] / 16`: | tensor class | count | trellis shape `[in/16, out/16, 16K]` | K | |---|---|---|---| | routed experts gate/up `L.mlp.experts.E.{gate,up}_proj` | 12096 each | `[256, 128, 48]` | 3 | | routed experts down | 12096 | `[128, 256, 48]` | 3 | | dense MLP (layers 0-2) gate/up | 3 each | `[256, 768, 64]` | 4 | | dense MLP down | 3 | `[768, 256, 64]` | 4 | | shared expert gate/up/down | 42 each | (2048 wide) | 5 | | KDA `qkv_proj` (34 layers) | 34 | `[256, 1536, 80]` (4096 -> 24576) | 5 | | `o_proj` (all 45 layers) | 45 | KDA `[512,256,80]` (8192->4096); MLA `[1024,256,80]` (16384->4096) | 5 | | MLA `q_a_proj` 4096->1536, `q_b_proj` 1536->16384, `kv_a_proj_with_mqa` 4096->512, `indexer.wq_b` 1536->4096 | 11 each | | 5 | | `lm_head` 4096 -> 154880 | 1 | `[256, 9680, 96]` | 6 | - The following tensors are **FP16/BF16/F32 rather than EXL3**, and are loaded through `Linear.load_fp16` -> `LinearFP16` or read raw by their module: - KDA: `b_proj` [64,4096], `f_a_proj` [128,4096], `f_b_proj` [8192,128], `g_a_proj` [128,4096], `g_b_proj` [8192,128] (all F16); `conv1d.weight` BF16 [24576,1,4]; `A_log` F32 [64]; `dt_bias` F32 [8192]; `o_norm` BF16 [128]. - MLA: `kv_b_proj.weight` F16 [32768,512]; `indexer.wk` F16 [128,4096]; `indexer.weights_proj` F16 [32,4096]; `indexer.k_norm.{weight,bias}` F16 [128]. - MoE router: `mlp.gate.weight` F16 [288,4096] and `e_score_correction_bias` F32 [288]. - Hyperconnections: `hc_{attn,ffn}_{base,fn,scale}` F32 ([24], [24,16384], [3]). - Norms: BF16. `embed_tokens` BF16 [154880,4096]. - The k-pool aux tensors (`index_kpool_compress_{ape,gate}`) live in `kpool_aux.safetensors`. - **The checkpoint has no MTP layer** (`layers.45.*` is absent, even though `num_nextn_predict_layers = 1`). MTP would need a `convert_mtp.py` output placed next to the model. The model card also ships 1007 `model.visual.*` tensors, which text inference does not use. ### 1.1 Op table (this part) Hot-path rank: 1 is hottest. "BC path" means `LinearEXL3.forward` -> `BC_LinearEXL3.run_alloc`, or a C++ BC class of another slice (BC_GatedMLP, BC_BlockSparseMLP, BC_MLAttention, BC_GatedDeltaNet) calling `BC_LinearEXL3::run_gr` internally. | op | category | reached in | rank | Python call sites | condition / avoidable? | recommended XPU path | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | `BC_LinearEXL3` (ctor, `run`, `run_alloc`) | bound class | load (ctor); decode and small prefill (run) | 1 | ctor `modules/quant/exl3.py:86`, `modules/linear.py:474` (pin only); `run_alloc` `modules/quant/exl3.py:142`; held by other BC classes via `.inner.bc` (`modules/mlp.py:223-224`, `modules/gated_delta_net.py:647-658,704-717,778-794`, block_sparse_mlp, mla) | `rows = x.numel()/x.shape[-1] <= 144` (`AUTO_RECONSTRUCT_THRESHOLD`, `exl3.py:10,140`), or `infer_params.no_reconstruct`. **Not avoidable**: the decode path for every quantized linear. | Python class holding trellis/suh/svh/K/mcg/mul1/bias/xh (other parts' BC shims read these fields). `run` = `exl3_linear_ref` below; fast path = trellis-serve `torch.ops.exl3xpu_C.linear`. | | `exl3_gemm` (C++-internal, not called from Python) | EXL3 GEMM/GEMV | decode, prefill <= 144 rows | 1 | only via `BC_LinearEXL3::run_gr` (`libtorch/linear.cpp:38,44`) and other BC classes | Internal dispatch: int8-act GEMV (mul1, m <= 2, K <= 5 on non-Hopper) -> QTIP GEMV (K 2..4) -> cooperative tensor-core GEMM. All compute the same math. | One function `exl3_linear_ref(x, ...)`. Fast: exl3xpu `linear` / `exl3_gemm_small` (layout identical, see 1.4). | | trellis decode (`dq*` in `quant/exl3_dq.cuh` + `decode_3inst<2>` in `quant/codebook.cuh`) | device helper | everywhere | 1 | - | - | 64K-entry fp16 LUT plus a vectorized window extract (1.3). | | `reconstruct_had_slice` | dequant to original-basis W | prefill, rows >= 1024 | 2 | `modules/quant/exl3.py:200,212` | `rows > 144` and `rows >= 1024`, `in%128==0`, `out%128==0`, and `EXL3_NO_FUSED_RECONSTRUCT` unset (`exl3.py:14,181-189`). Avoid with `EXL3_NO_FUSED_RECONSTRUCT=1` (falls to reconstruct + had_r_128). | Force `EXL3_NO_FUSED_RECONSTRUCT=1` at first; or implement as `diag(suh) H W_hat H diag(svh)`. | | `hgemm_recon` | fp16 GEMM | prefill rows > 144 | 2 | `exl3.py:203,216` | always on the reconstruct path | `torch.matmul` with fp32 accumulation into `c` (strided column slice allowed) | | `had_r_128` | 128-point Hadamard + scales | prefill 145..1023 rows (unfused), also other parts | 3 | `exl3.py:195` (pre, suh), `exl3.py:219` (post, svh); `model/moe_cpu_host.py` (CPU offload, n/a) | unfused reconstruct path only | torch: `(x.view(-1,128) [* suh]) @ H128 / sqrt(128) [* svh]` (exact rounding in 1.2.4) | | `reconstruct` | dequant to Hadamard-domain W_hat | prefill 145..1023 rows; `get_weight_tensor` | 3 | `exl3.py:229` | unfused path, `out_features <= 32768` | LUT decode (1.3) | | `reconstruct_slice` | same, column slice | prefill 145..1023 rows, lm_head (N > 32768) | 3 | `exl3.py:231` | unfused path, `out_features > 32768` (lm_head only) | LUT decode on a tile-column range | | `BC_LinearFP16` (ctor, `run`) | bound class | load; any FP16 linear | 4 | ctor `modules/quant/fp16.py:54`, `modules/linear.py:470`; `run` called from other BC classes (BlockSparseMLP's `shared_gate`) | fp16 tensors listed in 1.0 | `y = x @ W (+bias)` in torch | | `hgemm` | fp16 GEMM (cuBLAS) | any FP16 linear whose out dtype != in dtype | 4 | `modules/quant/fp16.py:96` (plus `modules/conv.py`, `moe_cpu_host.py`, not on path) | `LinearFP16.forward` uses `torch.matmul` when `dtype == x.dtype`, else `ext.hgemm` | `torch.matmul(x.float(), w.float())` or `torch.mm(...).to(out)`; fp32 accumulate | | `exl3_gemv_int8_max_k` | query | load (mgemm fusion heuristic) | 5 | `model/config.py:76` | Only when `device.type == "cuda"` (`config.py:74`). **Never reached on XPU**: `K_thr` stays at env `EXL3_MGEMM_K_THRESHOLD` (default 6). | Do not implement; or return 5. | | `exl3_mgemm` | multi-matrix EXL3 GEMM | decode (fused gate/up, MoE, qkv) | 1 (owned by Parts B–D) | `modules/mlp.py:761`, `modules/attn.py`, `modules/gated_delta_net.py`, `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py`, `modules/sliding_attn.py`, `modules/dsv4.py` | see Part B; math summarized in 1.2.9 | loop of `exl3_linear_ref` over a pointer-to-tensor registry | | `softcap` | elementwise | never (GLM lm_head `softcap=0`) | - | `modules/linear.py:634` | `Linear.softcap != 0` | `y = tanh(x/s)*s` if ever needed | | `count_inf_nan` | stats | never (conversion capture only) | - | `modules/linear.py:569` | `"capture" in params` | skip | | `pinned_cuda_view` | host alias | never (vision tower pinning only) | - | `modules/linear.py:466` | `EXL3_VISION_PINNED=1` and the vision component | skip | | `reconstruct_batch`, `reconstruct_had_batch`, `had_r_128_batch`, `hgemm_batched` | batched expert recon | MoE prefill (`modules/moe_batch_recon.py`), owned by Part B | - | `moe_batch_recon.py` | Part B decides | per-expert loop of the 1.2 ops | | `g_get_cc`, `g_get_num_sms`, `exl3_gemm_shape_compat`, `exl3_gemm_num_kernel_shapes`, `exl3_gemv`, `hgemm_f16acc`, `hgemm_f16acc_status`, `had_paley`, `had_paley2` | queries / test entries | not reached from inference Python | - | none outside tests/conversion; `had_paley*` from `util/hadamard.py:67,102` for non-power-of-2 sizes only | H128 is Sylvester, built from `hadamard_1.txt` | stub (raise) | | `quantize_tiles*`, `pack_trellis*`, `unpack_trellis*`, `pack_signs`, `decode`, `test_distribution` | conversion | never | - | `modules/quant/exl3_lib/quantize.py` (imported at load, but not called) | conversion only | stub | #### Path selection inside `LinearEXL3.forward` (`modules/quant/exl3.py:119-144`) 1. `params["ovr"]` is an override hook (conversion); `params["reconstruct"]` is set only by conversion. 2. `rows = x.numel() // x.shape[-1]`. - If `rows <= 144` or `no_reconstruct`, it calls `self.bc.run_alloc(x, out_features, dtype == float)`, with `dtype = out_dtype or self.default_out_dtype` (`Linear.out_dtype` or fp16). - Otherwise it calls `reconstruct_hgemm`: - `use_fused = in%128==0 and out%128==0 and not EXL3_NO_FUSED_RECONSTRUCT and rows >= 1024`. This is always true for GLM chunks of 1024 to 8192 rows. - Fused: `reconstruct_had_slice` (`w` = original-basis W) followed by `hgemm_recon(x, w, y)`. - Unfused: `had_r_128(x -> xh, pre=suh)`, then `reconstruct[_slice]` (W_hat), then `hgemm_recon(xh, W_hat, y)`, then `had_r_128(y, y, post=svh)` in place. - `out_features > 32768` (only `lm_head`) is processed in 32768-column slices through a shared scratch `w_`. 3. Bias add (none in GLM). 4. `x` must be contiguous (asserted, `exl3.py:135`). With `-chunk_size 8192` and batch <= 8, decode always takes the BC path. Prefill chunks take the fused reconstruct path. A final short tail chunk of 145 to 1023 rows takes the unfused path, and <= 144 takes the BC path. `model.load` (autosplit dummy forwards) and `model.warmup` (`model/model.py:190`) exercise both paths at load. **lm_head** (`architecture/glm5_next.py:334-343`, K=6, `caps={"logits_output": True}`, `out_dtype=None`): - It returns **fp16 logits**. - The generator sets `params["last_tokens_only"] = 1` (`generator/job.py:1396`), and `model/model_ls.py:326-327` slices x to the last token before the head. So the head always runs at rows <= batch, on the BC path. - Only a full-sequence forward reconstructs it in 5 slices of 32768 columns. Examples are `serve.py` `teacher_forced` (`model.forward(ids, {"attn_mode": "flash_attn_nc"})`) and `perplexity`-style calls. `select_hq_bits` (GLM uses 1 or 2) is **conversion-only**: `conversion/allocation.py` raises the bitrate floor. It has no runtime effect. `InferParams.use_mgemm` (`model/config.py:59-77`) decides fused-mgemm against separate GEMMs in other slices. On XPU the `exl3_gemv_int8_max_k` probe is skipped, so with defaults: - **mul1 tensors stay fused when `K >= 6` or `out_features < 8192`**; - non-mul1 tensors are always fused. ### 1.2 Per-op detail #### 1.2.1 `BC_LinearEXL3` Binding: `exllamav3_ext/libtorch/linear_bc.h:13-35`. Struct: `libtorch/linear.h:64-99`. Implementation: `libtorch/linear.cpp:34-71`. - **ctor** `BC_LinearEXL3(trellis: int16 [k/16, n/16, 16K], suh: fp16 [k], svh: fp16 [n], K: float, bias: fp16 [n] | None, mcg: bool, mul1: bool, xh: Tensor)`. - `xh` is a scratch buffer for the transformed input at m == 1. Python passes `g_tensor_cache.get(device, (1, in_features), out_dtype)` (`exl3.py:84,94`). That buffer has dtype `Linear.out_dtype`, possibly `float32` or `None` (the torch default, float32). The kernel treats it as raw fp16 scratch, so any buffer with >= 2*k bytes works. - The object only stores the tensors. Other C++ BC classes read the `trellis/suh/svh/K/mcg/mul1` fields directly to build `exl3_mgemm` calls. **A Python shim must expose those attributes with these names.** - **`run(x, y) -> None`**: `x` fp16 `[..., k]` (2D in practice), `y` preallocated fp16 or fp32 `[..., n]` (out dtype selected by `y.dtype`). - If `x.numel() == x.size(-1)` (one row), it calls `exl3_gemm_gr(x, trellis, y, suh, xh, svh, -1, mcg, mul1, 0, graph)` with `A_had = self.xh`. - Otherwise it allocates `A_had = empty_like(x)` and calls `exl3_gemm` (a graph with bsz > 1 is a TORCH_CHECK error). - Then it adds `bias` in place if present (`add_gr`). - **`run_alloc(x, out_features: int, output_fp32: bool) -> Tensor`**: allocates `y = empty(x.shape[:-1] + (out_features,), fp32 if output_fp32 else fp16)`. It returns `y` immediately if `out_features == 0`, otherwise calls `run(x.view(-1,k), y.view(-1,n))` and returns `y`. - `run_gr(x, y, Graph*)` is not bound. It is used only by other BC classes' graph capture. #### 1.2.2 `exl3_gemm` (internal; also bound but unused from Python) Binding: `bindings.cpp:162` (`exl3_gemm(A, B, C, suh, A_had, svh, force_shape_idx, mcg, mul1, force_num_sms) -> int`). Host: `quant/exl3_gemm.cu:114-346`. Kernel: `quant/exl3_gemm_kernel.cuh:9-55`. Inner loop: `quant/exl3_gemm_inner.cuh`. Arguments: - `A` fp16 `[..., k]`, contiguous. `size_m = prod(A.shape[:-1])`. - `B` int16 `[k/16, n/16, 16K]` (trellis), with `size_n = B.size(1)*16`. - `C` fp16 or fp32 `[..., n]` (written). - `suh` fp16 `[k]`, `A_had` fp16 scratch with `>= m*k` elements, `svh` fp16 `[n]`. - Checks: `A.size(-1) == B.size(0)*16`, `C.size(-1) == B.size(1)*16`, A fp16, B int16. K is read back from `B.size(2)`: `K = B.size(2)/16`, and `B.size(2) % 16 == 8` means half-integer K. **Math** (identical across all three internal kernels, up to accumulation order): ``` A_had[r, :] = fp16( H128_blockwise( fp16(A[r,:] * suh) ) / sqrt(128) ) # per 128-block of k acc[r, :] = A_had[r,:] @ W_hat (fp16 x fp16 MMA, fp32 accumulate*) C[r, :] = H128_blockwise(acc[r,:]) / sqrt(128) * svh # per 128-block of n ``` - `W_hat` is the decoded trellis matrix `[k, n]` (1.3). - `H128` is the natural-order Sylvester matrix, `H[i,j] = (-1)^popcount(i&j)` (unnormalized, symmetric). - `suh` and `svh` already include the sign flips and channel scales. - The input transform runs in-kernel (`exl3_gemm_kernel.cuh:13-28`, `had_hf_r_128_inner<pre=true>`): `fp16(x*suh)` is `__hmul2` (fp16 rounding), the butterfly runs in fp32, the result is multiplied by `1/sqrt(128)` in fp32 and rounded to fp16. - The output transform (`exl3_gemm_inner.cuh:595-620`) works on the fp32 tile sum: - fp32 out (`had_ff`): butterfly fp32, `*1/sqrt(128)` fp32, `*float(svh)` fp32. - fp16 out (`had_fh`): butterfly fp32, `*r` fp32, round to fp16, then `__hmul2` by `svh` (fp16 rounding). - \*Accumulation details: - sm_86 only (`EXL3_GEMM_H_ACC`, `exl3_gemm_inner.cuh:16`) accumulates MMA in fp16 within a k-slice, then folds into fp32. - k-split partial sums go through global memory **in C's dtype**. For fp16 outputs the partials are rounded to fp16 (`read_sum_gl`/`write_sum_gl`). - This is numerical noise; the reference should accumulate in fp32. **Internal dispatch** (all CUDA-specific, none needs porting): 1. If `mul1 && exl3_gemv_int8_enabled()` (env `EXL3_INT8_GEMV`, **default 2 = on**), it tries `exl3_gemv_int8` (`quant/exl3_gemv_int8.cu:236-377`). Requirements: suh/A_had/svh present, `n%256==0`, `k%128==0`, `K <= exl3_gemv_int8_max_k(dev)` (5, or 6 on Hopper/Blackwell; env `EXL3_INT8_GEMV_MAX_K`), and `m <= 2` (plain mode) or `m == 1`. - It **quantizes the transformed activations to int8**: `q_s = max(|a|, 1e-8)/127` per k-slice (or per row), `i = clamp(rint(a/q_s), -127, 127)`. - Mode 1 adds a residual int8 with `q2 = q/254`. - It computes `y = k_inv*(q*acc1 + q2*acc2) + (1024*k_inv + k_bias)*(q*sum1 + q2*sum2)`, then the output Hadamard and svh. - **This changes numerics vs fp16** (about 0.9% output RMS in mode 2). It is taken at decode for GLM's K=3/4/5 mul1 tensors. - To compare XPU output against a CUDA reference, run CUDA with `EXL3_INT8_GEMV=0`. 2. `exl3_gemv_try_launch` (`quant/exl3_gemv.cu:92`): QTIP-style GEMV for `K in 2..4` (`cb != 0` unless K == 4), `m <= EXL3_GEMV_MAX_M`, k/n % 128. Env `EXL3_GEMV` mode (`exl3_gemv_env_mode`). 3. Otherwise a cooperative tensor-core GEMM. It is autotuned (`CoopKernelAutotuner`, keyed by shape, K, cc, SM count, cb), uses `cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel`, a `grid.sync`, and a global `locks` buffer from `DevCtx`. Return value: kernel tag (ignored by callers). #### 1.2.3 Trellis decode `decode_3inst<cb>` / `dq*` Sources: `quant/codebook.cuh:56-123` and `quant/exl3_dq.cuh`. See 1.3 for the full layout. Codebooks: - cb=2 `mul1` (**GLM**): - `x = state * 0x83DCD12D (mod 2^32)`. - `s = sum of the 4 unsigned bytes of x` (`__dp4a(x, 0x01010101, 0x6400)`). - `h = fp16 bits (0x6400 + s) = 1024 + s` (exact). - `w = fp16(h * 0.00676727294921875 + (-10.3828125))` with one rounding (`__hfma`; constants are the fp16 bit patterns `0x1eee` and `0xc931`). - The fp32 computation `(1024+s)*k_inv + k_bias` is exact (22-bit product, |sum| < 4 at lsb 2^-18), so `float32 -> fp16` round-to-nearest-even gives a bit-exact LUT. - Values lie in [-3.453, 3.348], with mean about 0 and std about 1.0. - cb=1 `mcg` (not used by GLM): `x = state * 0xCBAC1FED`; `x = (x & 0x8FFF8FFF) ^ 0x3B603B60` (lop3 0x6a); `w = fp16(lo16) + fp16(hi16)` (`__hadd`). - cb=0 `3INST` (not used): `x = state*89226354 + 64248484`, then the same lop3 and add. #### 1.2.4 `had_r_128` Binding: `bindings.cpp:160` (`had_r_128(input, output, pre_scale, post_scale, scale)`). Host: `quant/hadamard.cu:121-~210`. Inner: `quant/hadamard_inner.cuh:95-212`. - Arguments: `input` and `output` must have the same shape, 2D `[rows, cols]`, with `cols % 128 == 0`. Both are fp16 (`had_hf`) or both fp32 (`had_ff`). In-place is allowed (`output is input`). - `pre_scale` and `post_scale` are optional fp16 `[cols]`. **If both are given, only `pre_scale` is applied** (the if/else chain). Callers never pass both. - `scale` is a float; the effective factor is `r = scale/sqrt(128)` (`0.088388347648f`). - Math per 128-block: `v = x[blk] (* pre)`; `out[blk] = (H128 @ v) * r (* post)`. H128 is symmetric, so `x @ H == H @ x`. - fp16 path rounding: - `pre`: `__hmul2` in fp16. - Butterfly in fp32 (4-point in registers, then 5 xor-shuffle stages; lane bit set means `v = partner - v`). - `*r` in fp32, then round to fp16. - `post`: `__hmul2` in fp16. - fp32 path: scales are converted to float, with no intermediate rounding. - Grid `(rows, cols/128)`, 32 threads, each thread holding 4 consecutive elements. #### 1.2.5 `reconstruct` / `reconstruct_slice` Binding: `bindings.cpp:155,159` (`reconstruct(unpacked, packed, K, mcg, mul1)`, `reconstruct_slice(unpacked, packed, K, mcg, mul1, n_offset)`). Source: `quant/reconstruct.cu:139-200`. - `unpacked`: fp16 `[k, n_slice]`, written row-major. - `packed`: trellis int16 `[k/16, n_total/16, 16K]`. - `K` float. `mcg`/`mul1` bool select the codebook: `cbi = K-1 + (mcg ? 8 : mul1 ? 16 : 0)`, or `+24` for half-integer K. - Checks: `unpacked.size(0) == packed.size(0)*16`, `n_slice % 128 == 0`, `n_offset % 128 == 0`, and `n_offset + n_slice <= n_total`. - Output: `unpacked[r, c] = W_hat[r, n_offset + c]` (Hadamard domain, no suh/svh applied). - `reconstruct` is `reconstruct_slice` with `n_offset = 0` (the Python wrapper only calls it for the full width). - Grid: `(n_slice/128, k/16)`, 256 threads. Each warp decodes one 16x16 tile, and a block covers 16 rows x 128 columns. #### 1.2.6 `reconstruct_had_slice` Binding: `bindings.cpp:156` (`reconstruct_had_slice(unpacked, packed, suh, svh, K, mcg, mul1, n_offset)`). Source: `quant/reconstruct.cu:202-~360`. Python passes `svh[n_start:]` **and** `n_offset = n_start` (`exl3.py:212-213`): svh is pre-sliced, the trellis is offset in-kernel. - Output: original-basis weights `W[k, n_slice] = diag(suh) · Hblk · W_hat[:, slice] · Hblk · diag(svh_slice)`, where `Hblk` is block-diagonal `H128/sqrt(128)`. - The kernel works on 128x128 tiles in shared memory. The column butterfly runs **in fp16** (`__hadd2`) with `r` pre-multiplied per stage. The row butterfly's first 4-point stage is fp32, then fp16 shuffles. It finally multiplies `*suh[row]` and then `*svh[col]` in fp16. - This path is noticeably less precise than the unfused one, but within quantization noise. - `y = x @ W` is then exactly the linear's output (no had calls needed). It requires `k%128 == 0` and `n%128 == 0`. #### 1.2.7 `hgemm_recon` / `hgemm` Binding: `bindings.cpp:191,193` (`hgemm(a, b, c)`, `hgemm_recon(a, b, c)`). Sources: `hgemm.cu:18-107` and `hgemm_f16acc.cu:527-531`. - `a`: fp16 `[..., k]`. `b`: fp16 `[k, n]` (2D). `c`: fp16 or fp32 `[..., n]` (written). The last dim of `c` must be contiguous; **the row stride of `c` may be larger than n** (Python writes into `y_[:, n_start:n_end]` column slices). - Math: `c = a @ b`, computed in fp32 (`cublasGemmEx`, `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F`, alpha=1, beta=0, overwrite), then rounded to c's dtype. - `hgemm_recon` first tries `hgemm_f16acc_try` (fp16-accumulator tensor-core kernel; GeForce only; env `EXL3_HGEMM_F16ACC`), else calls `hgemm`. The semantics are the same. - XPU: `torch.matmul(a, b, out=c_view)` works for an fp16 `c` (oneDNN accumulates in fp32). For fp32 `c`, use `c.copy_(a.float() @ b.float())` or `torch.mm(a, b).float()`; the latter rounds through fp16 first, a minor deviation. #### 1.2.8 `BC_LinearFP16` Binding: `libtorch/linear_bc.h:1-10`. Struct: `libtorch/linear.h:45-62`. Implementation: `libtorch/linear.cpp:11-25`. - ctor `BC_LinearFP16(weight: fp16 [in, out] (already transposed at load), bias: fp16 [out] | None)`. - `run(x, y)`: if `x.dtype == y.dtype` (and no graph), `at::matmul_out(y, x, weight)`; otherwise `hgemm(x, weight, y)` (fp32 out). Then `y += bias`. `LinearFP16.forward` (`modules/quant/fp16.py:69-100`) does the same in Python: `torch.matmul` when the out dtype equals the x dtype, else `ext.hgemm`. Load notes: - `Linear.load_fp16` transposes checkpoint `[out, in]` to `[in, out]` (`transposed_load=True`) and pads dims to multiples of 128 (`pad_to=128`). - KDA's `b_proj` is 64-wide, which pads to 128; `f_b_proj` has in=128. - MoE `gate.weight` (288) pads to 384, with `trim_padded_out` per the module. - Float biases are converted to fp16. FP8/E8M0 dequant paths are not used by GLM. #### 1.2.9 `exl3_mgemm` (core math only; call sites belong to Parts B–D) Binding: `bindings.cpp:185-190`. Host: `quant/exl3_gemm.cu:351-730`. Kernel: `quant/exl3_gemm_kernel.cuh:65-343`. Arguments: - `A` fp16 `[a_batches, m, k]`. `B`, `suh`, `svh` are **int64 tensors of device addresses**, one entry per matrix (trellis ptr, suh ptr, svh ptr). - `C` fp16 or fp32 `[c_batches, m, n]`. `A_had` is scratch for `bszm*m*k` elements. - `indices` int64 `[*, num]` (optional), `weights` fp16 (optional), `K` float, `force_shape_idx`. - `mcg`, `mul1` are uint32 in C++ (non-zero means true). - `min_index`, `max_index`, `force_num_sms`, `num_tokens`, plus the optional `size_n_list`, `c_ptrs`, `n_stride_list`, `had_src_list`, `num_had_src`. Math: for each active slot j: - `q = indices[j]` (or j); slots with `q < 0` are skipped. - `C[j] = exl3_linear(A[j or 0], B[q], suh[q], svh[q]) * (weights[j] if weights)`. - If `weights` is given, `C[t*m*n..]` receives the sum over the `stride = bszm/num_tokens` slots of each token. Accumulation is fp32, or **fp16 `__hadd` for fp16 C**. - `min_index`/`max_index` filter and rebase the indices. **Shim implication:** the pointer tables cannot be dereferenced in torch. They are built in `modules/multilinear.py` and `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:637-657`. The shim needs a `data_ptr() -> Tensor` registry, filled in `BC_LinearEXL3.__init__` and when `LinearEXL3` tensors are created, or those Python sites must be patched to keep tensor lists. #### 1.2.10 Minor ops - `softcap(x, y, s)` (`softcap.cu`): `y = tanh(x/s)*s` (fp32 math, fp16 or fp32 storage). Not used by GLM. - `count_inf_nan(x, counter)`: conversion only. - `pinned_cuda_view(t, device)` (`cuda_host.cpp:75-92`): `cudaHostGetDevicePointer` alias of a pinned CPU tensor. Vision only. On XPU, use USM host memory, or skip it. - `exl3_gemv_int8_max_k(device) -> int`: `EXL3_INT8_GEMV_MAX_K` env, else 6 on Hopper/Blackwell, else 5. ### 1.3 EXL3 data layout (exact) **Tensors per linear `<key>`:** - `trellis` int16 `[in/16, out/16, 16*K]`. Tile `(tk, tn)` covers W rows (input dim) `16tk..16tk+15` and columns (output dim) `16tn..16tn+15`. - `suh` fp16 `[in]`, `svh` fp16 `[out]`. - `mul1` I32 scalar flag (or `mcg`), optional `bias` fp16 `[out]`. - Old `su`/`sv` int16 sign bitfields (`LinearEXL3.unpack_bf`) are absent here. **Dequantized weight** (`modules/quant/exl3.py:239-249`; quantizer `exl3_lib/quantize.py:1641-1645`): ``` W[in, out] = diag(suh) · Hblk · W_hat · Hblk · diag(svh) Hblk = blockdiag(H128 / sqrt(128)) y = x @ W = ( Hblk( (x ∘ suh) ) @ W_hat ) Hblk ∘ svh (row-vector x; H symmetric) ``` H128 is Sylvester from `hadamard_1.txt` (`util/hadamard.py:34-42,107-131`). There is no Paley step at 128, and no random permutation. The 1/sqrt(128) normalization applies on each side. **Bit stream of a tile:** - A tile is 256 weights at K bits each, giving `256K` bits = `16K` int16 = `8K` uint32. - Reading the int16 array as little-endian uint32 (`word[i] = u16[2i] | u16[2i+1] << 16`, which is how every kernel loads it) gives a **MSB-first** bit stream. Bit `b` is bit `31 - b%32` of `word[b//32]`. - The packer (`quant/pack.cu:9-57`) writes 16-bit words MSB-first and then `SWAP16`s each uint32 pair. - Position `t` (0..255) owns the K bits ending at stream bit `(t+1)K`. - Its **16-bit state** is the window of stream bits `[(t+1)K-16, (t+1)K)`, read MSB-first and **circular** within the tile (tail-biting). For t=0 the window starts in the last word. - Decoded value: `w = codebook(state)` (1.2.3). **Position to (row, col) inside the 16x16 tile** (tensor-core fragment order; `tensor_core_perm`, `exl3_lib/quantize.py:22-44`; `reconstruct.cu:42-80`): ``` row (input/k) = 8*((t>>1)&1) + 2*((t>>3)&3) + (t&1) col (output/n) = (t>>5) + 8*((t>>2)&1) ``` **Torch reference** (self-checked in numpy against the packer and `tensor_core_perm`): ```python MUL1 = 0x83DCD12D def mul1_lut(dev): # [65536] fp16, bit-exact with decode_3inst<2> st = torch.arange(65536, dtype=torch.int64) x = (st * MUL1) & 0xFFFFFFFF s = (x & 255) + ((x >> 8) & 255) + ((x >> 16) & 255) + ((x >> 24) & 255) return ((1024 + s).float() * 0.00676727294921875 - 10.3828125).half().to(dev) # exact in fp32, 1 rounding def _pos_tables(K, dev): t = torch.arange(256, device=dev) e = (t + 1) * K; ih = (e - 1) // 32; sh = 32 * (ih + 1) - e; il = (ih - 1) % (8 * K) row = 8 * ((t >> 1) & 1) + 2 * ((t >> 3) & 3) + (t & 1) col = (t >> 5) + 8 * ((t >> 2) & 1) return il, ih, sh, row, col def reconstruct_w_hat(trellis, K, lut, n0=0, n1=None): # == ext.reconstruct_slice tk, tn, _ = trellis.shape tr = trellis[:, n0 // 16 : (n1 or tn * 16) // 16] words = tr.contiguous().view(torch.int32).to(torch.int64) & 0xFFFFFFFF # [tk, tn', 8K] il, ih, sh, row, col = _pos_tables(K, trellis.device) st = (((words[..., il] << 32) | words[..., ih]) >> sh) & 0xFFFF # [tk, tn', 256] tiles = torch.empty(*st.shape[:2], 16, 16, dtype=torch.half, device=trellis.device) tiles[..., row, col] = lut[st] return tiles.permute(0, 2, 1, 3).reshape(tk * 16, -1) # [in, n'] def had128(x, pre=None, post=None): # == ext.had_r_128 (fp16 in/out) H = sylvester128(x.device, torch.float32) / math.sqrt(128) v = x if pre is None else x * pre # fp16 multiply (rounded) y = (v.float().view(-1, 128) @ H).view(x.shape).half() return y if post is None else y * post def exl3_linear_ref(x, trellis, suh, svh, K, lut, out_dtype): xh = had128(x.reshape(-1, x.shape[-1]), pre=suh) acc = xh.float() @ reconstruct_w_hat(trellis, K, lut).float() # or chunk over n y = (acc.view(-1, 128) @ (sylvester128(x.device, torch.float32) / math.sqrt(128))).view(acc.shape) y = y * svh.float() if out_dtype == torch.float else (y.half() * svh) return y.view(*x.shape[:-1], -1).to(out_dtype) ``` Memory note: W_hat for one expert (4096x2048 fp16) is 16 MB. lm_head is 1.27 GB fp16, so reconstruct it in column slices as the CUDA path does (32768 columns). ### 1.4 Comparison with the existing XPU kernels (`~/work/trellis-b70/trellis-serve/xpu`) The layout is **identical**, so the kernels can be reused directly: - `csrc/exl3_esimd.h:4-8` documents the same MSB-first stream and the same "window ending at (t+1)K, circular". - It uses the same `row = 8*((t>>1)&1) + 2*((t>>3)&3) + (t&1)` and `col = (t>>5) + 8*((t>>2)&1)`. - It reads the int16 trellis tensor as uint32 words. - `decode_cb_h<2>` (`exl3_esimd.h:40-80`) uses the same `*0x83DCD12D`, `dp4a(0x6400, x, 0x01010101)`, and fp16 fma `0.00676727294921875 / -10.3828125`, and claims bit-exactness. - The input Hadamard also rounds `x*suh` to fp16 first (`exl3_esimd.h:300`). Differences and adapter work: - **Op signature.** The op is `torch.ops.exl3xpu_C.linear(x, trellis, suh, svh, shard_of_nb, group_bounds, K, cb, small_m_max, slice_n)` (`csrc/exl3_ops.sycl:651-757`). - `suh` is `[S, k]`, one row per fused shard: pass `suh.view(1, -1)`. - `shard_of_nb` is int32 `[n/128]` of zeros. `group_bounds = [0, n]`. `cb = 2`. - **Coverage.** `exl3_supported` covers `cb==2, K in {3,4,5,6}` (`exl3_ops.sycl:767-774`), exactly GLM's set. `exl3_reconstruct` also has K=2 and some cb 0/1. - **Output dtype** is always `x.dtype` (fp16 or bf16). exllamav3 often requests **fp32 outputs** (`out_dtype=torch.float` on o_proj, down, and other parts). Cast afterwards, or extend the op; there is a small precision difference, since CUDA never rounds the fp32 output to fp16. - **Prefill.** Above `small_m_max`: `had_in_rm` -> `exl3_reconstruct` slices -> `at::matmul_out` (fp16 y) -> `exl3_had_out_h`. This equals exllamav3's *unfused* path; there is no fused `reconstruct_had_slice` equivalent, and none is needed. - **Optional modes.** - `exl3_set_int8(1)` enables a W8A8 prefill (int8 weights and activations, K=4/6 only), off by default. - `exl3_set_fused` covers K=4/6 small-m fused decode. - Triton fallbacks exist in `exl3xpu/triton_kernels.py` (`reconstruct`, `had_in`, `had_out`, `gemm_small`). - **Not covered by the XPU kernels:** `exl3_mgemm` (multi-matrix, pointer tables, weighted reduce). The grouped-MoE `moe_forward` in `csrc/exl3_moe.sycl` uses its own pointer-table/blob format (Part B). ### 1.5 Non-ext CUDA dependencies in this part | file:line | what | XPU mapping | |---|---|---| | `modules/linear.py:458` | `if device.type != "cuda": return` in `pin_linears` | Already a no-op on XPU; Or implement it too; it is two `had128` calls around `reconstruct_w_hat`. 3. `BC_LinearFP16` (ctor, `run`) and `hgemm`. 4. A `data_ptr -> tensor` registry so the Part B-4 `exl3_mgemm` shim can resolve pointer tables. 5. Stubs that raise for everything else in the table. --- ## Part B. Dense MLP, MoE (routed + shared expert), routing, activations > **Reconciliation note (see section 3).** This part's phase-1 advice says "don't provide `BC_LinearEXL3`". That is not possible: `LinearEXL3.__init__` constructs `ext.BC_LinearEXL3` unconditionally (`modules/quant/exl3.py:86`), and `LinearEXL3.forward` calls `self.bc.run_alloc`. With a Python `BC_LinearEXL3`, `GatedMLP.load_local` builds `ext.BC_GatedMLP` for the dense MLPs and the shared experts even under `no_reconstruct` (`mlp.py:655-687`; `multi_gu` is None, so `can_separate` holds). The shim therefore provides a small Python `BC_GatedMLP` whose `run_bszN(x, d)` runs gate/up `run_alloc`, `silu_mul(g, u, a, 10)` and `down.run(a, d)`. Everything else in the `no_reconstruct` plan stands: no `BC_BlockSparseMLP`, no `MultiLinear`, no `exl3_mgemm`, and the torch per-expert loop for every MoE batch size. Decode batch is 1–4 under `-ambs 4` (section 1), not 1–8. Paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3/exllamav3/` unless stated. "ext" means `exllamav3_ext`. ### GLM-5.3-Flash facts that select the paths | item | value | where it matters | |---|---|---| | dense MLP layers | 0, 1, 2 only (`mlp_layer_types`; layer 44 is `sparse`) | `GatedMLP`, hidden 4096 -> 12288 -> 4096, `select_hq_bits=1` | | MoE layers | 3..44 (42 layers) | `BlockSparseMLP`, 288 experts, top-8, I = 2048 | | shared expert | 1, `GatedMLP` 4096 -> 2048 -> 4096, `select_hq_bits=2` | merged into the routed sum | | router | `router_type="dots"` -> `routing_dots` -> `ext.routing_ds3_nogroup` (sigmoid, noaux_tc bias, `norm_topk_prob`, x2.5) | every MoE layer, every token | | activation | `silu`, `act_limit = swiglu_limit = 10.0`, `interm_dtype=torch.half`, `out_dtype=torch.float` | `ext.silu_mul` / fused kernels | | expert codebook | mul1, K = 3 for routed experts (glm-flash-lite `docs/reference.md:444` "MUL1/K=3"); shared expert and dense MLP may carry higher K (`select_hq_bits`) | `support_fused` (mul1 uniform) and `mtile_ok` are True | | `f_threshold` | `min(288 // 8, 4) = 4` (`block_sparse_mlp.py:190,736`) | only matters when `self.bc is None` | | MLP input / output | input `(bsz, q_len, 4096)` fp16 (output of `post_attention_layernorm`, `transformer.py:177`); output fp32 to `mlp_hc.apply_` (`transformer.py:189`) | | | CPU expert offload | off: `-mcl`/`-mcs` default 0 and serve.py never passes them (`model_init.py:66-68`, `glm53/serve.py:43`); also gated on `device.type == "cuda"` (`block_sparse_mlp_cpu.py:113,151`) | `block_sparse_mlp_cpu.py`, `model/moe_cpu_host.py` are unreachable | glm-flash-lite's MoE monkeypatches (`exl3_tiers`, `expert_cache`, `cpu_tier`, `k_overlap`, `k_hcfuse`, `k_lookahead`) are only installed when their `GLM53_*` env vars are set (`glm53/serve.py:51-104`). Else shim: two EXL3 linears on the same input | | `BC_BlockSparseMLP.run_single_expert_dq` | per-expert prefill | prefill experts with `count > 512` (above batched-recon `max_rows`) | 8 | `block_sparse_mlp.py:1284` | `self.bc` set and `count > TEMP_ROWS_GRAPH(32)` and not fused/batched | shim: reconstruct + matmul (expert math below) | | `reconstruct_had_batch` | batched recon | prefill experts with `256 < count <= 512` | 9 | `moe_batch_recon.py:199` | `EXL3_MOE_BATCH_RECON=1` (default) and `EXL3_MOE_RECON_FOLDED=1` (default); `EXL3_MOE_BATCH_RECON=0` avoids the whole tier | shim: reconstruct per expert, then fold (Part A's reconstruct) | | `hgemm_batched` | batched GEMM | same as above | 9 | `moe_batch_recon.py:188` | same | `torch.bmm` with fp32 accumulation | | `reconstruct_batch`, `had_r_128_batch` | batched recon (unfolded) | only with `EXL3_MOE_RECON_FOLDED=0` | n/r | `moe_batch_recon.py:202,185,191` | off by default | not needed | | `exl3_moe_max_concurrency` | device query | load, when `support_fused` | load | `block_sparse_mlp.py:729` | only sizes `(C, R, H)` temp buffers | shim: return a small constant (for example 4) | | `BC_BlockSparseMLP.run_single_expert` | graphed per-expert | not reached for GLM (fused tier takes every count <= 256, so `min_rows = 256`) | n/r | `block_sparse_mlp.py:1260` | only when `fused_mode_buffers is None` | stub that raises | | `add_sigmoid_gate`, `add_sigmoid_gate_proj` | shared gate | not reached (no `shared_gate` key in GLM) | n/r | `block_sparse_mlp.py:1361,1363` | | not needed | | `gelu_mul`, `relu2_mul`, `relu_mul`, `silu_oai_mul`, `xielu` | activations | not reached (activation is silu) | n/r | `mlp.py:595-596`, `block_sparse_mlp.py:397-411`, `moe_batch_recon.py:55-61` (dict built at import) | import-time attribute access only in `moe_batch_recon.py:55-61` and `mlp.py`/`block_sparse_mlp.py` match statements | the shim must expose the names (import-time dict); bodies can raise | | `routing_std`, `routing_sel_norm`, `routing_gemm_det`, `routing_ds3_nogroup_logits`, `routing_std_logits`, `blocksparse_mlp_routing`, `exl3_moe_coop` (Python), `BC_MLP`, `moe_split_*`, `moe_unswizzle_trellis`, `exl3_moe_cpu_*`, `exl3_moe_flag_*`, `exl3_gemv_int8_max_k`, `add` | n/r | not reached for this model on XPU (`exl3_gemv_int8_max_k` is only called when `device.type == "cuda"`, `model/config.py:72-76`) | n/r | | | not needed | Internal C++ dependencies of the bound classes (not called from Python, but part of what the shim's BC classes replace): `exl3_moe_coop_prepare/run` (`quant/exl3_moe_coop.cu`), `exl3_gemm_gr`, `exl3_mgemm_gr` (`quant/exl3_gemm.cu`), `had_r_128`, `had_r_128_dual`, `reconstruct`, `hgemm_recon` (used by `run_single_expert_dq`, `libtorch/blocksparse_mlp.cpp:491-524`), `silu_mul_gr`, `routing_gemv` and `routing_gemm_det_` (inside `routing_ds3_nogroup`), CUDA `Graph` (`graph.cuh`), `CoopKernelAutotuner` (`quant/coop_autotune.cu`), `DevCtx` (num_sms, locks). ### Recommended XPU strategy for this part **Phase 1 (correct, not fast): set `config.infer_params.no_reconstruct = True` and don't provide `BC_LinearEXL3` in the shim.** Set it after `Config.from_directory` and before `model.load` (`model_init.py:194`). `InferParams.no_reconstruct` is a plain attribute (`model/config.py:17`). Effects in this part: - `BlockSparseMLP.load_local` builds no `MultiLinear`s, no `BC_BlockSparseMLP`, no `fused_mode_buffers` (`block_sparse_mlp.py:527,533,612`). `forward` takes the "Torch/C++/fused" branch for every batch size (`:1052-1055`, `no_reconstruct` is one of the OR terms). Inside it, `fused_mode_buffers is None`, `_batch_recon_layer` returns None (`bc is None`), so every expert with rows runs the **torch per-expert path** (`:1288-1304`): `ups[e].forward`, `gates[e].forward`, `ext.silu_mul(g, u, a, 10.0)`, `downs[e].forward`, `mul_(w)`, `index_add_`. - `GatedMLP.load_local` builds no `multi_gu` (`mlp.py:635`), and with no `inner.bc` the BC is never built (`mlp.py:655`). Every row count takes `mlp.py:746-755`: gate Linear, up Linear, `silu_mul`, down Linear. - The only ext ops left in this part are **`routing_ds3_nogroup`, `det_quant_weight`, `silu_mul`**, plus the EXL3 `Linear.forward` from Part A (with `no_reconstruct`, `quant/exl3.py:140` always takes the non-reconstruct branch). - Cost: one host sync per MoE layer (`expert_count.tolist()`, `block_sparse_mlp.py:1104`) and up to 3 x 288 Linear calls per layer at prefill. That is acceptable for correctness. **Required Python patch regardless of path:** `block_sparse_mlp.py:818` runs `cpu_post_load()`, `load_local()` and `load_routing()` only when `torch.device(device).type == "cuda"`. On XPU none of them run, so `routing_cfg` stays None and the first forward crashes in `routing_dots`. Change it to `in ("cuda", "xpu")`, or monkeypatch. **Phase 2 (fast decode):** drop `no_reconstruct` for the MoE (or keep it and monkeypatch `BlockSparseMLP.forward` for `bsz <= 8`) and route decode to an XPU grouped kernel: - The trellis-serve `moe_forward` (`~/work/trellis-b70/trellis-serve/xpu/csrc/exl3_moe.sycl:1183`) is the natural fit. Its requirements: mul1, uniform K in {3, 4} for gate/up/down, `H % 128 == 0`, `I % 128 == 0`, E <= 1024. - Repack exllamav3's per-expert tensors into one blob per expert (`moe_offload.py:81 pack_expert`): - gate|up trellis concatenated along the n-tile axis: `torch.cat([gate.trellis, up.trellis], dim=1)` gives `(H/16, 2I/16, 16K)` int16, viewed as uint32 words in PLANAR4 order; - then the down trellis `(I/16, H/16, 16K)`; - then fp16 `suh_g[H] suh_u[H] svh_g[I] svh_u[I] suh_d[I] svh_d[H]`. - The tile bytes are identical (16·K int16 = 8·K uint32 per 16x16 tile), so it is a repack, not a re-quantization. - Convert `selected_experts` int64 to int32 and `routing_weights` fp16 to fp32. Its output has x's dtype (fp16): cast to fp32 and add the shared expert separately. - **Activation clamp differs (see below):** the n128 build clamps the gate *before* SiLU, sglang style, while exllamav3 clamps `silu(g)` *after*. The difference is at most `10 - silu(10) ≈ 4.5e-4` on the activated gate. It is not bit-exact. If exactness to exllamav3 matters, change `EXL3_ACT_CLAMP` to clamp after SiLU. - The b70tier n128 file (`glm-flash-lite/b70tier/kernels/moe-n128/exl3_moe.n128.sycl`) is the same kernel plus that clamp (`-DEXL3_ACT_LIMIT=10.0f`). - Prefill: `moe_forward` has a DPAS path (`moe_prefill` when `M >= g_prefill_min_m`). Alternatively keep the torch per-expert loop. ### (b) Per-op detail #### Expert math (shared definition used below) Let `lin(x, L)` be the EXL3 linear of Part A: `lin(x) = Hn( Hk(x ⊙ suh) @ W ) ⊙ svh`, where: - `Hk`/`Hn` are block-diagonal normalized Hadamard-128 transforms along the feature axis (scale `1/sqrt(128) = 0.088388347648`); - `W` is the decoded trellis (k x n, fp16); - `suh` is (k,) fp16 and `svh` is (n,) fp16. This matches `run_single_expert_dq` (`libtorch/blocksparse_mlp.cpp:491-524`: `had_r_128(y, yh, suh, -)`, `hgemm_recon`, `had_r_128(out, out, -, svh)`) and the coop kernel epilogues (`quant/exl3_moe_coop_kernel.cuh:772-799,920-923`: had128 then `* svh`; down input `* d_suh` then had128). Expert e on input row x (fp16, width 4096): ``` g = lin(x, gate_e) # (2048,) ; fp16 in the separate/torch paths, fp32 inside the coop kernel u = lin(x, up_e) a = min(silu(g), L) * clamp(u, -L, L) # L = 10.0; a rounded to fp16 d = lin(a, down_e) # (4096,) fp32 (down Linear out_dtype float) ``` Routed sum for token t: `R_t = Σ_{k=0..7} w_{t,k} · d_{e_{t,k}}(x_t)` (fp32). MoE output: `R_t + S(x_t)`, where `S` is the shared-expert GatedMLP (same formula, its own weights, fp32 out). No shared gate, no post norms, no latent projections, no biases, `interm_div = 1`. #### `silu_mul(x, y, z, act_limit)` (`activation.cu:24-100`, `activation_kernels.cuh:140-260`) - **Signature:** `silu_mul(Tensor x [gate], Tensor y [up], Tensor z [out, in place OK], float act_limit)`. - `x` and `y` are both fp16 (kernel `act_mul_kernel_h`) or both fp32 (`act_mul_kernel_f`). `z` must be fp16. - Any shape. Elementwise over `numel`, processed as pairs (`numel/2` threads × 2), so numel must be even. Contiguous. - **fp16 kernel (GLM, interm_dtype half):** all in **half arithmetic**: - `s = x * hrcp(1 + hexp(-x))` (h2exp/h2rcp); - if `act_limit != 0`: `y = clamp(y, -L, L)` (`__hmax2`/`__hmin2`) and `s = min(s, L)`; - `z = s * y` (`__hmul2`), fp16, no finite clamp. - **fp32 kernel:** `s = x * (1/(1+__expf(-x)))` in fp32; same clamps in fp32; `z = half_rn(s*y)` then clamped to ±65504. - **Clamp semantics:** the gate is clamped *after* SiLU, from above only; the up lane is clamped symmetrically. `act_limit == 0.0` disables both. - **Torch reference:** `z = (torch.clamp_max(F.silu(x.float()), L) * y.float().clamp(-L, L)).half()`. Computing in fp32 is the coop kernel's precision; the fp16 kernel differs by fp16 rounding only. - Called with `z` aliasing `y` (`mlp.py:749-750` when `interm_dtype == half`; `moe_batch_recon.py:283`). #### `routing_ds3_nogroup(hidden, gate, scores, bias, topk_indices, topk_weights, scaling_factor, gate_t, act_fn, gate_i8, gate_sb)` (`routing.cu:649-710`) **Arguments:** | arg | dtype / shape | role | |---|---|---| | `hidden` | fp16 `(bsz, 4096)` | router input `z = y` (no `router_pre_norm`) | | `gate` | fp16 `(4096, 288)` | the routing Linear's `inner.weight`, loaded with `float2half=True`, transposed (`linear.py:212`) | | `scores` | fp16 `(bsz, 288)` | out: logits | | `bias` | optional fp16 `(288,)` | from `_esb_h`; see below | | `topk_indices` | int64 `(bsz, 8)` | out | | `topk_weights` | fp16 `(bsz, 8)` | out | | `scaling_factor` | float | 2.5 | | `gate_t` | fp16 `(288, 4096)` | contiguous transpose | | `act_fn` | int | `ROUTING_ACT_SIGMOID = 0` | | `gate_i8`, `gate_sb` | `(2, 288, 4096)` int8 / `(288,)` fp32 | from `det_quant_weight` | - **`bias` construction:** `e_score_correction_bias` is loaded with `allow_bf16` and becomes `.float()` unless already fp16 (`block_sparse_mlp.py:785-795`). `_esb_h` (`block_sparse_mlp_routing.py:43-51`) mean-centers it (`esb - esb.mean()`) and casts to fp16. Selection is shift-invariant, so this is equivalent to the unshifted bias. - **Outputs:** - for bsz 1: the persistent `cfg.*_bsz1` buffers; - for bsz <= 128: `g_tensor_cache.get_bucketed` workspaces **shared by all MoE layers on the device** (consumed before the next layer routes); - otherwise fresh tensors (`block_sparse_mlp_routing.py:19-37`). - **Step 1, logits** (`routing_gemv`, `routing.cu:243-281`): `scores = half_rn(hidden @ gate)`. - bsz 1 with `gate_t`: one warp per expert; fp32 FMA dot over `half2` pairs, lane-strided, `__shfl_down` tree reduce, then rounded to fp16. - bsz > 1 with `gate_i8`: `routing_gemm_det` (`routing_gemm.cu`). This is an exact int8 hi/lo split GEMM: activations quantized per (row, 128-wide K chunk) to |q| <= 16319, weights per row; fp32 combine; fp16 out. Its accuracy is that of an fp16 tensor-core GEMM. - Fallback: `hgemm`. - On XPU, `(hidden.float() @ gate.float()).half()` is equivalent up to rounding. The point of these kernels is cross-arch bit-reproducibility for TP, which we don't need. - **Step 2, top-k** (`routing_ds3_nogroup_topk_kernel`, `routing.cu:284-339`). One block per row, `ceil(E/32)*32` threads. Per expert i: - `act_i = sigmoid(float(scores_i))`, using `sigmoid_stable_hf` (`routing.cu:19-25`): `ez = exp_det(-|x|); base = ez/(1+ez)` (correctly rounded div), returning `1-base` for x >= 0 and `base` otherwise. `exp_det` is a FMA-only polynomial with input clamped to [-87, 88] (`det_gemm.cuh:124-139`). - `key_i = act_i + float(bias_i)` and `payload_i = act_i` (with no bias: `key = logit`, `payload = logit`, and sigmoid applied after selection). - **Top-8 by key, descending**, via warp 0 (`warp_topk_shared`, `routing.cu:88-134`): K rounds of argmax. Within a lane, the strict `>` keeps the lowest position. Across lanes, the lowest tied lane wins (sm80 `__reduce_max_sync` + `__ffs(__ballot)`; the shfl_down fallback also keeps the lower lane). **Tie-break:** among exactly equal keys, the expert with the smallest `index % 32` wins first, then the smallest `index / 32`. This is not "lowest index", but exact fp32 ties are practically nonexistent. Output slot k = k-th best (descending key order). - `sum = Σ_{k<8} payload_k` (warp tree reduce of the first K lanes) `+ 1e-20f`. - `topk_weights[k] = half_rn(payload_k * scaling_factor / sum)`. - **Weights come from the un-biased sigmoid scores.** The bias is used for selection only. Normalization is over the 8 selected; epsilon 1e-20 is added to the sum; scaling (2.5) is applied after normalization. - **Torch reference:** ```python logits = (z.float() @ gate.float()).half() s = torch.sigmoid(logits.float()) key = s + bias_h.float() idx = torch.topk(key, 8, dim=-1, sorted=True).indices w = s.gather(-1, idx) w = (w * 2.5 / (w.sum(-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-20)).half() ``` Returns `(idx (int64), w (fp16))` and also writes `scores`, `topk_indices`, `topk_weights` in place, since bsz 1 and decode graphs reuse those buffers. Order of the k slots matters only for summation order. - HF reference difference: the HF `noaux_tc` computes `scores_for_choice = sigmoid(logits) + bias` with fp32 logits. exllamav3 rounds logits to fp16 first. Keep fp16 rounding for parity with CUDA exllamav3. #### `det_quant_weight(w, w_i8, w_s)` (`routing_gemm.cu:330-372`) - `w`: fp16 `(N, K)` contiguous (`gate_t`, N=288, K=4096). `w_i8`: int8 out `(2, N, K)`. `w_s`: fp32 out `(N,)`. - Per row: `scale = max|w|` (1 if 0), `inv = 16319 / scale`, `q = clamp(round_rn(w*inv), ±16319)`, `hi = (q+64) >> 7`, `lo = q - hi*128`, `w_s = scale/16319`. - Only consumed by `routing_gemm_det_`. A shim that computes logits with torch can make this a no-op. The tensors are pre-allocated by Python at `block_sparse_mlp_routing.py:84-85`. #### `BC_BlockSparseMLP` (`libtorch/blocksparse_mlp_bc.h`, `.h`, `.cpp`) **Constructor** (`block_sparse_mlp.py:666-719`). Positional order exactly as bound (`blocksparse_mlp_bc.h`): | # | args | |---|---| | 1-10 | `yh2` fp16 `(max(64, 64), Hi)`, `yh` fp16 `(64, 1, Hi)`, `interm_gu` fp16 `(max(64,128), I)`, `interm_g` `(64,1,I)`, `interm_u` `(64,1,I)`, `interm_a` fp16 `(64,1,I)`, `interm_a2` fp16 `(64, I)`, `out_d` fp32 `(64,1,Ho)`, `out_d2` fp32 `(64, Ho)`, `out_d_sh` optional fp32 `(1, 8, H)` | | 11-16 | `coop_ctr` int32 `(64*(I/128) + 8*(Ho/128) + 2*64 + 3,)` zeroed, `had_u` fp16 `(64, Hi)`, `dq_temp_up` fp16 `(Hi, I)`, `dq_temp_down` (view `(I, Ho)` of the same storage), `min_expert`, `max_expert` (both -1 = no shard) | | 17-34 | gate/up/down each as `ptrs_trellis`, `ptrs_suh`, `ptrs_svh` (int64 device tensors `(288,)` of `data_ptr()`s), `K` (float), `mcg` (bool), `mul1` (bool) | | 35-37 | `act_silu`, `act_gelu`, `act_silu_oai` | | 38-40 | `shared_experts` (`BC_GatedMLP` or None), `shared_gate` (`BC_LinearFP16` or None; None for GLM), `act_limit` | | 41-46 | `gates`, `ups`, `downs` as lists of `BC_LinearEXL3`; `gu_trellis_ptr`, `gu_suh_ptr`, `gu_svh_ptr` (int64 `(288, 2)`, [gate, up] per expert) | | 47 | `out_bszn` fp32 `(8, H)` | | keyword | `gate_bias_ptrs=None`, `up_bias_ptrs=None`, `down_bias_ptrs=None`, `act_relu2=False`, `sh_coop=bool` | Notes on the constructor: - With H = Hi = Ho = 4096, I = 2048, `numex = 8`, `bszn_rows = 64`, `TEMP_ROWS_GRAPH = 32`. - `g_tensor_cache` keys are device-global: every MoE layer shares the same `yh`/`interm`/`out_bszn` buffers. - `sh_coop` is `shared_coop_ok()` = True for GLM (`block_sparse_mlp.py:1394-1417`; env `EXL3_MOE_SHARED_COOP=0` disables it). - Read-only attribute: `sh_coop`. **`run_bszN(y, selected_experts, routing_weights)`:** - Inputs: `y` fp16 `(bsz, 4096)`, bsz 1..8; `selected_experts` int64 `(bsz, 8)`; `routing_weights` fp16 `(bsz, 8)`. - Returns nothing. Writes `out_bszn[:bsz]` = routed sum + shared expert (fp32). **Python reads `experts_cfg.out_bszn[:bsz]` afterwards** (`block_sparse_mlp.py:1320`) and skips the Python shared-expert add (`bc_sh_exp`), so the shim must write into the constructor's `out_bszn` tensor and must include the shared expert. - Shared expert: if `sh_coop`, a one-expert coop launch at the shared expert's own K writes `out_d_sh[:, :bsz]`. Otherwise `shared_experts.run_bszN(y.unsqueeze(0), out_d_sh[:, :bsz])`. - Routed launches (`quant/exl3_moe_coop_kernel.cuh`): - **rot** (bsz > 1): `had_g/had_u[slot] = Hk(x_row ⊙ suh)`. - **A**: per (expert run, 32-col group, projection) trellis GEMV. On the last arrival per (slot, 128-chunk), sum the split-k partials, apply had128 · svh to g and u, compute `act_gate` in fp32 (`min(silu(g),L)·clamp(u,±L)`, `:113-129`), multiply by d_suh, apply had128, and store fp16 `act_out`. - **B**: down GEMV, then per (token, chunk): for k = 0..7 in order (top_k 8 <= 16 warps, so warp k owns slot k), `o += w_k · (had128(d_out) · d_svh)`. Then sum the warp partials in warp order and `o += 1.0 · sh_out[row]` (gate 1, no `sh_gate_w`). Store fp32, trimmed to H. - A slot with `w == 0` or an out-of-range expert is inactive (contributes 0). Duplicate experts across tokens are grouped into runs (weights read once). A token with no active slots gets zeros plus shared. - No CUDA graph on this path (`blocksparse_mlp.cpp:74-79`). **`run_single_expert(y, expert_idx)`:** `y` fp16 `(count<=32, H)`. Graphed gate/up `exl3_gemm`, `silu_mul`, down into `out_d2[:count]`, which Python reads (`block_sparse_mlp.py:1261`). **Not reached for GLM.** **`run_single_expert_dq(y, expert_idx, yh, interm, interm_a, out)`:** | arg | dtype / shape | role | |---|---|---| | `y` | fp16 `(count, H)` | input rows | | `yh` | fp16 `(2*count, H)` | scratch | | `interm` | fp16 `(2*count, I)` | [gate rows; up rows] | | `interm_a` | fp16 `(count, I)` | activation | | `out` | fp32 `(count, H)` | written in place | - Math: `yh1 = Hk(y⊙suh_g)`, `yh2 = Hk(y⊙suh_u)`, `interm1 = yh1 @ recon(gate)`, `interm2 = yh2 @ recon(up)` (hgemm_recon: fp16 in, fp16 out, fp32 accumulate), `interm{1,2} = Hn(·)⊙svh`, `interm_a = silu_mul(interm1, interm2, L)` (fp16 kernel), `interm_a = Hk(interm_a⊙suh_d)` (in place), `out = interm_a @ recon(down)` (fp32 out), `out = Hn(out)⊙svh_d`. - Python then does `out_state.mul_(w)` and `index_add_` (`block_sparse_mlp.py:1303-1304`). - Note the Python sizing quirk: `out_state_` is a slice but the call passes the full `out_state`, so the C++ side uses `y.size(0)` rows. #### `exl3_moe(...)` (`quant/exl3_moe.cu:69-341`, kernel `quant/exl3_moe_kernel.cuh`) **Positional args:** | # | arg | dtype / shape | notes | |---|---|---|---| | 1 | `hidden_state` | fp16 `(rows, H)` | | | 2 | `output_state` | fp32 `(rows, H)` | zero-initialized `fhs_ext[:rows]` | | 3 | `expert_count` | int64 `(E+1,)` | bincount; last = sentinel | | 4 | `token_sorted` | int64 `(rows*8,)` | | | 5 | `weight_sorted` | fp16 `(rows*8,)` | | | 6-9 | `temp_state_g`, `temp_state_u` | fp16 `(C, R, H)` | | | | `temp_intermediate_g`, `temp_intermediate_u` | fp16 `(C, R, I)` | | | 10 | `act_function` | int | 0 = silu | | 11-13 | `K_gate`, `K_up`, `K_down` | float | | | 14-22 | 9 pointer tables | int64 `(E,)` | | | 23-28 | 6 codebook bools | | must be uniform; mcg xor mul1 | | 29 | `act_limit` | | | | 30 | `num_active` | | | | 31 | `output_scratch` | optional fp32 `(slots, H)` | | | 32 | `fused_base` | optional int64 `(E+1,)` | `tables[0]` | | 33-35 | `count_lo`, `count_hi`, `m_tile` | | | **Semantics.** For every expert e with `count_lo <= count_e <= count_hi` and `0 < count_e <= R` (R = `temp_state_g.size(1)`, here 256), process its rows `token_sorted[start_e : start_e+count_e]` (`start_e = cumsum(count)[e-1]`) with expert math: - Gate/up input had is `had_hf_r_128_inner` on fp16. - g/u GEMM out fp16. Activation in **half arithmetic** with the same clamp order (`hadamard_inner.cuh:355-411`). Down input had fp16. - Down output: `had(·) · (0.0884·w_row) · svh_d` in fp32. - With `output_scratch`: stored to `output_scratch[fused_base[e] + row_in_expert]`. Without it: `atomicAdd` into `output_state[token]`. The `m_tile` (16/32/64) and `num_active` parameters only pick kernel instances and launch width; they don't change math beyond accumulation-order rounding. Python issues one launch per tier (`block_sparse_mlp.py:1207-1224`): - if `rows*8 <= 256` (rows <= 32): `run_fused(-1)` with `expert_count_list = None` and no host sync; - else per-tier launches with `[1,16]`, `[17,32]`, `[33,256]` (m_tile 16/32/64). #### `exl3_moe_gather(output_state, output_scratch, flat_expert, inv_order, expert_start, slot_base, slot_kind, weight_sorted)` (`quant/exl3_moe.cu:376-504`) - All index tensors are int64. `output_state`/`output_scratch` are fp32; `weight_sorted` is fp16. - Shapes: `flat_expert`/`inv_order` `(rows*8,)`; tables `(E,)` (Python passes `tables[1|0|2, :288]`). - For each token t and column c: - `slots = [(slot_base[e] + inv_order[a] - expert_start[e], wt) for k in 0..7, a = t*8+k, e = flat_expert[a], 0 <= e < E, slot_kind[e] != 0]`; - `wt = 1` for kind 1 (fused, already weighted), `wt = weight_sorted[inv_order[a]]` for kind 2 (batched recon, unweighted); - `output_state[t,c] += Σ_k scratch[slot_k, c] * wt_k`, summed in k order in fp32. #### `exl3_mgemm` (GatedMLP fused gate+up only) (`quant/exl3_gemm.cu:347-678`) **Bound signature:** `(A, B, C, suh, A_had, svh, indices, weights, K, force_shape_idx, mcg, mul1, min_index, max_index, force_num_sms, num_tokens=1, size_n_list=None, c_ptrs=None, n_stride_list=None, had_src_list=None, num_had_src=0)`. Returns the int shape index. **General semantics:** `B`, `suh`, `svh` are int64 device tables of addresses (trellis / suh / svh per matrix). Active slot j uses `q = indices[j]` or j, and computes `C[j] = lin(A[j or 0], q)`: - `a_batches == 1` broadcasts A. - With `weights`, `C[0] = Σ_j weights[j] · C[j]`. - `min_index >= 0` filters to `[min, max)` and rebases. - Negative indices are skipped. - Sliced mode (`size_n_list`/`c_ptrs`/`n_stride_list`/`had_src_list`) is for equal-width column slices of several source matrices written in place to per-slice output pointers. Used by attention, not here. `A_had` scratch needs `bszm * m * k` fp16. `k % 16 == 0`, `n % 128 == 0`. Kernel shape is autotuned with cooperative launch. **Call in this part** (`mlp.py:761-777`): - `A = xv` fp16 `(1, rows, 4096)`, `B = ptrs_trellis` `(2,)` [gate, up], `C = gu` fp16 `(2, rows, 2048)`, `suh`/`svh` tables `(2,)`, `A_had = guh` fp16 `(2, rows, 4096)`. - `indices = weights = None`, `K = multi_gu.K`, `force_shape_idx = -1`, mcg/mul1 as stored, `min = max = -1`, `force_num_sms = 0`, `num_tokens = 1`. - So it is just `C[0] = lin(x, gate)` and `C[1] = lin(x, up)`. Reached only for the shared expert at 9-32 rows. #### `BC_GatedMLP` (`libtorch/mlp_bc.h`, `mlp.h`, `mlp.cpp`) **Constructor** (`mlp.py:672-687`): `(guh fp16 (2,8,4096), gu interm (2,8,out_f), a fp16 (1,8,out_f), down_xh fp16 (1,8,out_f), gu_ptrs_trellis|None, gu_ptrs_suh|None, gu_ptrs_svh|None, gu_K float, gu_mcg, gu_mul1, act_silu, act_gelu, act_relu2, gate BC_LinearEXL3|None, up BC_LinearEXL3|None, down BC_LinearEXL3, act_limit)`. - Requires either the fused tables or both gate and up handles. - Dense MLP (K < 6, out 12288 >= 8192): separate gate/up. - Shared expert: fused tables. **`run_bszN(x, d)`:** - `x` fp16 `(1, n, 4096)` with n = 1..8; `d` fp32 `(1, n, 4096)` written in place. - Math: `g, u = lin(x, gate), lin(x, up)` (fp16 out); `a = silu_mul(g, u, L)` (fp16 kernel); `d = lin(a, down)` (fp32). No biases. - CUDA graph per n (`graph_bszN[8]`): records on the 2nd call, replays on the 3rd+, patching A/C pointers. #### `exl3_moe_max_concurrency(device)` (`quant/exl3_moe.cu:15-19`) Returns `num_sms / 8`. Python uses it only to size `fused_mode_buffers` `(C, R=256, H|I)` fp16 x 4 (`block_sparse_mlp.py:729-735`). #### `hgemm_batched(a, w, c)` and `reconstruct_had_batch(W, ptrs, suh_ptrs, svh_ptrs, K, mcg, mul1)` (batched-recon tier) - **`hgemm_batched`** (`hgemm.cu:109-...`): `a` fp16 `(B, m, k)`, `w` fp16 `(B, k, n)`, `c` fp16|fp32 `(B, m, n)`, all contiguous. `c = a @ w` with fp32 accumulation; on GeForce the fp16-accumulator variant with fp32 accumulation across K chunks. - **`reconstruct_had_batch`**: `W` fp16 `(B, k, n)` out; `ptrs`, `suh_ptrs`, `svh_ptrs` int64 `(B,)` addresses. Writes `W[b]` = the folded dense weight such that `x @ W[b] == lin(x, expert b)` (suh, Hadamards and svh folded in, rounded to fp16). See Part A for `reconstruct` / `reconstruct_had_slice`. - **`run_group`** (`moe_batch_recon.py:208-311`): 1. gather rows padded to cmax (pads read a zero row and write a sink row); 2. `u = x @ Wu` (fp16), `g = x @ Wg` (fp16); 3. `silu_mul(g, u, u, L)`; 4. `d = u @ Wd` into the fp32 slot scratch, unweighted (kind 2), summed by `exl3_moe_gather`. - Tier bounds for GLM: `RECON_TILES=64` gives `max_rows = max(128, 64*128*128 // min(2048, 4096)) = 512`; `cap = min(16, (256 << 20) // (2*(max(4096*2048, 2048*4096) + 4096*2048))) = min(16, 8) = 8` experts per group; `PAD_MAX = 1.1`. ### (c) MoE layer forward, step by step (`BlockSparseMLP.forward`, `block_sparse_mlp.py:964-1377`) Input `x` fp16 `(b, q, 4096)` gives `y = x.view(-1, 4096)` and `bsz = b*q` rows. 1. **Routing** (`:997-998`): `selected (bsz,8) int64, weights (bsz,8) fp16 = routing_dots(...)` (ext `routing_ds3_nogroup`). During warmup (`params["tp_warmup"]`, set by `model.warmup`) these are overwritten with random experts and weights 1/8 (`:1018-1023`). 2. **Path choice** (stock flags; `bc` built, `support_fused`, mul1): - **bsz <= 8 (decode, batch 1-8):** `bc.run_bszN(y, selected, weights)` gives `out_bszn[:bsz]`, which already includes the shared expert (`:1317-1321`). No host sync, no graph. - **bsz > 8 (prefill chunks, up to 8192 rows; also bsz 9-32 multi-token verify):** 1. `fhs = zeros(bsz+1, 4096) fp32`. 2. `flat_token = arange(bsz).repeat_interleave(8)`; `order = flat_expert.argsort()`; `token_sorted`, `weight_sorted`; `expert_count = bincount(flat_expert, minlength=289)`. 3. If `bsz*8 <= 256`: all fused, no host sync. Otherwise `expert_count.tolist()` (host sync). 4. Tiers by row count c: - `0 < c <= 256`: `exl3_moe` (fused, m_tile 16/32/64 by c); - `256 < c <= 512`: batched recon groups; - `c > 512`: per-expert `run_single_expert_dq` (count > 32, so the DQ branch). 5. With `EXL3_MOE_FUSED_DET=1` (default), the fused and batched tiers write per-assignment fp32 slots and `exl3_moe_gather` sums them per token in k order. Per-expert DQ experts `index_add_` afterwards, in expert-index order. 6. Shared expert (`:1352-1365`): `self.shared_experts.forward(x)` (GatedMLP): - 9-32 rows: `exl3_mgemm` gate+up; - > 32 rows: separate Linears; - then `silu_mul` and down (fp32), and `fhs += y_shared`. - **With `no_reconstruct` (recommended phase 1):** the bsz > 8 structure for every bsz, but every expert takes the torch path: `Linear` forwards plus `silu_mul`, weighted with `mul_(w)` (fp16 weight broadcast onto fp32), then `index_add_` in expert order. The shared expert is added by the Python `+=`. 3. **Output:** fp32 `(b, q, 4096)` = routed + shared. Note that the decode path returns a **view of the device-shared `out_bszn` buffer**. The caller (`mlp_hc.apply_`) consumes it before the next MoE layer overwrites it. **Accumulation dtype:** fp32 throughout the routed sum and the shared add. Intermediate gate/up/activation are fp16 (fp32 inside the coop decode kernel before the fp16 store of the activation). Routing weights are fp16. **Dense MLP (layers 0-2)** `GatedMLP.forward` (`mlp.py:721-792`): - `rows <= 8` with `bc`: `BC_GatedMLP.run_bszN`. - Otherwise (multi_gu None for dense): gate Linear (fp16 out), up Linear (fp16 out), `silu_mul(g, u, u, 10)`, down Linear (fp32 out). - One slice (12288 < `MAX_MLP_INTERMEDIATE` 55296). ### (d) Non-ext CUDA dependencies in this part | file:line | what | reached? | XPU mapping | |---|---|---|---| | `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:818` | `if torch.device(device).type == "cuda": cpu_post_load(); load_local(); load_routing()` | **yes, load** | must accept `"xpu"`, otherwise `routing_cfg` is None and the first forward crashes | | `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:888` | `autosplit_extra_measure` returns early unless `device.type == "cuda"` | load (autosplit) | harmless; loses the worst-case prefill VRAM reservation. Accept `"xpu"` if autosplit under-reserves | | `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:1104` | `expert_count.tolist()` host sync per MoE layer (prefill with > 32 rows; every bsz under `no_reconstruct`) | yes | works on XPU (sync) | | `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:1164`, `modules/moe_batch_recon.py:243` | `torch.from_numpy(...).to(dev, non_blocking=True)` from pageable memory | prefill | works on XPU | | `modules/block_sparse_mlp.py:1606-1611`, `modules/mlp.py:435,911` | `consumer.recv(cuda=True)`, `torch.cuda.synchronize()` in `tp_import` | no (TP only) | not needed | | `model/config.py:72-76` | `use_mgemm`: `device.type == "cuda"` then `ext.exl3_gemv_int8_max_k` | load | on XPU the branch is skipped and `K_thr = 6` (`EXL3_MGEMM_K_THRESHOLD` default); fine | | `modules/block_sparse_mlp_cpu.py:19-20,73,113,151` | `torch.cuda.Event`, `torch.cuda.synchronize`, `type == "cuda"` gates | no (offload off; gated on cuda) | not needed | | `model/moe_cpu_host.py` (whole file) | `cudaHostRegister`, flags, streams | no | not needed | | C++ (inside BC classes) | CUDA graphs (`BC_GatedMLP::graph_bszN`, `BC_BlockSparseMLP::graph_single`), cooperative launches (`exl3_mgemm`, `exl3_moe` group barriers via `DevCtx` locks), completion counters/atomics (coop kernels), cuBLAS (`hgemm_batched`, routing fallback), `cudaFuncSetAttribute` | stock path | replaced wholesale by the shim. Optional later: `torch.xpu` graph capture if available in the installed torch; not required | No `torch.compile`, flash-attn, xformers, pinned memory or explicit streams in `mlp.py`, `block_sparse_mlp.py`, `block_sparse_mlp_routing.py`, `moe_batch_recon.py` or `multilinear.py`. `util/tensor.py:162-163` uses `is_pinned()` for non_blocking copies (generic). ### Part B summary - **Minimal op set for a correct forward from this part:** with `no_reconstruct=True`, no `BC_LinearEXL3` in the shim, and the `:818` cuda-check patch, only `routing_ds3_nogroup`, `det_quant_weight` (may be a no-op) and `silu_mul` are needed. The shim must also expose the names `gelu_mul`, `relu2_mul`, `relu_mul`, `silu_oai_mul`, `xielu` because they are referenced at import/construct time (`moe_batch_recon.py:55-61`, `mlp.py:593-596`, `block_sparse_mlp.py:392-411`). Plus Part A's EXL3 Linear forward. - **Stock path (no flags):** `BC_BlockSparseMLP` (ctor, `run_bszN`, `run_single_expert_dq`), `BC_GatedMLP` (ctor, `run_bszN`), `exl3_moe`, `exl3_moe_gather`, `exl3_mgemm`, `exl3_moe_max_concurrency`, `hgemm_batched`, `reconstruct_had_batch`, plus the three above. - **Surprises:** - (1) The `type == "cuda"` gate at `block_sparse_mlp.py:818` silently skips routing setup on XPU. - (2) Router logits are rounded to fp16 before the sigmoid. - (3) `act_limit` clamps `silu(g)` after the activation, while the trellis-serve n128 SYCL kernel clamps g before SiLU. The difference is small but the paths are not bit-equal. - (4) Decode returns a view of a device-global static `out_bszn` buffer shared by all MoE layers. A shim `run_bszN` must write it in place and include the shared expert. - (5) Top-k tie-break is lane-major, not index-major. - (6) `run_single_expert` (graphed) is unreachable for GLM. Prefill uses fused (<= 256 rows), batched-recon (257-512) and DQ (> 512) tiers. - **Cross-slice:** `lin()` / `reconstruct` / `had_r_128` / `BC_LinearEXL3` / `exl3_gemm` semantics and the `no_reconstruct` effect on `quant/exl3.py:140` are Part A's. The `Graph` class is Part E's. The MLP input (fp16, from `mlp_norm` after `mlp_hc.mix`) and output (fp32, into `mlp_hc.apply_`) are Part E's HC. Warmup (`model.warmup`, `tp_warmup` random routing; `-nw` skips it) is Part E's. --- ## Part C. KDA linear attention (GatedDeltaNet in KDA mode) and recurrent state Paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3/exllamav3`; C++/CUDA paths are relative to `exllamav3_ext/`. ### Checkpoint facts (shard-1 safetensors header, rev 332ab457, layer 0) These come from an HTTP range read of the header of `model-00001-of-00015.safetensors` (rev 332ab457). | tensor | dtype | shape | notes | |---|---|---|---| | `self_attn.qkv_proj.{trellis,suh,svh,mul1}` | I16 / F16 / F16 / I32 scalar | trellis `[256, 1536, 80]` (K/16, N/16, 16·5 bits), suh `[4096]`, svh `[24576]` | EXL3 5 bpw (select_hq_bits=2 bump), mul1 codebook.
