The torch KDA path calls `Linear.forward` 7 times per layer. ### (b) Per-op detail #### `cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule(mixed_qkv, g, beta, recurrent_state, core_attn_out, num_k_heads, num_v_heads, k_head_dim, v_head_dim, slots, history)` Source: `gdn.cuh:42-55` (decl), `gdn.cu:851-1031` (host), `gdn.cu:660-848` (`cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule_kernel_128<save_history, V_SPLIT, CHANNELWISE=true>`); binding `bindings.cpp` `m.def("cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule")`, positional with no kwargs. Arguments (all must be contiguous; the Python KDA branch calls `.contiguous()`): - `mixed_qkv`: bf16 `[bsz, seqlen, F]`. Row layout `[q(H·128) | k(H·128) | v(Hv·128)]`. For head h, q is at `h*128`, k at `(H+h)*128`, and v at `2H*128 + h*128`. - `g`: fp32. KDA (channelwise) is selected by `g.dim()==4`, shape `[bsz, seqlen, Hv, Dk]`, holding the natural-log decay per k-channel. KDA requires Dk = Dv = 128. - `beta`: bf16 `[bsz, seqlen, Hv]`. - `recurrent_state`: fp32 `[num_slots, max_history+1, Hv, Dk, Dv]`. Element `S[k][v]` is at `k*128 + v`, so v is contiguous. **In place.** - `core_attn_out`: bf16 `[bsz, seqlen, Hv, Dv]`. **Output, in place.** - `slots`: optional int32 `[bsz]` mapping each batch row to a state slot (identity if None). In the KDA call the Python passes `recurrent_slots`, an int32 device tensor. - `history`: bool. Launch: grid `(bsz, Hv, v_split)`, block `(128 / v_split, 4)` (`SUBK=4`). `v_split = 4` iff bsz==1 (and Dk≤128, Dv==128, Hv≤64). The batch-1 split only partitions V columns, so the math is identical either way. `scale = 1/sqrtf(Dk)`. Per token s, per head h (state read from `R` and written to `W`; see the history addressing below): ``` q = float(q_bf16); k = float(k_bf16) # length 128 q = q * rsqrtf(sum(q*q) + 1e-6); k = k * rsqrtf(sum(k*k) + 1e-6) # fp32, eps inside the sqrt a = __expf(g[s,h,:]) # fast exp, per k-channel decay factor dot1[v] = sum_k k[k] * a[k] * R[k][v] # read OLD state, decayed per k (fixed order: 4 k-slices of 32, summed slice 0..3) vprime[v] = float(v_bf16[v]) - dot1[v] W[k][v] = R[k][v] * a[k] + k[k] * vprime[v] * float(beta[s,h]) # fp32, written in place o[v] = sum_k q[k] * W[k][v] # read NEW state out[s,h,v] = __float2bfloat16_rz(o[v] * scale) # NOTE: round-toward-zero to bf16 (truncation), scale = 1/sqrt(128) ``` In matrix form: `S' = diag(exp g) S`, `u = v − Sᵀ'k`, `S = S' + β·k uᵀ`, `o = (Sᵀ q̂)/√Dk`, where q̂ and k̂ are L2-normalized and β multiplies after the subtraction. The reference `torch_recurrent_kda` (`gated_delta_rule.py:64-85`) computes the same thing but scales q before the dot and rounds the output with RN. Expect last-bit bf16 differences from the RZ rounding only. History addressing (`save_history=true`, i.e. `history=True`): `slot_state = state[slot]`, and `final = slot_state[0]`. - Token 0 reads `final`. Token s>0 reads `slot_state[s]`. - Token s<seqlen-1 writes `slot_state[s+1]`. The last token writes `final`. - Result: `state[slot,0]` is the state after the last token, and `state[slot,i]` (1≤i≤seqlen−1) is the state after token i−1. - Non-history: read and write `state[slot,0]` in place. - The host asserts `state.size(1) >= seqlen` when history is set. Torch reimplementation (exact semantics except the fast-exp and summation-order ULPs): ```python def recurrent_kda(mixed_qkv, g, beta, state, out, H, Hv, Dk, Dv, slots, history): B, S, _ = mixed_qkv.shape q, k, v = mixed_qkv.float().split([H*Dk, H*Dk, Hv*Dv], -1) q = q.view(B,S,H,Dk); k = k.view(B,S,H,Dk); v = v.view(B,S,Hv,Dv) q = q * torch.rsqrt((q*q).sum(-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-6) k = k * torch.rsqrt((k*k).sum(-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-6) a = g.exp(); bt = beta.float() idx = slots.long() if slots is not None else torch.arange(B, device=state.device) for b in range(B): sl = idx[b] Sst = state[sl, 0].clone() # [Hv, Dk, Dv] for t in range(S): Sst = Sst * a[b,t].unsqueeze(-1) # decay rows (k) u = v[b,t] - torch.einsum('hk,hkv->hv', k[b,t], Sst) Sst = Sst + bt[b,t,:,None,None] * k[b,t].unsqueeze(-1) * u.unsqueeze(-2) o = torch.einsum('hk,hkv->hv', q[b,t], Sst) * Dk**-0.5 out[b,t] = o.to(torch.bfloat16) # CUDA uses RZ; use RN or emulate RZ via view-int truncation if history and t < S-1: state[sl, t+1] = Sst state[sl, 0] = Sst ``` Vectorize over b for decode, since all rows have seqlen 1. Note: the CUDA kernel reads the old (undecayed) state and folds `a[k]` into dot1. This is algebraically identical to decaying first. #### `cuda_causal_conv1d_update(x, conv_state, slots, weight, bias, out, activation, history)` Source: `gdn.cuh:158-168`, `gdn.cu:1320-1515` (`conv1d_update_kernel<ACT,HISTORY>`). Binding name `cuda_causal_conv1d_update`, positional. Arguments: `x` bf16 `[bsz, F, seqlen]` (channels-major, made by `qkv.transpose(1,2).to(bf16).contiguous()`); `conv_state` bf16 `[num_slots, F, state_size]`, **in place**; `slots` int32 `[bsz]` or None (identity); `weight` bf16 `[F, K]` (K ≤ 16, here `conv1d_weight_flat` = `conv1d.weight.squeeze(1)`, shape `[24576, 4]`); `bias` bf16 `[F]` or None (None for GLM); `out` bf16 `[bsz, seqlen, F]`, **output in place, transposed to token-major**; `activation` (Python passes True = SiLU); `history`. Math, per channel d, with `in = concat(conv_state[slot,d,0:K], x[b,d,0:seqlen])`: `out[b,s,d] = bf16_rn(silu( bias[d] + Σ_{k=0..K-1} w[d,k]·in[s+k+1] ))`. It accumulates in fp32 via fmaf in k order 0..K-1, starting from the bias, and uses `silu(a) = a / (1 + __expf(-a))`. So `w[d,K-1]` multiplies the current token and `conv_state[slot,d,0]` (the oldest of K stored inputs) is never used. This is identical to `F.conv1d(cat(state[:,:,:K], x), w[:,None,:], groups=F)[..., -seqlen:]` (`conv1d.py:341-361`). State write-back: - Non-history: `conv_state[slot,d,0:K] = in[seqlen : seqlen+K]`, the last K inputs, oldest first. - History: write the last `min(state_size, K+seqlen)` inputs to the tail `conv_state[slot,d, state_size-w : state_size]`. The head `[0:K]` is left stale until `rewind` copies the right window into it. That is why the generator always calls `rewind(0)` after a fully accepted history step. `conv_state` is allocated as `[max_batch_size, F, K + max_history]` (`gated_delta_net.py:184-193`). With no drafting, state_size = K = 4. #### `gated_rms_norm(x, w, y, g, epsilon, constant_bias, w_groups, gate_first, gate_act)` Source: `norm.cuh` decl, `norm.cu:499-590` (kernel), `:608-707` (host); called as `ext.gated_rms_norm(x, self.weight, y, gate, eps, constant_bias, groups, gate_first, gate_act)` (`gated_rmsnorm.py:120`). For KDA, the arguments are: x = core_attn_out bf16 `[B,S,64,128]`; w = o_norm bf16 `[128]`; y = half `[B,S,64,128]` (`out_dtype=torch.half`, `gated_delta_net.py:542-544`); g = z fp32 `[B,S,64,128]` (the g_b output); eps = 1e-5; constant_bias = 0.0; w_groups = 1; gate_first = False; gate_act = 1 (sigmoid). The host accepts x bf16 only, w fp32 or bf16, y half or fp32, and g bf16 or fp32. Rows = B·S·64 and dim = 128, so the "small" variant runs with 32 threads per row. Math per row (fp32 throughout): `ss = Σ x²` (fma, warp reduce); `r = rsqrtf(ss/dim + eps)`; `y = half( (x · (w + constant_bias) · r) · sigmoid(g) )`, with `sigmoid(g) = __fdividef(1, 1 + __expf(-g))`. For gate_act = 0 the gate is `silu(g)`. With gate_first, the gate multiplies x before the sum of squares (the Mamba2 variant, not used here). Torch: the fallback at `gated_rmsnorm.py:114-118` (`h = x.float(); h *= rsqrt(mean(h²)+eps); h = w.float()*h; h *= sigmoid(gate.float()); h.to(half)`) is the exact reference up to ULPs. #### `BC_GatedRMSNorm(weight, rms_norm_eps, constant_bias, w_groups=1, gate_first=False, gate_act=0)` Source: `libtorch/gated_rmsnorm.h`, `gated_rmsnorm.cpp`, binding `gated_rmsnorm_bc.h`. It is a data holder whose only method, `.run(x, y, gate)`, calls `gated_rms_norm(x, weight, y, gate, eps, constant_bias, w_groups, gate_first, gate_act)`. Python constructs it positionally with 6 args. #### `BC_GatedDeltaNetSplit` — KDA constructor (only if BC enabled) Binding `libtorch/gated_delta_net_bc.h` (2nd `py::init`). Positional args: `qkv_proj: BC_LinearEXL3, o_proj: BC_LinearEXL3, b_weight_t [64,4096] f16, f_a_weight_t [128,4096] f16, f_b_weight_t [8192,128] f16, g_a_weight_t [128,4096] f16, g_b_weight_t [8192,128] f16, dt_bias [8192] bf16, a_log [64] f32|bf16, lower_bound float (-5.0), num_k_heads, num_v_heads, k_head_dim, v_head_dim, conv1d_weight [F,4] bf16, conv1d_bias opt, norm: BC_GatedRMSNorm, beta_scale float`. The `*_t` tensors are empty at load and filled on the first forward from `Linear.inner.get_weight_tensor().T` (`gated_delta_net.py:1025-1032`). `dt_bias_bc` is a bf16 copy of the fp32 dt_bias. Methods: - `needs_configure(bsz, seqlen, history) -> bool`. It asserts 1≤bsz≤8 and 1≤seqlen≤16. - `configure_slot_kda(bsz, seqlen, history, qkv f32[B,S,F], z f32[B,S,64,128], b_out f32[B,S,64], fa_out f32[B,S,128], fb_out f32[B,S,8192], ga_out f32[B,S,128], beta bf16[B,S,64], g f32[B,S,64,128], mixed_qkv bf16[B,F,S], conv_out bf16[B,S,F], core_attn_out bf16[B,S,64,128], core_attn_out_f f16[B,S,8192], qkv_xh f16[B,S,4096], o_xh f16[B,S,8192])`. - `run_bszN(x f16[B,S,4096], y f32[B,S,4096] out, conv_state, recurrent_state, slots int32[B], history)`. Run sequence (`libtorch/gated_delta_net.cpp:216-324`): 1. `exl3_gemm(x→qkv)`. 2. `b_out = x@b_wᵀ`, `fa_out = x@f_aᵀ`, `ga_out = x@g_aᵀ` (`gdn_ba_gemv`: fp16 in, fp32 accumulate and out). 3. `fb_out = fa_out@f_bᵀ` and `z = ga_out@g_bᵀ` (`gdn_lowrank_gemv_f`: **fp32 input**, fp16 weight, fp32 out). 4. `kda_gate_op`. 5. `cuda_causal_conv1d_update(mixed_qkv→conv_out, act=true)`. 6. `cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule(conv_out, g, beta → core_attn_out)`. 7. `norm.run(core_attn_out → core_attn_out_f, gate=z)`. 8. `exl3_gemm(core_attn_out_f → y)`. Graph capture policy (`gated_delta_net.cpp:326-411`): the first call per slot runs eager. The next call captures a CUDA graph (via the `Graph` class in graph.cu, Graph slice). After that it replays with patched pointers: x is patched into 4 nodes, plus conv_state, slots ×2, recurrent_state, and y. If the state-buffer geometry differs from the captured one, it falls back to eager. Numerical difference vs the torch path (both are "correct"): - The torch path rounds the f_a and g_a outputs to fp16 before f_b and g_b (`gated_delta_net.py:1101`, `:1109`). BC keeps them fp32. - BC uses a bf16 copy of dt_bias. Torch uses fp32. - BC uses `__expf` fast exp. #### `kda_gate_op(qkv, b, f, dt_bias, a_log, mixed_qkv, beta, g, lower_bound, beta_scale)` Source: `gdn.cu:1786-1896`. It is a single elementwise kernel over three index ranges: - `mixed_qkv[b,f,s] = bf16_rn(qkv[b,s,f])`, a transpose and cast. - `beta = bf16_rn(sigmoid_fast(b) · beta_scale)`. - `g[b,s,h,c] = lower_bound · sigmoid_fast(exp(a_log[h]) · (f[b,s,h·Dk+c] + dt_bias[h·Dk+c]))` when lower_bound ≠ 0, otherwise `−exp(a_log[h]) · softplus(f+dt_bias)` with `softplus(x) = x>20 ? x : log1p(exp(x))`. Dtypes: qkv, b, f fp32; dt_bias bf16; a_log fp32 or bf16; outputs bf16, bf16, fp32. #### `gdn_ba_gemv(x f16[..,k], w_t f16[n,k], bias f16[n]|None, y f32[..,n])` / `gdn_lowrank_gemv_f(x f32[..,k], w_t f16[n,k], y f32[..,n])` Source: `gdn.cu:1630-1784`. Both compute `y = x @ w_tᵀ (+bias)` with fp32 accumulation. Lanes stride over k and the reduction uses warp `__shfl_down`. k must be even for `ba_gemv`. #### `batched_conv_rewind(jobs: list[ConvRewindJob], device_index)` / `batched_state_rewind(jobs: list[StateRewindJob], device_index)` Source: `gdn.cuh:207-250`, `gdn.cu:1912-2010`. Jobs are raw device pointers. - `ConvRewindJob(src, dst, dim, cdim, stride)`: for each channel d<dim, copy `cdim` bf16 values from `src + d*stride` to `dst + d*stride`. It reads all values before writing, so overlapping windows are safe. It implements `conv_state[slot,:, :K] = conv_state[slot,:, p-K:p]` with `p = state_size - num_tokens`. - `StateRewindJob(src, dst, num_elements)`: flat fp32 copy (float4) of `num_elements` values. Python passes `rs.stride(1)` = Hv·Dk·Dv as `num_elements`. It implements `state[slot,0] = state[slot, last_history+1-num_tokens]`. Torch equivalent: `GDNLayerState.rewind` (`gated_delta_net.py:238-251`) already does this with slice copies. In the shim, build the jobs as tuples and do `ctypes`-free copies, or monkeypatch `_dispatch_rewind_jobs` to call `l.rewind()`. ### (c) KDA layer forward, step by step (torch path = recommended XPU path; `gated_delta_net.py:980-1177`) Input: `x` f16 `[B,S,4096]`, the output of `attn_norm` after the hyperconnection mix (`transformer.py:150-155`). Output: fp32 `[B,S,4096]` (out_dtype float), added back through the HC apply. 1. **qkv** `qkv = qkv_proj(x)`: EXL3, fp32 `[B,S,24576]`. Then `mixed_qkv = qkv.transpose(1,2).to(bf16).contiguous()`, giving `[B,F,S]` (`:1095-1096`). 2. **output gate (z)** `z = g_b_proj( g_a_proj(x).to(f16) )`: fp16 Linears with fp32 out, viewed `[B,S,64,128]` (`:1099-1100`). There is no activation here; the sigmoid is applied inside the norm. 3. **beta** `beta = sigmoid(b_proj(x).float() * beta_scale).to(bf16)`, shape `[B,S,64]` (`:1102-1103`). 4. **decay** `f = f_b_proj( f_a_proj(x).to(f16) )`: fp32 `[B,S,8192]`. Then `gf = (f + dt_bias.float())`, viewed `[B,S,64,128]`, and `g = lower_bound · sigmoid( exp(A_log)[h] · gf )` with lower_bound −5.0, so g ∈ (−5, 0) is the natural-log decay per k-channel (`:1107-1114`). The softplus form `g = −exp(A_log)·softplus(gf)` (threshold 20) is used only if lower_bound is None. 5. **short conv** `causal_conv1d_update` (`:1139`, `conv1d.py:364-419`): a depthwise causal conv with K=4 over the 24576 channels, followed by SiLU, using and updating `conv_state[slot]`. Output bf16 `[B,S,F]`, token-major. - CUDA kernel if is_cuda, S≤32, and everything is bf16. - Else the Triton kernel `_causal_conv1d_update_slotted_kernel` for S≤256. - Else the `_output_kernel` + `_state_kernel` pair. - If no recurrent state is passed (no cache, e.g. `teacher_forced` in serve.py with `attn_mode="flash_attn_nc"`), a zero conv_state is used. 6. **delta rule** `gated_delta_rule_fn(..., channelwise_g=True)` (`gated_delta_rule.py:112-163`). - If S ≥ 64 and not history: split q, k, v, and loop **per batch row** over `recurrent_slots` (CPU copy): `chunk_kda(q,k,v,g,beta, initial_state=state[slot,0][None], output_final_state=save_state, use_qk_l2norm_in_kernel=True)`, then `state.copy_(new_state)`. Output cast to bf16. - Else `cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule`. - Both normalize q and k internally (eps 1e-6) and use scale = 1/√128. 7. **gated norm** `core = o_norm(core_attn_out, gate=z)`: `gated_rms_norm`, sigmoid gate, eps 1e-5, output f16 `[B,S,64,128]`, then viewed `[B,S,8192]`. 8. **o_proj** EXL3, `[B,S,8192]` → fp32 `[B,S,4096]`. Branch selection summary: | shape | EXL3_BC_GDN=1 (default) | EXL3_BC_GDN=0 | |---|---|---| | decode B∈1..8, S=1 | BC graph (all-CUDA) | torch path: conv CUDA op, recurrent CUDA op | | S ≤ 16 (B ≤ 8) | BC | torch: conv CUDA op (S≤32), recurrent op | | 17 ≤ S ≤ 32 | torch: conv CUDA op, recurrent op | same | | 33 ≤ S ≤ 63 | torch: Triton conv, recurrent op | same | | S ≥ 64 (prefill, chunk 8192) | torch: Triton conv (two-kernel variant for S>256), fla `chunk_kda` | same | On XPU, `is_cuda` is False, so the "conv CUDA op" rows go to the Triton conv once the `is_cuda` gate at `conv1d.py:245` is patched. **Cache state per KDA layer** (`GDNLayerState`, `gated_delta_net.py:174-315`; one per layer instance, allocated by `Cache` with `max_batch_size = args.autosplit_max_batch_size` slots, `cache/cache.py:160-171`; note `model_init.py` passes `-ambs` (4 in the serve command), and `generator.py:235` then caps the generator batch to `cache.num_slots`): - `conv_state`: bf16 `[slots, 24576, 4 + max_history]` (192 KiB per slot with max_history = 0). - `recurrent_state`: fp32 `[slots, max_history + 1, 64, 128, 128]` (4 MiB per slot per history entry), indexed `[slot, hist, h, k, v]`. - Zeroed at alloc, and `clear(slot)` zeroes it for each new `GDNState` (`:95-100`, `:225-228`). - Total for 34 layers × 4 slots ≈ 570 MiB with max_history = 0. - `max_history = max(num_draft_tokens, MTP default draft size, 4 if ngram)`, which is 0 for the serve.py command. **Checkpointing / second tier**: - `RecurrentCache` (`cache/recurrent.py:21-124`) is an LRU dict in system RAM. Its size is `recurrent_cache_size` (serve.py: `-rcs`, default 4.0 GiB). - Keys are page hashes. `GDNState.stash()` (`gated_delta_net.py:132-145`) copies `recurrent_state[slot,:1].cpu()` and `conv_state[slot,:,:4].cpu()` for every recurrent layer. These are plain torch D2H copies, pageable and synchronous. - `checkpoint_size = 24576·4·2 + 64·128·128·4 = 4,390,912` bytes per layer, ≈149 MB per checkpoint for 34 layers, so 4 GiB holds about 28 checkpoints. - Restore: `Cache.new_from_stashed` → `unstash` → `.copy_` H2D (`:147-155`, `:290-302`). - Checkpoints are taken every `default_recurrent_checkpoint_interval = 2048` tokens near the generation boundary, and every 32768 (`recurrent_checkpoint_interval_pp`) during long prompt ingestion (`generator/job.py:1565-1579`). - Prefill chunks are aligned to those boundaries. - Rewinding to an arbitrary earlier point (`rollback_capacity()==0`) restores the nearest page-aligned stash and replays. - **No ext ops are involved**: it is all torch `.cpu()` / `.copy_`, which works unchanged on XPU. **Rewinds**: a cheap in-place rewind is only possible right after a forward with `recurrent_history=True`, which the generator sets only when `draft_tokens is not None` (`generator/generator.py:1036`), i.e. MTP, a draft model, or n-gram. Not reached in the target configuration. ### (d) Triton kernels reached on the KDA path Common host-side issues for every fla kernel: - **`vendor/fla/utils.py:117`** `input_guard` enters `torch.cuda.device(t.device.index)` for any tensor with an index. That fails on an XPU-only torch, so patch it to `torch.xpu.device` or a device-type dispatch. - `utils.py:35-44`: `device_platform` comes from `triton.runtime.driver.active.get_current_target().backend`, which is "xpu" on Intel Triton. So `IS_NVIDIA`, `IS_NVIDIA_HOPPER`, `IS_NVIDIA_BLACKWELL` and `IS_TF32_SUPPORTED` are all False, and `IS_AMD` is False. This means: - `safe_dot` takes the plain `tl.dot` (no inline PTX `mov.f32` asm, `op.py:50-59`; that branch is Blackwell only). - `SOLVE_TRIL_DOT_PRECISION='ieee'` (`kda_chunk_intra.py:21-24`). - The `TRITON_F32_DEFAULT` env is not set (`utils.py:56-58` is NVIDIA only). - `utils.py:49`: `torch.cuda.get_device_capability` is only evaluated when IS_NVIDIA. - `utils.py:62`: `device="cuda"` allocator, Blackwell only. Not reached. - `utils.py:80-87`, `check_shared_mem`: uses `torch.cuda.device_count()`, which is 0 on XPU, so `min([])` raises, gets caught, and the function returns False. Every `check_shared_mem(...)` is therefore False on XPU and the **smallest** tile lists are chosen: cumsum `BS_LIST=[16,32]`; chunk_delta_h `num_stages∈[2,1]` and `BV∈[32]`. This is harmless but slower. If you patch it to `torch.xpu.device_count()`: BMG SLM is about 128 KiB, so `'ada'` (101376) and the default (102400) would pass and `'ampere'` would not. - `op.py:12`: `import triton.language.extra.libdevice as tldevice` runs at package import. It must resolve on Intel Triton (upstream ≥3.x has a backend-dispatching `extra/libdevice`). Only `tanh` (unused by KDA) and the `FLA_USE_FAST_OPS=1` variants touch it; keep `FLA_USE_FAST_OPS` unset. `exp2` = `tl.math.exp2`. - `IS_GATHER_SUPPORTED = hasattr(tl, "gather")` is only used in the safe-gate sub-chunk kernel, which is not reached. `IS_TMA_SUPPORTED=False`, and `make_tensor_descriptor` is only resolved at import (solve_tril, not reached). - Autotune: all configs are expressed in NVIDIA `num_warps` (1…32) and `num_stages` (1…4). On Intel Triton, `num_warps` is the number of sub-groups (`threads_per_warp` 16 or 32), so `num_warps=32` → up to 1024 work-items. That fits BMG's 1024 max workgroup with 32-wide warps but is invalid if a larger warp is configured. `num_stages` is accepted and is a software-pipelining hint. `cache_results=True` is used when the Triton version supports it. - **glm-flash-lite `glm53/triton_pin.py`** pre-fills `Autotuner.cache` with NVIDIA picks from a reference run, for bit-reproducible KDA prefill. Those picks are not meaningful on XPU, so don't install it (`GLM53_TRITON_PIN` unset). Make a new pin set on the B70 if reproducibility matters. - A fp32 `tl.dot` without explicit precision runs in `chunk_kda_fwd_kernel_inter_solve_fused` (`kda_chunk_intra.py:158-215`, fp32 operands). On NVIDIA it uses tf32 by default. On XPU it uses Triton's default `input_precision` (tf32 unless `TRITON_F32_DEFAULT=ieee`). Xe2 DPAS tf32 support is a risk: **set `TRITON_F32_DEFAULT=ieee` for correctness parity**. That is more accurate than NVIDIA's tf32 and slower. - No inline PTX, no `tl.extra.cuda`, and no fp8 in any reached kernel. | kernel (file:line) | reached via | dtypes | CUDA-specific flags | XPU risk | |---|---|---|---|---| | `_causal_conv1d_update_slotted_kernel` (`modules/gated_delta_net_fn/conv1d.py:16`) | torch-path conv, 32 < S ≤ 256 | bf16 io, fp32 acc | host: `x.is_cuda` check `:245`, `torch.cuda.device` `:279`; `num_warps=4` fixed | low (pure tl ops, `tl.sigmoid`) once the host checks are patched | | `_causal_conv1d_update_slotted_output_kernel` (`conv1d.py:116`) + `_state_kernel` (`:186`) | S > 256 (every 8192 prefill chunk) | same | same | low | | `l2norm_fwd_kernel` (`vendor/fla/l2norm.py:54`) | chunk_kda q and k (D=128 ≤ 512 branch) | bf16 in/out, fp32 rstd, eps 1e-6: `x/sqrt(Σx²+eps)` | autotune `num_warps` 1..16 × BT | low | | `chunk_local_cumsum_vector_kernel` (`fla/cumsum.py:90`) | g `[B,T,H,K]`, scale RCP_LN2=1.4426950216, chunk-local (64) inclusive cumsum, fp32 out | fp32 | autotune `BS∈BS_LIST`, warps 2..8 | low | | `chunk_kda_fwd_kernel_intra_token_parallel` (`fla/kda_chunk_intra_token_parallel.py:29`) | `safe_gate=False` (default; `BK_LIST`/`BV_LIST` are computed but unused) | low | Imported but not executed for KDA: `gdn_chunk_fwd`, `chunk_scaled_dot_kkt`, `gdn_wy_fast`, `solve_tril`, `chunk_o`, `chunk_h`, and `l2norm_fwd_kernel1` (D>512). Their module-level code (autotune decorators, `IS_*` flags, `make_tensor_descriptor` lookup) still runs at `import exllamav3.vendor.fla`, which happens on the first prefill ≥ 64 tokens (`gated_delta_rule.py:114`). Numerics of the chunk path: - q and k are L2-normalized into **bf16** before the chunk math. The CUDA recurrent kernel keeps them fp32. - Per-chunk states h are bf16, while the carried final state is fp32. - g is pre-scaled by 1/ln2 and exp2 is used. - The output is cast to q's dtype (bf16). - The existing test `exllamav3/tests/test_fla_vendored_.py` compares the vendored `chunk_kda` against upstream fla. Use `torch_recurrent_kda` (`gated_delta_rule.py:64`) as the oracle on XPU. ### (e) Non-ext CUDA dependencies in this part | file:line | what | path reached? | torch.xpu mapping | |---|---|---|---| | `modules/gated_delta_net_fn/conv1d.py:386` | `mixed_qkv.is_cuda` gate for the CUDA conv op | every torch-path conv | `mixed_qkv.device.type in ("cuda","xpu")`; on XPU it otherwise silently routes to Triton | | `modules/gated_delta_net_fn/conv1d.py:245` | `if not x.is_cuda: raise` | every Triton conv | same replacement; **hard blocker** | | `modules/gated_delta_net_fn/conv1d.py:279` | `with torch.cuda.device(x.device)` | every Triton conv | `torch.xpu.device(x.device)` or `torch.accelerator` / `torch.device` context | | `vendor/fla/utils.py:117` | `torch.cuda.device(idx)` in `input_guard` | every chunk_kda call | `torch.xpu.device(idx)`; **hard blocker** | | `vendor/fla/utils.py:49`, `:84` | `torch.cuda.get_device_capability`, `torch.cuda.device_count` | import (49 skipped when not NVIDIA; 84 returns [] and falls back to False) | `torch.xpu.device_count()` (optional, tile sizes) | | `vendor/fla/utils.py:62` | `torch.empty(..., device="cuda")` Triton allocator | Blackwell only | n/a | | `vendor/fla/op.py:12` | `triton.language.extra.libdevice` import | package import | must exist in Intel Triton | | `vendor/fla/op.py:50-59` | `tl.inline_asm_elementwise("mov.f32 ...")` PTX | Blackwell only | n/a | | `modules/gated_delta_net.py:677`, `:748` | `device != torch.device("cpu")` gates BC construction | load | true on XPU; set `EXL3_BC_GDN=0` | | `modules/gated_delta_net.py:1389`, `modules/gated_rmsnorm.py:167` | `torch.cuda.synchronize()` | TP import only | n/a (single GPU) | | `modules/gated_delta_net.py:44-46` | `torch.device(l.device).index` for rewind batching | drafting only | works for "xpu:0" | | `libtorch/gated_delta_net.cpp:326-411` | CUDA graph capture/replay (`Graph`) with pointer patching | BC only | `torch.xpu.graph` (XPUGraph, torch ≥ 2.8), or drop it by disabling BC | | `GDNState.stash/unstash` `gated_delta_net.py:290-302` | `.cpu()` / `.copy_` | checkpointing | device-agnostic | ### Minimal KDA op set for a correct XPU forward Set `EXL3_BC_GDN=0` (no BC_GatedDeltaNetSplit, no kda_gate_op, no gemvs, no graphs). Then: 1. Shim `gated_rms_norm` (torch) and `BC_GatedRMSNorm` (holder). 2. Shim `cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule` (torch, channelwise and history-aware as specified above). 3. Shim `cuda_causal_conv1d_update` (torch, via F.conv1d plus the state write-back), or skip it by routing all conv through Triton. 4. Patch three Python CUDA-isms: `conv1d.py:245/279/386` and `vendor/fla/utils.py:117`. 5. Export `TRITON_F32_DEFAULT=ieee`. 6. Rewind ops are only needed if MTP or n-gram drafting is enabled. The fp16 Linears (b/f/g) and EXL3 linears (qkv/o) come from Part A. --- ## Part D. MLA attention (NoPE), k-pool DSA indexer, KV/indexer caches All paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3/` (Python package in `exllamav3/`). GLM-5.3-Flash MLA geometry (config): 11 MLA layers (`full_attn_layers` 3,7,...,43), all with `indexer_types == "full"` (no "shared" layers). H = 64 heads, q_lora_rank 1536, kv_lora_rank D_c = 512, qk_nope_head_dim = 256, **qk_rope_head_dim D_r = 0 (NoPE)**, v_head_dim D_v = 256, sm_scale = 256^-0.5 = 0.0625, rms_norm_eps 1e-5. Indexer: H_i = 32, D_i = 128, index_topk 2048, index_kpool P = 4, kpool_tail True, index_norm_eps = 1e-6 (MLAttention default; glm5_next.py does not pass it). PAGE_SIZE = 256 (`exllamav3/constants.py:2`). ### Key findings first 1. **No ext op is mandatory for MLA if `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0`.** The eager ("dispatch") MLA path is pure torch + Triton, except `ext.dsa_topk` (needed whenever visible context > 2048) and the generic ops owned by other parts (EXL3 linears for q_a/q_b/kv_a/o/idx_wq_b, `ext.rms_norm` for q_a_layernorm/kv_a_layernorm). `ext.rope` is never reached (rope is None for NoPE, and the indexer's rope is tied to qk_rope_head_dim = 0, so `indexer_rope_interleave` in config.json is ignored by exllamav3). 2. **Force `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0` on XPU** (not just `EXL3_BC_MLA=0`). `EXL3_BC_MLA=0` only gates `bc_mla_step` (`modules/mla_attn.py:1027`); `autosplit_extra_measure` (`modules/mla_attn.py:1110-1118`, called at load from `model/model_ls.py:200`) calls `build_bc_mla` directly, which then AOT-compiles cubins via `triton.compile(...).asm["cubin"]` and constructs `ext.TritonKernel`/`ext.BC_MLAttention` (`modules/attention_fn/bc_attn.py:84-87`, `bc_mla.py:86`). `bc_attn_enable` (`bc_attn.py:42`, env `EXL3_BC_ATTN`) is checked inside `build_bc_mla` (`bc_mla.py:575`) and turns all of it off. Alternatively `EXL3_AUTOSPLIT_WORSTCASE=0` skips the extra measure, but BC must still be off for decode. 3. Even on CUDA the BC graph path is used only for **bsz == 1** decode on kpool layers (`bc_mla.py:520-521`: kpool slots return None for bsz > 1), so batch 2-8 decode already runs the eager path. The eager path is the reference to port. 4. No flash-attn / xformers is involved for MLA. `attn_mode` is `"flash_attn"` (paged cache; generator, warmup, autosplit) or `"flash_attn_nc"` (cache-less; serve.py teacher-forced `/generate` logprobs, `glm53/serve.py:266`). Both route to MLA's own Triton kernels (`modules/mla_attn.py:421-428`); the generic `attn_dispatch` backend list (`attention_fn/dispatch.py`) is imported but never called by MLA. xformers/torch-SDPA fallbacks are lazily imported and unused. 5. Default cache is fp16 (`model_init.py` builds `CacheLayer_fp16` unless `-cq`; serve.py passes no `-cq`, and reports `kv_cache_dtype = args.cache_quant or "fp16"`). MLA maps it to `CacheLayer_MLA_fp16` (`mla_attn.py:308-323`). So `quant_cache_cont`, `dequant_cache_cont`, `_mla_kv_quant_scatter_kernel`, all `QC>0` Triton branches and `_rot_h32` are unreached. 6. The Triton kernels on the path contain **no** inline PTX/asm, `tl.extra.cuda`, libdevice, fp8, tf32/`input_precision`, atomics, `tl.sort/topk/histogram/gather`, or cache/eviction hints (grep over `mla_triton.py`, `dsa_triton.py`, `triton_paged.py` is clean). The CUDA specifics are all in the Python wrappers: `with torch.cuda.device(...)` around every launch and `torch.cuda.get_device_properties(i).multi_processor_count` for split heuristics. 7. **Zero-width tensors**: with D_r = 0 the rope-key cache `kpe` is `(pages, 256, 1, 0)` and `q_pe` is `(R, H, 0)`; both are passed to Triton kernels as pointer args (never dereferenced: all D_r uses are `if D_r > 0` constexpr-guarded). A zero-numel torch tensor can have `data_ptr() == 0`. Verify the Intel Triton launcher accepts a null pointer arg; if not, pass a 1-element dummy fp16 tensor for those args on XPU. ### (a) Op table (MLA slice) Hot-path rank: 1 = every MLA layer every step, 2 = every step once context > 2048 tokens, 3 = load/warmup/rare, 4 = not reached by default. | op | category | when reached | rank | Python call sites | condition / avoidable? | recommended XPU path | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | `ext.dsa_topk` | ext fn (CUDA radix select) | sparse regime: visible ctx `max(cache_seqlens)+seqlen > 2048`, prefill and decode, every MLA layer | 2 | `modules/mla_attn.py:696, 708` (kpool path, used); source `exllamav3_ext/dsa_topk.cu:925-935` (wrapper) -> `dsa_topk_gr` `:684-777`; kernels `dsa_topk_kernel<VEC>` `:69-276`, `dsa_topk_split_kernel` / `dsa_topk_merge_kernel` (`:278-680`). - Signature: `scores` (R, T) **fp16**, innermost stride 1, any row stride (here a view of a fixed-stride backing, `s_stride = ceil(t_tile/128)*128`); `indices` (R, k_pad) **int32**, contiguous, written in place; `k` int <= k_pad; `t_ptr` optional device int tensor overriding T (always None from Python); `t_seq` 0. - Python call (kpool path, `mla_attn.py:696`): `ext.dsa_topk(sc, pool_idx, k_sel, None, 0)` with `sc = dsa_indexer_scores(...)[:, :pools_slab]`, `k_sel = min(512, pools_slab)`, `pool_idx` (rows, ceil(k_sel/32)*32) int32, then sliced `[:, :k_sel]`. - Exact semantics: - Key = 16-bit order-preserving map of the fp16 bits: `u & 0x8000 ? ~u : u | 0x8000`. Entries with key <= key(-inf) = 0x03ff are **excluded** (-inf and negative-sign NaNs; positive NaN 0x7e00 maps above +inf and would be selected as largest). - Selected set = the k largest finite fp16 scores; ties at the k-th value are broken by **lowest column index** (ordered compaction). - Output order: **ascending column index** (all entries > threshold in index order, followed by the needed ties == threshold in index order, so not globally sorted when ties are taken). Not sorted by score. - If fewer than k finite entries exist, all finite entries are emitted (index order). - Columns `[emitted, k_pad)` are filled with -1. - Split variant (R <= 16, T >= 32768, k_pad <= 4096, `EXL3_DSA_TOPK_SPLIT` != 0): identical result (per-span top-k superset then merge, same total order). - Torch reimplementation: `s = scores.float().masked_fill(scores == -inf, -inf)`; `v, i = torch.sort(s, dim=1, descending=True, stable=True)` (stable gives lowest index first among ties); take `i[:, :k]`, drop where `v[:, :k] == -inf`; `torch.sort` the kept indices ascending; pad with -1 to k_pad. For speed `torch.topk(s, k)` + `-inf` mask is equivalent up to tie-breaking and order (attention is order-invariant up to fp summation order). - The tiled merge in Python (`mla_attn.py:699-717`) uses `torch.topk` already; only taken when `pools_slab > t_tile = 32768` pools (> 131072 tokens), i.e. never at `-cs 131072`. #### `ext.BC_MLAttention` (do not port; documented for reference) - Binding `exllamav3_ext/libtorch/mla_attention_bc.h` (ctor with 29 args: dims, the `BC_LinearEXL3` handles of q/q_a/kv_a/o, norm weights, rope params, `w_uk_flat`, `w_uv_flat`, cache tensors, `xh`, `h32`; methods `set_indexer`, `needs_configure`, `configure_slot`, `configure_slot_dsa`, `run`). Source `libtorch/mla_attention.cpp` (`run_gr` `:307-818`, `run` `:819-989`). - `run` executes the slot eagerly on its first call, captures a CUDA graph (`Graph` class, `graph.cu`) on the second, and replays it afterwards, patching pointer/scalar params (`GP_gemm_A`, `GP_attn_block_table`, `GP_attn_seqlens`, `GP_dsa_T`, ...). Kernels launched through `TritonKernel::launch` (CUDA driver `cuLaunchKernel`), EXL3 GEMMs through `exl3_gemm_gr`, fp16 GEMMs through `hgemm_gr` (cuBLASLt), top-k through `dsa_topk_gr`. - Stage order (useful as a cross-check of the eager math, bsz 1 decode): q_a GEMM -> rms_norm(q_a) -> q_b GEMM -> kv_a GEMM -> `_mla_stage_kernel` (fp32 RMS norm of the latent: `x*w*rsqrt(mean(x^2)+eps)`) -> absorb -> cache append -> [indexer: copy x, hgemm wk, `_mla_idx_norm_kernel` (biased LayerNorm fp32), plane append (k at offset 0 of the 256-wide row), hgemm gate, plane append at offset 128, `_dsa_pool_update_kernel`] -> regime 0: decode split+combine; regime 1: wq_b GEMM, hgemm weights_proj, `_dsa_indexer_fewq_kernel` over the pool plane (T in pool units), `dsa_topk_gr(k = topk/P)`, `_dsa_pool_expand_kernel`, `_dsa_attn_split_kernel` + `_dsa_attn_combine_kernel` -> unfold -> o_proj GEMM. - Differences vs eager: pool update is a Triton kernel that also (re)writes partial pools (never selected); eager writes only pools completed by the append. Selection and attention results are the same set. #### `ext.TritonKernel(cubin: bytes, name: str, num_warps: int, shared_bytes: int)` - `exllamav3_ext/triton_kernel.{h,cpp}`: `cuModuleLoadData` on the cubin, `cuModuleGetFunction`, sets `CU_FUNC_ATTRIBUTE_MAX_DYNAMIC_SHARED_SIZE_BYTES` if > 48 KB; `launch()` appends two null trailing scratch pointers and calls `cuLaunchKernel(fn, grid, 32*num_warps threads, shared)`. Uses `cuda_drv.cpp` (dlopen'd driver API). Python side compiles with `triton.compile(ASTSource(...)).asm["cubin"]` under `torch.cuda.device` (`bc_attn.py:64-89`). - No XPU equivalent needed: disabled by `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0`. (An XPU port would need SPIR-V/zebin from `asm["spv"]`/`asm["zebin"]` and a Level Zero launcher; not worth it for a correct port.) #### `ext.cache_rotate(cache, order, temp)` (owned by Part F; MLA-relevant semantics) - `exllamav3_ext/generator/cache.cu:65-97`. `cache` contiguous (num_pages, ...), any dtype, treated as bytes; `order` int32 (2n,) of (dst, src) page pairs; `temp` one page of bytes. For i in 0..n-1: `(cache[a] if a >= 0 else temp)[:] = (cache[b] if b >= 0 else temp)[:]`, sequentially. Early return if page_size == 0 (the MLA `kpe` tensor has 0 bytes, so `temp` must also be 0 bytes; torch handles it). - MLA tensors passed by `Cache.get_all_tensors()`: `k` (pages,256,1,512) fp16, `v` (pages,256,1,0), `k_idx` (pages,256,256) fp16, `k_pool` (pages,64,128) fp16. ### (c) MLA + indexer layer forward, step by step (eager path, `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0`) Entry: `MLAttention.forward` (`mla_attn.py:409-433`) -> `decode_flash_attn` (`:964-1017`) with `params["attn_mode"] == "flash_attn"` (generator/warmup/load) or `decode_flash_attn_nc` (`:1053-1086`) for cache-less forwards. Input `x` (bsz, seqlen, 4096), the post-attn_norm hidden (fp16). Output (bsz, seqlen, 4096) **fp32** (`out_dtype = torch.float`, glm5_next.py:197). Notation: R = bsz*seqlen, H = 64, D_c = 512, D_nope = D_v = 256, H_i = 32, D_i = 128, P = 4. 0. `host_seqlens` = host list of `cache_seqlens` (pre-append lengths), cached in `params["_mla_host_seqlens"]` (`:41-55`); free when the generator passes CPU tensors. `sparse = max(host_seqlens) + seqlen > 2048` (`:796-802`). 1. Query: `q_resid = RMSNorm_{q_a_layernorm}(EXL3(q_a_proj, x))` (fp16, R x 1536); `q = EXL3(q_b_proj, q_resid)` -> view (R, 64, 256) = q_nope (q_pe is empty). 2. Latent: `ckv = RMSNorm_{kv_a_layernorm}(EXL3(kv_a_proj_with_mqa, x))` -> (bsz, seqlen, 512) fp16. (kv_a output width is exactly 512, no rope part.) k_pe is (bsz, seqlen, 1, 0). 3. If not MHA-form prefill (`use_mha = seqlen > 16 and EXL3_MLA_PREFILL=="mha" and causal and not sparse`, `:833`): absorb `q_lat[h, r, :] = q_nope[r, h, :] @ W_UK[h]` where `W_UK[h] = w_uk_flat[:, h*256:(h+1)*256]^T` i.e. `q_lat[h,r,c] = sum_d q[r,h,d] * w_uk_flat[c, h*256+d]`, fp32 accumulate, stored fp16, layout (H, R, 512) head-major (`_mla_absorb_kernel`, `mla_triton.py:555-591`). `w_uk_flat (512, 64*256)` / `w_uv_flat (512, 64*256)` are built at load from `kv_b_proj.weight` (64*(256+256), 512) fp16 (unquantized; fp8 checkpoints get the block inverse scale applied) as `w.view(64, 512_rows=256+256, 512)`: rows [0,256) per head -> W_UK, rows [256,512) -> W_UV, permuted to (D_c, H, dim) (`mla_attn.py:368-382`). 4. Cache append (`append(ckv, k_pe)` -> `CacheLayer_MLA_fp16.update_kv_direct`, `cache/mla.py:110-122` -> `_mla_kv_update_kernel`): for row (b, i): `abs = cache_seqlens[b] + i; page = block_table[b, abs // 256]; ckv_cache[page, abs % 256, 0, :] = ckv[b, i]`. 5. Indexer keys, every forward, both regimes (`_indexer_keys`, `:467-486`): `k = LayerNorm(x @ wk^T; weight, bias, eps=1e-6)` (F.layer_norm in fp16 -> fp16, 128 wide); no rope; `gate = F.linear(x.half(), idx_kpool_gate)` (gate weight (128, 4096) bf16->fp16 from `kpool_aux.safetensors`) -> 128 wide; plane row = `cat(k, gate)` (256 wide fp16) appended to `k_idx` plane at the token's slot (`update_idx_direct`, `_mla_plane_update_kernel`). 6. Pool plane update (`_update_pool_plane`, `:581-613`), per batch row b: pools `p in [pos0//4, (pos0+seqlen)//4)` (those completed by this append): members j = 0..3 = tokens 4p+j read back from the `k_idx` plane (fp32), `probs[j, d] = softmax_j(gate[4p+j, d] + ape[j, d])` (ape (4,128) bf16 -> **fp32**), `pool_k[p, d] = sum_j probs[j, d] * k[4p+j, d]` -> fp16, appended to `k_pool` (pages, 64, 128) with page = `block_table[b, p // 64]`, slot `p % 64` (`update_pool_direct` -> `_mla_plane_update_kernel` with `cache_seqlens := [first_pool]`). 7. Dense regime (visible ctx <= 2048): - seqlen <= 16 (decode / short chunk): `o_lat = mla_attn_triton_decode(q_lat, q_pe_hm, ...)`: for query row (b, i) at absolute position `q_abs = cache_seqlens[b] + seqlen - seqlen + i` (cache already holds the chunk; `pre_appended_len = seqlen`), keys n in `[0, total)`, causal `n <= q_abs`: `s = (q_lat[h,r] . ckv[n]) * 0.0625` (fp16 dot, fp32 acc), online softmax fp32, `o_lat[h,r] = sum_n p_n ckv[n] / sum_n p_n` (P cast to fp16 before the PV dot), split-K flash-decoding (`num_splits = min(2*SM/programs (cap 128), ceil(max_k_len/128))`, combine kernel merges with max-rescaling; fully masked rows give 0). Then unfold `o[r, h, :] = o_lat[h, r, :] @ w_uv_flat[:, h*256:(h+1)*256]` (fp32 acc, fp16 out, token-major (R, 64, 256)) and `o_proj`. - seqlen > 16 (prefill chunk): MHA form (`mla_attn_triton_prefill_mha`): for each batch row, tiles of 4096 cache rows `[a, e)` over `[0, total)`: gather latent tile (fp16), `K_nope = tile @ w_uk_flat`, `V = tile @ w_uv_flat` (torch.mm fp16, (tile, 16384)), then per head standard causal attention `softmax(q_nope_h . K_nope_h^T * 0.0625) V_h` with running (m, l, acc) state in global fp32 buffers across tile launches (same-stream ordering); output token-major -> `o_proj`. Mathematically identical to the absorbed form. 8. Sparse regime (visible ctx > 2048), `_indexer_topk_kpool` paged path (`:617-728`): - `q_idx = EXL3(idx_wq_b, q_resid)` -> (bsz, seqlen, 32, 128) fp16 (no rope); `w = x @ weights_proj^T` (bsz, seqlen, 32) fp16, raw. - Per batch row b, per slab of 256 query rows: `pools_slab = (pos_b + r1) // 4` complete pools visible to the slab's last row, `k_sel = min(512, pools_slab)`. Scores over pools s in [0, pools_slab): `score[r, s] = (128^-0.5 * 32^-0.5) * sum_h w[r,h] * relu(q_idx[r,h] . pool_k[s])` (fp16 dot, fp32 accumulate over heads, stored **fp16**), with the causal bound `s < (pos_b + r0 + r + 1) // 4` (complete pools only; others -inf). Kernel: `_dsa_indexer_fewq_kernel` if rows <= 4 else `_dsa_indexer_kernel` (block_m 64, block_n 128, 8 warps), reading `k_pool` through the block table (`epp = 64` entries per page, row = `bt[s // 64]*64 + s % 64`). - `ext.dsa_topk(scores, pool_idx, k_sel)` -> pool ids; expand `tok = pool*4 + [0,1,2,3]` (-1 propagates) into columns `[0, 4*k_sel)`. - Tail (`append_tail`, `:645-657`): for query position q (absolute), `vis = q+1`, `tail_count = vis % 4`, tokens `[vis - tail_count, vis)` are appended raw in columns `[base_cols, base_cols+3)`, rest -1. Note: when `vis % 4 == 0` the query's own token sits in a complete pool and is attended only if that pool is selected; there is no forced "most recent pool" selection beyond the tail. - `indices` (R, k_pad) int32, k_pad = ceil((512*4 + 3)/32)*32 = **2080**, -1 padded. Indices are absolute token positions within the sequence (pre-page-table). - Sparse attention (`_attend_sparse`, `:930-961` -> `dsa_attn(q_lat, ckv_cache, kpe_cache, bt, indices=..., q_pe=(R,H,0), out_latent=True)`): for each row r, head h: over selected entries n (idx >= 0): page lookup `tok = bt[row, idx // 256]*256 + idx % 256`, `s = (q_lat[h,r] . ckv[tok]) * 0.0625`, online softmax fp32, `o_lat[h,r] = sum p ckv[tok] / sum p` (fp32 acc, P cast to fp16), no causal mask (selection is causal by construction). Block table: shared single row when bsz==1 or seqlen==1 (stride 0 trick), else `repeat_interleave(seqlen)`. R <= 8 -> split (16 splits if k_len > 256) + combine kernels, block_h 16; R > 8 -> one-shot kernel block_h 32, block_n 32, num_stages 3. Output head-major (H, R, 512) -> unfold -> o_proj. 9. `flash_attn_nc` (cache-less) variant: scratch pages `ckv (bsz*pages, 256, 1, 512)` with identity block table, `cache_seqlens = 0`, `host_seqlens = [0]*bsz`; the indexer uses the eager cache-less branch (`:730-760`) on the packed chunk rows: pools built with `torch.softmax`, scores via einsum (`relu(q.k * D_i^-0.5)` then `w * H_i^-0.5`, fp32), causal mask `pool_end = 4p+3 <= q_pos`, `torch.topk(min(512, n_pools))`, expand + tail. No `ext.dsa_topk` on this branch. State kept per MLA layer in the cache (`cache/mla.py:13-89`, all fp16, allocated zeroed at load, pages = cache_size/256 = 512 for `-cs 131072`): | tensor | shape | bytes/token | contents | |---|---|---|---| | `k` (ckv) | (pages, 256, 1, 512) | 1024 | kv_a_layernorm-normalized latent | | `v` (kpe) | (pages, 256, 1, 0) | 0 | NoPE: empty | | `k_idx` | (pages, 256, 256) | 512 | [LayerNorm'd indexer key (128) or gate scores (128)] | | `k_pool` | (pages, 64, 128) | 64 | pooled indexer keys, one per 4 tokens | Total 1600 B/token/layer, 11 layers = 17.6 KB/token, 2.31 GB at 131072 tokens. No per-sequence recurrent state for MLA layers; `params["dsa_topk_indices"]` is set per full layer (`:869`) but only consumed by "shared" layers (none in GLM5.3). ### (d) Triton kernel inventory (MLA slice) Files: `exllamav3/modules/attention_fn/mla_triton.py`, `dsa_triton.py`, helpers imported from `triton_paged.py` (`_qc_load_kt`, `_qc_load_v`, `_rot_h32`, `_get_h32`; correctness is unaffected, tuning will differ. CUDA graphs only exist inside `BC_MLAttention` (`Graph` in `exllamav3_ext/graph.cu`), disabled with `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0`. ### Minimal set for a correct MLA forward on XPU - Env: `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0` (covers `EXL3_BC_MLA`), keep default `EXL3_MLA_PREFILL=mha`, no `-cq`. - Compat shim: `torch.cuda.device` -> `torch.xpu.device`; SM count -> XPU subslice count. - ext: `dsa_topk` (torch sort/topk). Plus the Part A EXL3 linear and the Part E `rms_norm`. - Triton kernels as listed "yes" above, unchanged except launch configs (prefill MHA block_m, one-shot DSA block_h). If any Triton kernel fails on XPU, each has a direct torch equivalent given in (c): dense attention = gather pages + `softmax((q_lat @ ckv^T) * 0.0625, causal)` @ ckv; sparse = same over gathered `indices` rows; absorb/unfold = per-head bmm; appends = index_put. --- ## Part E. Hyper-connections, norms, TransformerBlock, model forward / load / warmup, loader, graphs All paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3`. Python files are under `exllamav3/`, and CUDA files are under `exllamav3/exllamav3_ext/`. The line numbers are for v1.5.1 (commit 958ec93). ### (a) Op table for this part "Calls/fwd" means calls per model forward, the same for prefill chunks and decode steps.
