It is owned by `BC_LinearEXL3`, `BC_GatedDeltaNet`, `BC_MLAttention`, `BC_BlockSparseMLP`, `BC_GatedMLP`, `BC_GatedRMSNorm`, and so on. | Avoid it by not exposing `BC_*` classes from the shim, and by setting env switches such as `EXL3_BC_GDN=0` (`modules/gated_delta_net.py:19`), `EXL3_BC_MLA` (`modules/mla_attn.py:26`) and `EXL3_BC_ATTN` (`attention_fn/bc_attn.py:37`, `bc_mla.py:20`). Parts A–D give the exact per-module gates. | none | | `rope`, `gen_mrope_pos_ids` (`util/rope.py`) | RoPE | **not reached from this part**. GLM is NoPE (`rope_settings=None`, `glm5_next.py:57,211`). | 0 | `util/rope.py` | Any indexer RoPE belongs to Part D. | none | ### (b) Per-op detail #### `hc_mix_num_chunks(int R, int row_len) -> int` (`hc_mix.cu:894`) ``` chunks_a = min(128, max(1, 512 / max(R,1))) // integer division chunk_cols = ceil((row_len / chunks_a) / 256) * 256 // round up to a multiple of 4*NUM_THREADS_A = 256 return ceil(row_len / chunk_cols) ``` For GLM, row_len = 4·4096 = 16384. R=1 gives 64 chunks. R=8 gives 64. R=8192 gives 1. The value only sizes the `partials` workspace `(R, chunks, M+1)`. A torch shim may ignore the workspace. #### `hc_mix(streams, fn, base, scale, rms_eps: float, hc_eps: float, sinkhorn_iters: int, partials, post, comb, collapsed) -> None` Bindings: `bindings.cpp` `m.def("hc_mix", &hc_mix)`. Declared in `hc_mix.cuh:12`. Implemented in `hc_mix.cu:901`, with the launcher at `:807`, kernels at `:41` (partials) and `:118` (finalize). Arguments (all positional, and the checks are enforced): - `streams`: `(R, H=4, D=4096)` **fp32**, contiguous. The Python caller passes `streams.view(R,H,D)`. - `fn`: `(M=24, H*D=16384)`. This is **fp32 when R > 32, and fp16 when R ≤ 32**. Python caches `self.fn_h = self.fn.half()` (`hyperconnections.py:146-151`), and the kernel dots in fp32 either way. A bit-faithful port must upcast the fp16 `fn` it was given, not use the fp32 original. - `base`: `(24,)` fp32. `scale`: `(3,)` fp32. - `rms_eps` = `rms_norm_eps` = 1e-5. `hc_eps` = 1e-6. `sinkhorn_iters` = 20. - Outputs, all written in place: `partials` `(R, chunks, 25)` fp32 (scratch), `post` `(R,4)` fp32, `comb` `(R,4,4)` fp32, `collapsed` `(R,4096)` **fp16** (the Python fused path always allocates half, `:143`). The kernel also accepts fp32 `collapsed`. - Workspaces: for R ≤ 32 they come from the static `g_tensor_cache.get_bucketed` shared by every site (`:136-139`). For larger R they are fresh `torch.empty` allocations. Checkpoint tensors per site: - Key `model.language_model.layers.{i}.hc_attn` or `.hc_ffn`. - Tensors `{key}_fn` `(24, 16384)`, `{key}_base` `(24,)`, `{key}_scale` `(3,)`. They are loaded as `.float()` (`hyperconnections.py:91-93`). - The unweighted `{key}.norm` RMSNorm submodule has no tensor and is used only by the torch fallback. - `fn` row layout: rows 0–3 are `pre`, rows 4–7 are `post`, rows 8–23 are `comb`, with comb element (i,j) at row `8 + 4*i + j`. - `fn` column index is `h*4096 + d`, so the flattened streams are stream-major. Exact math, per row r. Everything is fp32. The CUDA build uses `--use_fast_math`, `__expf` and `__fdividef`. ``` s = streams[r].reshape(16384) # fp32 ss = sum(s*s) # fp32, chunked tree reduce, deterministic order mix[m] = sum(s * fn[m].float()) m=0..23 # dot on the RAW (un-normalised) streams rmr = rsqrt(ss / 16384 + rms_eps) # == unweighted RMSNorm applied after the (linear) projection pre[h] = sigmoid(mix[h] * rmr * scale[0] + base[h]) + hc_eps # fmaf(mix*rmr, scale, base) post[h] = 2 * sigmoid(mix[4+h] * rmr * scale[1] + base[4+h]) L[i,j] = mix[8+4i+j] * rmr * scale[2] + base[8+4i+j] C = softmax(L, dim=-1) (over j, max-subtracted) + hc_eps C = C / (C.sum(dim=-2, keepdim) + hc_eps) # column normalise (sum over i) repeat sinkhorn_iters-1 (=19) times: C = C / (C.sum(dim=-1, keepdim) + hc_eps) # row normalise C = C / (C.sum(dim=-2, keepdim) + hc_eps) # column normalise comb[r] = C # stored row-major comb[r, i, j] collapsed[r, d] = sum_h pre[h] * streams[r, h, d] # fmaf in h order 0..3, then round-to-nearest to fp16 ``` The torch fallback (`hyperconnections.py:155-170`) is identical math. It normalises first and then projects, and it keeps `collapsed` in fp32. This equivalence is exact in real arithmetic and differs only in fp32 rounding. Torch shim: ```python def hc_mix(streams, fn, base, scale, rms_eps, hc_eps, iters, partials, post, comb, collapsed): R, H, D = streams.shape s = streams.reshape(R, H * D) mix = s @ fn.float().t() # (R, 24) rmr = torch.rsqrt(s.pow(2).sum(-1, keepdim=True) / (H * D) + rms_eps) mix = mix * rmr pre = torch.sigmoid(mix[:, :H] * scale[0] + base[:H]) + hc_eps post.copy_(2 * torch.sigmoid(mix[:, H:2*H] * scale[1] + base[H:2*H])) c = (mix[:, 2*H:] * scale[2] + base[2*H:]).view(R, H, H) c = torch.softmax(c, -1) + hc_eps c = c / (c.sum(-2, keepdim=True) + hc_eps) for _ in range(iters - 1): c = c / (c.sum(-1, keepdim=True) + hc_eps) c = c / (c.sum(-2, keepdim=True) + hc_eps) comb.copy_(c) collapsed.copy_((pre.unsqueeze(-1) * streams).sum(1)) # copy_ rounds to collapsed.dtype (fp16) ``` Performance note: about 20 small launches times 90 sites per token. It is fine for correctness. A one-kernel Triton version is the obvious follow-up, and `glm-flash-lite/glm53/k_hcfuse.py` (described below) shows the fusion target. #### `hc_apply(x, y, post, comb: Optional[Tensor]) -> None` (`hc_mix.cu:962`, kernel `:723`) Arguments: - `x`: `(R,4,4096)` fp32 contiguous, **updated in place**. - `y`: `(R,4096)` fp32 or fp16 contiguous. For GLM it is fp32, because the attention and MLP modules use `out_dtype=torch.float`. - `post`: `(R,4)` fp32. - `comb`: `(R,4,4)` fp32, or `None`. Only GatedResidual passes `None`. Math, all in fp32: ``` x_new[r, h, d] = post[r,h] * y[r,d] + sum_g comb[r, g, h] * x_old[r, g, d] # i.e. x_new = post⊗y + combᵀ·x (comb None: x_new[r,h,d] = post[r,h]*y[r,d] + x_old[r,h,d]) ``` Each thread reads all 4 stream values of a column before it writes any of them, which is what makes the in-place update safe. The FMA order is: start from `post*y`, then add `fmaf(comb[g][h], x[g])` for g = 0..3. Torch shim: ```python def hc_apply(x, y, post, comb): yf = y.float().unsqueeze(1) if comb is None: x.add_(post.unsqueeze(-1) * yf) else: x.copy_(post.unsqueeze(-1) * yf + torch.matmul(comb.transpose(-1, -2), x)) ``` #### `hc_head(...)` (`hc_mix.cu:933`) Not reached. If it ever were reached, it is the finalize kernel with M=4: `collapsed = sum_h (sigmoid(mix[h]*rmr*scale[0]+base[h])+hc_eps) * streams[h]`. #### `rms_norm(x, w: Optional[Tensor], y, epsilon: float, constant_bias: float, constant_scale: float, span_heads: bool, add_residual: bool, w_groups: int = 1) -> None` Bindings: `bindings.cpp` with py::args `x,w,y,epsilon,constant_bias,constant_scale,span_heads,add_residual,w_groups=1`. Implemented in `norm.cu:448`, with `rms_norm_impl` at `:318` and the kernel at `:159`. GLM calls it as `ext.rms_norm(x_2d, weight, y_2d, 1e-5, 0.0, 1.0, False, False, 1)`: - `x_2d` is `(R,4096)` **fp16**. This is the `collapsed` output of `hc_mix`, and `y.half()` is a no-op. - For the final `norm`, `x_2d` is **fp32**, the HyperHead mean. - `y_2d` is fp16. The final norm also has `out_dtype=torch.half` (`glm5_next.py:332`). Allowed dtypes: - x: fp16 or fp32. - w: fp16 or bf16. It is loaded with `float2half=True, allow_bf16=True` (`rmsnorm.py:54`), so a bf16 checkpoint weight stays bf16 and an fp32 one becomes fp16. - y: fp16 or fp32. **No bf16 output.** - The dim must be divisible by 4. Exact math, per row: ``` xf = x.float(); if x is fp16: xf = clamp(xf, -65504, 65504) # CLAMP_FP16 on read (turns ±inf into ±65504) rmf = rsqrt(sum(xf*xf) / dim + epsilon) * constant_scale # fp32, eps INSIDE the sqrt, mean over dim out = (xf * (w.float() + constant_bias)) * rmf # w None -> xf * rmf ; order: (x*w)*rmf add_residual (res_mode POST): out += y_old.float() # y read then overwritten y = out rounded RN to y.dtype w_groups>1: w row = (row % w_groups); span_heads: x,y flattened over the last two dims first ``` Do not reuse `forward_torch` (`rmsnorm.py:71`). It rounds to the input dtype before multiplying by the weight, so it is not bit-identical to the kernel. A shim that follows the formula above is the correct reference. Torch shim: ```python def rms_norm(x, w, y, eps, cbias, cscale, span_heads, add_residual, w_groups=1): xs, ys = (x.flatten(-2), y.flatten(-2)) if span_heads else (x, y) xf = xs.float() if xs.dtype == torch.half: xf = xf.clamp(-65504, 65504) rmf = torch.rsqrt(xf.pow(2).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + eps) * cscale if w is not None: wf = w.float() if w_groups > 1: wf = wf.view(w_groups, -1).repeat(xf.shape[0] // w_groups, 1) # row % groups xf = xf * (wf + cbias) out = xf * rmf if add_residual: out = out + ys.float() ys.copy_(out) ``` #### `rms_norm_res_in(x, w, y, r, epsilon, constant_bias, constant_scale)` (`norm.cu`, RES_IN) Not reached for GLM. Semantics: `r = round_to_r_dtype(x + r)` (written back), then `y = norm(r) * w`. #### stloader ops (`stloader.cpp`, `stloader.h`, `stloader_cu.cu`) Signatures: - `stloader_open_file(const char*) -> list[uintptr]` (8 per-thread handles). - `stloader_close_file(list)`. - `stloader_read(handles, offset, size, target: Tensor)` reads raw bytes at the file offset into `target`. For a CUDA target it stages through a pinned pool (`cudaMallocHost`, `stloader.cpp:344`) and copies with `cudaMemcpyAsync`. - `TensorLoadJob(handles, file_offset, bytesize, dest_ptr, dest_size, bf16_to_fp16, fp32_to_fp16, cuda, device_id)`. - `stloader_deferred_cpu(jobs)` and `stloader_deferred_cuda(jobs, max_chunk_size=4 MiB)` run batched jobs. The CUDA path coalesces reads into 4 MiB pinned slots per thread (8 threads × 4 slots), then does an async H2D copy, plus an **in-place bf16→fp16 kernel** (`__float2half_rn`, no clamp) when `bf16_to_fp16` is set, then a per-slot event and a final `cudaDeviceSynchronize`. `fp32_to_fp16` is not done in C++: Python loads fp32 into a temp tensor and `copy_`s it (`safetensors.py:981-997`). Semantics to reproduce: raw safetensors bytes go to the device. If a tensor is bf16 and the caller did not pass `allow_bf16`, it becomes fp16 with RN rounding, so values above 65504 become ±inf. If it is fp32 and the caller passed `float2half`, it becomes fp16. Transpose and `pad_to` are applied afterwards. The `"python"` method (`safetensors.py:839-857`) reproduces the same semantics with `torch.frombuffer` and `.to()`. ### (c) GLM5Next forward through this part (hyper-connections, block order, model loop) Module list (`architecture/glm5_next.py:149-344`): | index | module | |---|---| | 0 | `Embedding` (caps `prefer_cpu`, so it lives on the **CPU**) | | 1 | `ExpandStreams` | | 2..46 | 45 × `TransformerBlock` (`first_block_idx=2`, `last_kv_module_idx=46`) | | 47 | `HyperHead(mean=True)` | | 48 | `RMSNorm` final, `out_dtype=half` | | 49 | `Linear lm_head` (EXL3, 6-bit `head_bits`, caps `logits_output`; `logit_layer_idx=49`) | Model loop. `Model.forward` (`model/model.py:374`) runs these steps: 1. `prepare_inputs`: `prepare_for_attn` and `prepare_for_recurrence`. These are Parts C and D. They fill params with cache seqlens, block tables and recurrent states. 2. `forward_ls` (`model/model_ls.py:317-330`) iterates `fwd_modules`: - For each module it calls `module.prepare_for_device(x)`, which does `x.to(module.device)`, non-blocking if the source is pinned (`modules/module.py:117-125`). - It then calls `module.forward(x, params)`. - Just before the logits layer, if `params["last_tokens_only"]` is set (the generator sets it to 1 at `generator/job.py:1396`), it slices `x = x[..., -n:, :]`. 3. `advance_recurrent_states` runs at the end (Part C). `Model.prefill` (`model/model.py:349`, `model_ls.py:298-314`) is used for the non-final chunks of a prompt: - It sets `params["prefill"] = True` only on module 46, the last block. - That block runs its attention, so the KV/recurrent caches fill, and then **returns before its MLP** (`transformer.py:160`). - The loop then breaks, so HyperHead, the norm and lm_head do not run, and it returns `None`. Shapes and dtypes along the residual: - Embedding: `nn.Embedding` on the CPU. The weight is fp16, or bf16 because of `allow_bf16`. The output is cast to **fp32** (`out_dtype=torch.float`) with shape (b,s,4096) on the CPU. - In the generator's decode loop (`generator/generator.py:1037`, `pinned_staging=True`) it is copied into a reused `pin_memory=True` buffer (`modules/embedding.py:158-166`) so that the upload is asynchronous. - ExpandStreams (`hyperconnections.py:37-38`): `x.float().unsqueeze(2).expand(-1,-1,4,-1).contiguous()` on the device. The result is fp32 **(b, s, 4, 4096)**, with 4 identical copies, and this stack is the residual for the rest of the network. - TransformerBlock.forward (`modules/transformer.py:137-215`), for GLM, which has HC and no post-norms or scalars: 1. `hc_post, hc_comb, y = attn_hc.mix(x)` at `:151`. This is `ext.hc_mix`, and `y` is (b,s,4096) **fp16**. 2. `y = input_layernorm(y, out_dtype=half)` at `:154`. This is `ext.rms_norm`, fp16 to fp16, eps 1e-5. 3. `y = self_attn(y)` at `:159`. This is either GatedDeltaNet (KDA) or MLAttention, both with `out_dtype=torch.float`, so `y` is fp32 (b,s,4096). 4. Prefill early-out for the last block (`:160`). 5. `x = attn_hc.apply_(x, y, hc_post, hc_comb)` at `:165`. This is `ext.hc_apply`, in place on the fp32 stream stack. 6. `hc_post, hc_comb, y = mlp_hc.mix(x)` at `:175`, then `post_attention_layernorm` at `:178` (`ext.rms_norm`). 7. `y = mlp(y)` at `:187`. This is either GatedMLP (layers 0–2, dense) or BlockSparseMLP with a shared expert, both with `out_dtype=torch.float`. 8. `x = mlp_hc.apply_(x, y, ...)` at `:191`. The block's output is the in-place updated (b,s,4,4096) fp32 stack. `to2(x, None, None)` does nothing. - HyperHead with `mean=True` (`hyperconnections.py:640-641`): `x.mean(dim=2)` gives fp32 (b,s,4096) and has no weights. - Final `RMSNorm` gives fp16 output (`ext.rms_norm` with an fp32 input). - `lm_head` produces the logits (Part A). State: the HC streams are carried only through the forward pass. There is **no HC state in the cache**. Decode cost: each block has 2 sites, and each site runs `hc_mix` (2 kernels) + `rms_norm` + `hc_apply`. That is about 360 launches per token, which `k_hcfuse.py` measured at about 1.1 ms/token on CUDA. `glm-flash-lite/glm53/k_hcfuse.py` is the optional decode fusion, enabled with `GLM53_K_HCFUSE=1`. It applies for R ≤ 8 and D%256==0, and is bit-exact with the stock kernels. It replaces the four launches per site with two: - `k_apply_partials` is the previous site's deferred `hc_apply` fused with this site's `hc_mix` partials. - `k_finalize_norm` is the `hc_mix` finalize fused with the site's RMSNorm. The MLP site's apply is deferred into the next block's attention site and is flushed with the stock `ext.hc_apply` before any other consumer, such as HyperHead. It is an inline CUDA `load_inline` extension, so it is **not usable on XPU**. Leave `GLM53_K_HCFUSE` unset. It is a good template for a later Triton fusion. ### (d) Model load and warmup sequence (serve.py → `model_init.init(args)`) 1. `Config.from_directory` (`model_init.py:207`) parses the config and constructs `SafetensorsCollection(directory, load_method=kwargs.get("load_method"))` (`model/config.py:132`). - Only headers are read here, in Python. - The default `load_method` is `"mt_fread"` (`loader/safetensors.py:413`). - **XPU: pass `load_method="python"`.** `model_init` does not expose this, so patch `model_init.py:207` or the default at `safetensors.py:413`. 2. `Model.from_config` (`model_init.py:262`) builds the modules and `prepare_layer_map`. No ext calls. 3. `Cache(model, 131072, CacheLayer_fp16, max_history, max_batch_size=args.autosplit_max_batch_size=4)` at `model_init.py:303`. It attaches to the model. Cache tensors are allocated when the owning attention or recurrent module loads (`cache/cache.py:134,178`). Details are in Parts D and F. 4. `model.load(use_per_device=None, tensor_p=False, max_batch_size=4, max_chunk_size=8192, ...)` at `model_init.py:360`, which calls `load_gen` (`model/model.py:413`): - `free_mem()` (`gc` plus `torch.cuda.empty_cache`). - `device=None` takes the **autosplit** path: `num_devices = torch.cuda.device_count()` (`model.py:551`), a reserve of 0.5 GB per device, `active_devices=[0..n-1]` as **ints**, then `_load_autosplit` (`model_ls.py:66-295`). Per module: - `torch.device(active_devices[i])` (`:150`). **An int device ordinal means CUDA.** - The first time a device is used, `set_memory_fraction_reserve` (`util/memory.py:27`) calls `touch_device`, `torch.cuda.mem_get_info`, `memory_reserved` and `set_per_process_memory_fraction`. - It allocates the dummy hidden state `zeros((1,8192), long)` on the device. Because Embedding is `prefer_cpu`, `x_device` is the CPU for module 0. - Inside `begin_deferred_load` / `module.load(device, max_chunk_size=8192)` / `end_deferred_load` it runs `stloader_deferred_cuda` or `_cpu`. - **It then runs a real forward of the dummy 8192-token chunk through the module** (`:197-198`). The params are `attn_mode="flash_attn"`, the cache, `past_len=0`, `batch_shape=(1,8192)`, plus `prepare_for_attn` and `prepare_for_recurrence` (`:94-109`). So the **entire 8192-token prefill path runs at load time**. - Memory is measured with `reset_peak_memory_stats`, `memory_allocated`, `max_memory_allocated` and `mem_get_info`. An OOM moves loading on to the next device. - At the end it calls `unset_memory_fraction`, frees the recurrent states and runs `stc.close()` (`stloader_close_file`). - It then calls `free_mem()` and `g_tensor_cache.drop_all()`, and sets `cache.initialized=True` (`model.py:636-645`). - The forward at load can be skipped with `-asnf` (`--autosplit_no_forward`). Single-device loading also skips it: `model.load(device="xpu:0")` takes `_load_single` (`model_ls.py:30-55`), which runs no forward and does no memory accounting. - `model_init.init(args, device=torch.device("xpu",0))` forwards `**kwargs` to `model.load`, so `serve.py` needs a one-line change, or `model_init.py:360` can be patched. - `_load_single` calls `module.load(device)` **without** `max_chunk_size`. Check that the modules have a default for it (Part A and 2 modules size workspaces from it). 5. Warmup (`model_init.py:377`, `Model.warmup` at `model/model.py:190-346`) runs unless `-nw`. - `max_batch_size` keeps its default of 8 (model_init does not pass it). - The families (PAGE_SIZE=256) are: - "rows n" for n in 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 at past_len 0. - "chunk 8192". - "batch B decode" with B = min(8, cap//256, free recurrent slots). Its steps are a pre-fill of min(32, 256-16-2)=32 tokens, a 1-token step, and a q_hist = min(16, cache.max_history+1)-token step with `recurrent_history=True`. - "batch 1 decode" with the same steps. - "long context 8192", which runs 8192 tokens, then 64 tokens, then 1 token, so the DSA top-k sparse regime compiles. - Each step is a real `self.forward(...)` with `attn_mode="flash_attn"`, the cache and the recurrent states. Failures are caught and printed (`:332-336`), so warmup cannot crash a load. - **It can be skipped with `-nw`.** It only pays first-use costs: Triton autotune and compile, the GEMM autotuner and the BC graph capture. - On XPU, skip it while bringing up correctness. Use it later to pre-JIT the Triton kernels. 6. Loading the tokenizer is pure Python. Load-time numerics to preserve: - HC tensors: `.float()`. - RMSNorm weight: fp16, or bf16 if the checkpoint has bf16. - Embedding: on the CPU, fp16 or bf16. ### (e) CUDA dependencies in this part (non-ext) and the torch.xpu mapping | file:line | dependency | reached? | XPU mapping | |---|---|---|---| | `model/model.py:551` | `torch.cuda.device_count()` | load (autosplit) | `torch.xpu.device_count()`. Better: load single-device with `device="xpu:0"`. | | `model/model_ls.py:150-152` | `torch.device(active_devices[i])` with an **int**, which means CUDA | load (autosplit) | `torch.device("xpu", i)`. A bare int is not safe on an XPU build. | | `model/model_ls.py:193` | `measure = load_device.type == "cuda"` | load | On XPU `measure` becomes False, so memory accounting is silently skipped. This is harmless for a single device. Use `in ("cuda","xpu")` with the `torch.xpu` equivalents if accounting is wanted. | | `model/model_ls.py:195-226` | `torch.cuda.reset_peak_memory_stats / memory_allocated / max_memory_allocated / mem_get_info / memory_reserved`, `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError` | load (only if measure) | The `torch.xpu.*` equivalents exist (`reset_peak_memory_stats`, `memory_allocated`, `max_memory_allocated`, `memory_reserved`, `mem_get_info`). Use `torch.OutOfMemoryError`. | | `util/memory.py:13-17` (`touch_device(int)`) | `torch.empty(..., device=int)` | load | Same int-device issue. Pass `"xpu:N"`. | | `util/memory.py:32,37,40,55,56,61,63,70` | `torch.cuda.mem_get_info`, `memory_reserved`, `get_device_properties().total_memory`, `set_per_process_memory_fraction` | load (autosplit) | `torch.xpu.mem_get_info`, `torch.xpu.memory_reserved` and `torch.xpu.get_device_properties(i).total_memory`. **`set_per_process_memory_fraction` may be missing or no-op in torch.xpu** depending on the version. Guard it, or bypass autosplit. | | `util/memory.py:76` (`free_mem`) | `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` | load, and wherever `free_mem` is called | `torch.xpu.empty_cache()` | | `util/memory.py:364-467` (`vram_accounting`) | various `torch.cuda.*` | diagnostics only, not on the path | none | | `util/misc.py:35-43` (`cuda_sync_active`) | `torch.cuda.synchronize` over `cuda:{i}` | callers are outside this part (check the generator in Part F) | `torch.xpu.synchronize()` | | `util/device_copy.py:39-40,81` | `torch.cuda.synchronize` for the P2P probe. Only for cuda→cuda. | no (single device) | none | | `loader/safetensors.py:658` | arena only if `device.type == "cuda"` | load | On XPU the arena is off and each tensor gets its own allocation. This is fine. Expect allocator fragmentation with about 288×45×3 expert tensors. Consider porting the arena with `xpu` as the type. | | `loader/safetensors.py:793-794` | `"cuda": tensor.is_cuda` routes deferred jobs | load (mt_fread) | **Hazard**: an XPU tensor has `is_cuda == False`, so its job goes to `stloader_deferred_cpu`, which writes through a raw **device** pointer from the host. Use `load_method="python"`. | | `stloader.cpp:187-411` | `cudaMallocHost` pinned pool, `cudaMemcpyAsync`, `cudaStreamCreate`, events, `cudaDeviceSynchronize`, bf16 to fp16 kernel | load (mt_fread) | Replaced by the `"python"` loader or by `safetensors` `safe_open(device="xpu:0")` followed by `.to(torch.half)`. | | `model/config.py:74` | `device.type == "cuda"` guard around `exl3_gemv_int8_max_k` | load | Skipped on XPU. OK. | | `modules/embedding.py:164` | `torch.empty_like(x, pin_memory=True)` (generator decode only) | decode | Pinned memory on an XPU build pins for the current accelerator in recent torch (2.5+). Verify it. If it fails, set `pinned_staging` False or drop the staging, which only costs a synchronous H2D of 4096 floats per row. | | `modules/module.py:123`, `util/tensor.py:162` | `x.is_pinned()` lets a copy use `non_blocking` | forward | Works for XPU tensors. A non-blocking H2D copy from pinned memory is stream-ordered on XPU too. | | `modules/rmsnorm.py:220`, `modules/attn.py:1368` | `torch.cuda.synchronize()` | TP import only | none | | `modules/hyperconnections.py:330` | `torch.version.hip`, `dev.type == "cuda"` | GatedResidual only | none | | `exllamav3_ext/graph.cu:33-…` | `cudaStreamBeginCapture`, `cudaGraphInstantiate`, kernel-node param patching, driver-API nodes for Triton cubins | only inside `BC_*` | Don't port. Disable the BC paths so that no graphs are used. `torch.xpu` graphs (`torch.xpu.XPUGraph`, torch ≥ 2.7/2.8) could later capture the eager decode step as a whole. | | `exllamav3_ext/triton_kernel.cpp:7-49` | `cudaFree(nullptr)`, `cuModuleLoadData` of a **cubin**, `cuLaunchKernel` | only inside the BC attention graph | Don't port. Call Triton kernels from Python instead. | | `exllamav3_ext/cuda_host.cpp` | `cudaHostRegister` / `cudaHostGetDevicePointer` | TP and `vision_pinned` only | none | | all `.cu` in this part | `at::cuda::getCurrentCUDAStream`, `OptionalCUDAGuard` | n/a | In the shim, torch ops on XPU tensors use the current XPU stream automatically. | Minimal set for this part to run a correct forward: - `hc_mix` and `hc_mix_num_chunks`, as torch versions of the references above. - `hc_apply`. - `rms_norm`. - Loader: `load_method="python"`, so no stloader ops are needed. - Loading: a single-device `xpu:0` load (`_load_single`), or autosplit with the device-string, int-device and memory-API patches. - Warmup off (`-nw`) during bring-up. Everything else in this part is unreachable for GLM-5.3-Flash: `hc_head`, `gr_mix*`, `det_quant_weight` (HC use), `rms_norm_res_in`, `add`, `cuda_host_*`, `pinned_cuda_view` and `rope`. --- ## Part F. Generator, page table, sampling, cache allocation, recurrent-state machinery Paths are relative to `glm/exllamav3`. "serve config" means `glm53/serve.py -cs 131072 --max-batch-size 8 -chunk_size 8192 -ambs 4`, with no `--mtp`, `-dm`, `-ngram`, `-cq` or `-ccs`, and the samplers built by `make_sampler` (`ArgmaxSampler()` when temperature <= 0, otherwise `ComboSampler(temperature, top_p, top_k, min_p)`). The `GLM53_*` monkeypatches are off by default. ### Key findings first 1. **Decode batch is capped at 4, not 8, under the serve config.** `model_init.init` builds the cache with `max_batch_size = args.autosplit_max_batch_size` (`exllamav3/model_init.py:300-309` (fp16 branch)). `-ambs 4` therefore gives `Cache.num_slots = 4` (`exllamav3/cache/cache.py:161`), and the Generator clamps `self.max_batch_size = min(8, cache.num_slots)` for recurrent models (`exllamav3/generator/generator.py:235`). The warmup batched family also uses `min(8, len(cache.free_list)) = 4` (`exllamav3/model/model.py:281-285`). The decode shapes to support are bsz 1-4 with q_len 1. Raising `-ambs` to 8 would make bsz 5-8 reachable. 2. **No drafting is reached.** `model_init` sets `use_mtp` only when `--mtp` is passed or `-dm` points at the model dir (`exllamav3/model_init.py:197-204`). `--ngram_match_min` defaults to 0 (`model_init.py:121`), so `num_draft_tokens = 0` (`generator.py:168-171`) and `max_history = 0` (`model_init.py:270-275`). As a result `recurrent_history` is always False, `GDNState.last_history` is always 0, and the GDN history dimension is 1. **MTP is not used by default; `glm5_next_mtp.py` is not on the path.** 3. **On the default path the sampler is always the fused kernel.** `EXL3_FUSED_SAMPLER` defaults to on (`generator/sampler/custom.py:19`). Both `ArgmaxSampler` and every `ComboSampler` that serve.py builds collapse to a single `SS_Fused` step with no head steps, so `fused_only = True`. That means `ext.fused_sampler` is the **only** sampling extension op reached. `gumbel_noise_*`, `apply_rep_pens`, `apply_pres_freq_pens`, `dry_penalty`, `adaptivep_gumbel_noise_f32`, `apply_logit_bitmask`, `argmax_sample` and `gumbel_sample` are not reached. 4. **The generator calls a CUDA-only API on the decode path.** `torch.cuda.synchronize(batch_logits.device)` at `generator/generator.py:1128` runs every decode step and raises on an XPU device or on a CUDA-less torch. The pinned-readback branch is gated on `next_token.is_cuda` (`generator.py:1122`), which is False on XPU. Both must be patched (see section D). 5. **Partial-page prompt reuse (`count_match_tensor`, `Cache.copy_page`) is skipped for recurrent models** (`generator/job.py:1268`), so neither is reached. Prompt-cache reuse for GLM5Next works only at page granularity and only up to the last page that has a stashed recurrent checkpoint. ### (a) Op table (this part) Hot-path rank: D = every decode step; P = prefill/allocation; R = rare (checkpoints, requeue); I = idle-time housekeeping; N = not reached under the serve config. | op | category | when reached | rank | Python call sites | condition / avoidable? | recommended XPU path | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | `fused_sampler` | sampling | once per active job per decode step (bsz=1 each call) | D | `generator/sampler/custom.py:248` (SS_Fused.run) | Avoidable with `EXL3_FUSED_SAMPLER=0`, which falls back to step-by-step torch + `gumbel_noise_*` | Implement in torch (modes 0-3) inside the shim. The eager fallback still needs `gumbel_noise_*`, so the fused op is the smaller port | | `FUSED_SAMPLER_MAX_BLOCKS` (=128), `FUSED_SAMPLER_HIST_STRIDE` (=24640) | module attrs | read when SS_Fused allocates workspaces | D | `custom.py:229`, `custom.py:240` | required attrs | export the same int constants; the buffers can be ignored | | `cache_rotate` | page table / defrag | `on_queue_drained()` → `PageTable.defrag()`, only when the queue is empty, ≥ 8×512 page accesses since the last defrag, and >10% of pages misplaced | I | `generator/pagetable.py:1025` | Avoidable: `Generator(enable_defrag=False)`, or a shim no-op is safe for correctness. Defrag only improves page contiguity | torch per-page `copy_` loop (see detail), or patch `defrag` to return early | | `partial_strings_match` | job / stop strings (CPU) | per sampled token, only if the request has stop **strings** (EOS ids alone do not trigger it) | D (CPU) | `generator/job.py:988` (stop strings); `job.py:966` (banned strings, N) | CPU-only pybind; no GPU | pure Python/numpy reimplementation | | `count_match_tensor` | job / partial-page reuse (CPU) | — | N | `generator/job.py:1275` | skipped because `recurrent_cache is not None` | pure Python, or omit | | `batched_conv_rewind`, `batched_state_rewind`, `ConvRewindJob`, `StateRewindJob` | recurrent-state rewind | only via `GDNState.rewind()`: draft rejection (`generator.py:1089`, `1246`), atomic-MM prefill overrun (`job.py:1437-1438`), banned-string rewind (not taken, since `rollback_capacity()==0`) | N | `modules/gated_delta_net.py:62-64`, `266`, `283` | not reached without drafts | patch `_collect_rewind_jobs`/`_dispatch_rewind_jobs` to call the existing torch `GDNLayerState.rewind()` (`gated_delta_net.py:238-250`). The raw-pointer job classes cannot be honoured in torch | | `gumbel_noise_f16` / `_f32` / `_log` | sampling (eager) | only with `EXL3_FUSED_SAMPLER=0` or a non-collapsible stack | N | `custom.py:120,123,126,129,133` | avoidable (default) | torch if ever needed (see detail) | | `apply_logit_bitmask` | sampling | packed-bitmask filter mask on a non-fused stack | N | `custom.py:1226` | no filters in serve.py | — | | `apply_rep_pens`, `apply_pres_freq_pens`, `dry_penalty`, `adaptivep_gumbel_noise_f32` | sampling penalties | serve.py's ComboSampler leaves rep_p=1, pres/freq=0, dry=0, adaptive=1, so `alt()` turns each into `SS_NoOp` | N | `custom.py:770,779,833,843,1019,1079` | avoidable (default) | — | | `argmax_sample`, `gumbel_sample` | sampling (basic) | never called from library Python (only `tests/test_ext_sampling_edges_.py`) | N | — | — | — | | `BC_SAM` | n-gram drafting | `ngram_match_min > 0` | N | `generator/job.py:1173` | default 0 | — | | `quant_cache_*`, `dequant_cache_*` | cache quant | only with `-cq` (and `CacheLayer_MLA_quant` packing in `mla_triton`) | N | `cache/quant.py`, `attention_fn/mla_triton.py` | `-cq` not passed | — | | `paged_kv_cache_update`, `dspark_write_rows` | other cache layers | MHA fp16 cache / DSpark only; MLA uses Triton `mla_kv_append` | N | `cache/fp16.py:76`, `cache/dsa.py:486` | not this model | — | | `histogram`, `count_inf_nan` | diagnostics | calibration/eval only (`linear.py:569` under `params["capture"]`) | N | — | — | — | The `ext.encode` / `ext.endswith` / `ext.lower` names in the earlier grep of `generator/job.py` are false positives. They are `held_text.lower().encode(...)` and `held_text.endswith(...)`, where `held_text` happens to contain `ext.`. The same applies to `ext.device`, `ext.shape` and `ext.index_*` in `modules/moe_batch_recon.py`. ### (b) Per-op detail #### fused_sampler (hot, the only GPU sampling op on the default path) **Binding:** `m.def("fused_sampler", &fused_sampler)` (`exllamav3_ext/bindings.cpp:238`), with the module attrs `FUSED_SAMPLER_MAX_BLOCKS = 128` and `FUSED_SAMPLER_HIST_STRIDE = 2*1024*12 + 64 = 24640` (`exllamav3_ext/generator/sampling_fused.cuh:5-11`). **Signature** (`exllamav3_ext/generator/sampling_fused.cu:770-791`), positional order as called at `custom.py:248-264`: ``` fused_sampler( logits: Tensor (bsz, dim) half|float, contiguous # per-job (1, vocab) view of batch_logits logit_mask: Optional[Tensor] (1|bsz, >=size) half # additive mask; None on serve path logit_bitmask: Optional[Tensor] (1|bsz, words) int32 # bit clear = masked; None on serve path out: Tensor (bsz, 1) int64 # written in place workspace: Tensor float, >= bsz*128*3 # scratch (partial max + ValIdx partials) size: int # effective vocab bound <= dim: min(dim, tokenizer.actual_vocab_size[, mask width]) inv_temp: float # 1/T minp_log: float # additive log-space min-P threshold random: uint32 # per-sample seed (job.rng.randint(0, 2**32-1), job.py:578) mode: int # 0 greedy, 1 gumbel, 2 gumbel+minP, 3 gumbel+topK/topP(+minP) filters: int # mode 3 bits: 1=TOPK, 2=TOPP, 4=MINP top_k: int; top_p: float inv_temp_filter: float # 1/T if temperature precedes the filters in the stack, else 1.0 histogram: Optional[Tensor] uint8 >= bsz*24640, 8-byte aligned # mode 3 only; zeroed internally ) ``` Before the call, `CustomSampler.forward` writes `-inf` in place into `logits[..., tokenizer.actual_vocab_size:]` (`custom.py:1182-1183`) and passes `size = min(dim, actual_vocab_size)` (`custom.py:1200-1213`). **How serve.py stacks collapse** (`presets.py:105-151`, `custom.py:279-344`, `custom.py:1131-1159`): | serve.py request | stack after `alt()` | SS_Fused mode | |---|---|---| | temperature <= 0 → `ArgmaxSampler` | `[Argmax]` | 0 (greedy) | | `ComboSampler(T, top_p=1, top_k=0, min_p=0)` | `[Temperature(T)?, Sample]` (Temperature is a NoOp when T=1) | 1, inv_temp=1/T | | `ComboSampler(..., min_p>0)` only | `[Temp?, MinP, Sample]` | 2, minp_log = T·ln(min_p) if Temp present (temp_first) else ln(min_p) | | any top_k>1 and/or top_p<1 | `[Temp?, MinP?, TopK?, TopP?, Sample]` | 3, filters bitset; inv_temp_filter = 1/T if Temp present else 1 | | top_k == 1, or top_p == 0 (impossible via serve.py, which maps 0 → 1.0) | — | 0 | `ComboSampler` places temperature first (`temp_last=False`), so when T≠1 the filters act on the tempered distribution. `make_sampler` reads `top_p or 1.0` and `top_k or 0`, so the typical "temperature 1.0, top_p 0.95" request gives mode 3 with `filters=TOPP`, `inv_temp=1` and `inv_temp_filter=1`. **Exact math** (per row; `x_i` = fp32(logit_i) plus mask, for i < size): - **Mode 0:** `argmax_i x_i`. Ties go to the lowest index (`fs_amax_first`, `sampling_fused.cu:62-69`). If every value is -inf or NaN the result is index 0 (`out = idx==INT_MAX ? 0 : idx`, `:764`). NaN never wins because both `>` and `==` are false. - **Gumbel noise** (modes 1-3, `sampling_fused.cu:722-727`): `u = curand_uniform(Philox4x32-10; seed = random (u32 widened to u64), subsequence = row*dim + i, offset 0)`. - `curand_uniform` returns `x*2^-32 + 2^-33` with `x` = the first 32-bit output, so u ∈ (0, 1]. - Philox setup: counter = (0, 0, subseq_lo, subseq_hi); key = (seed_lo, 0); standard 10 rounds with M0=0xD2511F53, M1=0xCD9E8D57, W0=0x9E3779B9, W1=0xBB67AE85; output word .x. - `g = -__logf(max(-__logf(max(min(u, 0.99999994f), 1e-20)), 1e-20))`. - Objective: `v_i = x_i * inv_temp + g_i`. The result is `argmax v` with lowest-index tie-break. - Noise depends only on (random, flat index), so skipped (filtered) elements do not shift anyone else's noise. - Bit-matching the CUDA stream is **not** required for correctness. A torch port may use `torch.rand` with an XPU `torch.Generator` seeded from `random`, using the same clamp/log formula. - **Mode 2:** `M = max_i x_i` (masked). Elements with `x_i < M + minp_log` are dropped, then Gumbel-argmax runs over the rest. - **Mode 3:** kept set K, then Gumbel-argmax over K. - The min-P threshold is the same as mode 2 when the MINP bit is set. - Top-K keeps the k largest (after min-P). All tokens tied with the k-th value are kept, since ties share a sub-bucket. - Top-P uses weights `w_i = exp((x_i - M) * inv_temp_filter)` over the min-P-filtered, top-K-truncated set. `target = top_p * Σw`. Walking tokens in descending order, the token whose cumulative mass first **strictly exceeds** the target is dropped, along with everything below it. The top token is always kept. - This matches the eager rule `mask = cumsum(p) <= top_p; p[1:] *= mask[1:]` (`custom.py:465-468`) up to tie handling. The eager path renormalizes after MinP/TopK through `SS_Normalize` before TopP; the fused histogram excludes filtered tokens from Σw, which is the same thing. - Implementation detail that matters only for bit-exactness: CUDA uses a 1024-bucket coarse histogram over 32 nats below M (bin = `(M - x)*1024/32*inv_temp_filter`), then 1024 refinement sub-buckets (1/32768 nat) and u64 fixed-point mass at 2^40. Logits deeper than 32 nats land in the last bucket, which a top-K crossing never truncates. A torch reimplementation by sort + cumsum gives the same kept set except at exact ties and in that deep tail. - **Torch reference, correct rather than fast:** ```python x = logits.float()[:, :size] # mask added M = x.max(-1, keepdim=True) keep = x >= M + minp_log # only if the MINP bit is set (or mode 2); with -inf excluded # top-K / top-P: sort x[keep] descending, apply the rules above v = torch.where(keep, x * inv_temp + gumbel(u), -inf) out = v.argmax(-1) # stable lowest-index tie-break ``` `torch.argmax` returns the first maximal index, which matches the CUDA tie-break. #### cache_rotate (idle defrag) **Signature** (`exllamav3_ext/generator/cache.cu:84-108`): `cache_rotate(cache: Tensor (num_pages, ...) any dtype, contiguous; order: Tensor (2n,) int32 on the cache device; temp: Tensor, nbytes == one page)`. It returns nothing and writes `cache` and `temp` in place. If page_size or n is 0 it returns immediately, so the zero-width MLA `v` plane (`qk_rope_head_dim == 0`) is a no-op. **Semantics:** ``` for i in range(n): a, b = order[2i], order[2i+1] (page[a] if a >= 0 else temp) <- (page[b] if b >= 0 else temp) ``` The copies run strictly in order, bytewise per page. Each CTA owns a disjoint byte range of every page, so the sequential order holds per byte. **Order encoding** (`pagetable.py:977-999`): each permutation cycle is encoded as `[-1, s, d0, s0, d1, s1, ..., dk, -1]`. The first pair saves page `s` to temp, the middle pairs shift pages along the cycle, and the last pair restores temp into `dk`. **Caller** (`pagetable.py:1018-1025`): rotates every tensor in `Cache.get_all_tensors()`. For GLM5Next that is, per MLA layer, `k` (latent), `v` (0-wide), `k_idx` and `k_pool`. The draft cache is not rotated because none exists. Recurrent state is not paged and is not rotated. **Torch port:** ```python flat = cache.view(cache.shape[0], -1) t = temp.view(-1) if flat.shape[1] == 0: return for a, b in pairs(order.cpu().tolist()): (flat[a] if a >= 0 else t).copy_(flat[b] if b >= 0 else t) ``` Alternatively, set `enable_defrag=False`. Defrag is a pure layout optimization and does not affect correctness. #### partial_strings_match (CPU) **Signature** (`exllamav3_ext/generator/strings.cpp:16-63`): `partial_strings_match(match: buffer u8 = UTF-32LE of held_text, offsets: buffer u8 = int32 byte offsets (n+1), strings: buffer u8 = concatenated UTF-32LE needles) -> int`. **Return value:** - -1: no match. - -2: some needle runs off the right end of `q` (a partial match at the tail) starting before every full match. - k ≥ 0: the code-point index of the **earliest** full match over all needles. **Algorithm:** for each needle and each start `a0 < min(best_full, best_partial)`, extend the match. A full match sets `best_full = a0` and breaks; running off the end of `q` sets `best_partial = a0` and breaks. The function returns -2 if `best_partial < best_full`, otherwise the full index or -1. **Caller:** `job.py:988` decodes the result as a string index into `held_text`. A plain Python port over `str` is exact. #### batched_conv_rewind / batched_state_rewind (not reached; listed because they take raw pointers) - `ConvRewindJob(src, dst, dim, cdim, stride)` copies `conv_state[slot, :, :cdim] <- conv_state[slot, :, p-cdim:p]` in bf16 (`gdn.cu:1924-1945`, `gated_delta_net.py:253-272`). - `StateRewindJob(src, dst, num_elements)` copies `recurrent_state[slot, 0] <- recurrent_state[slot, last_history+1-n]` as a flat fp32 copy of `stride(1)` elements (`gdn.cu:1949-1959`, `gated_delta_net.py:275-287`). - The torch equivalent already exists in `GDNLayerState.rewind()` (`gated_delta_net.py:238-250`). Route `_dispatch_rewind_jobs` to it in the shim. Do not try to emulate pointer jobs. #### gumbel_noise_f16 / f32 / log (eager path only) Source: `exllamav3_ext/generator/gumbel.cu:12-169`. All variants use the same Philox per element: seed = `random`, subsequence = flat element index, `curand_uniform`, and the same `gumbel()` clamp formula. | variant | output | |---|---| | f16 | `out = half_rn(float(in) + g)`, processed in element pairs, each element with its own subsequence | | f32 | `out = in + g` | | log | `out = __logf(p) + g` | `out` may alias `in`. ### (c) Generator step for GLM5Next (single GPU, no TP, no drafts) **Construction** (`generator.py:138-247`): - `PageTable(cache)`: `max_pages = 131072/256 = 512` pages of 256 tokens (`constants.py:2`). - `RecurrentCache(model, 4 GiB)`: an OrderedDict LRU of host-RAM stashes. `-rcs` defaults to 4.0 and serve.py passes `int(args.recurrent_cache_size*1024**3)`. - `cache.reset_states()`, and `max_batch_size = min(8, 4) = 4`. - `recurrent_checkpoint_interval = 2048` (model caps), `recurrent_checkpoint_interval_pp = ceil(32768/8192)*8192 = 32768`. - `cpu_cache_size = 0`, so there is no `CPUPageCache`. - `max_q_size` is computed but never stored or used (`generator.py:176`). It is inert. **Cache allocation for `-cs 131072`** (`model_init.py:300-309`, `cache/cache.py:145-171`, `cache/mla.py:31-82`, `modules/gated_delta_net.py:174-216`). Tensors are allocated at `model.load()` by each module's `load_local` (`mla_attn.py:312-313`, `gated_delta_net.py:622-623`) with `torch.zeros/empty(..., device=<layer device>)`. | per layer | tensor | shape | dtype | size | |---|---|---|---|---| | MLA (11 layers: 3, 7, ..., 43; all `indexer_types = "full"`) | `k` (latent ckv) | (512, 256, 1, 512) | fp16 | 128 MiB | | | `v` (rope key) | (512, 256, 1, **0**) | fp16 | 0 | | | `k_idx` (indexer keys: `idx_plane_dim = index_head_dim*2 = 256` because `index_kpool` is set, holding [k ‖ gate scores]) | (512, 256, 256) | fp16 | 64 MiB | | | `k_pool` (pooled keys, `PAGE_SIZE // kpool = 64` per page) | (512, 64, 128) | fp16 | 8 MiB | | KDA (34 layers) | `conv_state` (`fdim_qkv = 2*64*128 + 64*128 = 24576`, `conv_kernel 4 + max_history 0`) | (4 slots, 24576, 4) | bf16 | 0.75 MiB | | | `recurrent_state` (slots, history+1, heads, k_dim, v_dim) | (4, 1, 64, 128, 128) | fp32 | 16 MiB | Totals: - MLA ≈ 200 MiB/layer × 11 ≈ 2.15 GiB of paged KV. - KDA state ≈ 16.75 MiB × 34 ≈ 570 MiB. - One recurrent checkpoint (`GDNState.checkpoint_size`) = 34 × (24576·4·2 + 64·128·128·4) B ≈ 142 MiB of host RAM, so the 4 GiB recurrent cache holds about 28 checkpoints. **`iterate()`** (`generator.py:443-563`). One call per asyncio tick (`async_generator.py:36`). It is fully synchronous on the event-loop thread. **1. `iterate_start_jobs`** (`generator.py:1386-1443`). Admits jobs while `sum(seqs) < 4` and enough unreferenced pages exist. - `Job.allocate_pages` → `Sequence.allocate_pages` (`pagetable.py:258-297`): - Prompt pages are keyed by a chained blake2b hash (CPU). - Pages are revived by hash or evicted. - The usable cached prefix is cut back to the last page that **also** has a stashed recurrent checkpoint (`pagetable.py:626-634`). - Then `cache.get_new_state()` takes a slot from the free list. `GDNState(clear=True)` zeroes `conv_state[slot]` and `recurrent_state[slot]` with torch `zero_`. - Or `cache.new_from_stashed(stash, position=cached_pages*256)` → `GDNLayerState.unstash` runs `copy_(cpu_tensor)` into `recurrent_state[slot,:1]` and `conv_state[slot,:,:4]` (`gated_delta_net.py:298-302`). **2. Prefill, one chunk per job per iterate** (`job.py:1203-1479`). - Chunk: `[kv_position, min(floor((kv_position+8192)/256)*256, len-1))`. - The **last prompt token is never prefilled**; it becomes the first decode input. - Because the model is recurrent, a chunk that would cross the last full-page boundary of the prompt is split at that boundary (`job.py:1305-1313`). The forward ending there is followed by `maybe_stash_recurrent(interval=256)`, so the prompt's last full page gets a checkpoint. - Call: `model.prefill(ids (1, n) long CPU, params)`, with params: - `attn_mode = "flash_attn"` - `block_table = seq.block_index_tensor` (CPU int32 (1, pages), not pinned) - `cache` - `cache_seqlens = torch.tensor([start], int32)` (CPU) - `recurrent_states = [state]` - `indexed_embeddings = []`, `inv_freq = None`, `mm_span_prefix = 0` - `Model.prefill` (`model/model.py:349-371`) → `prepare_inputs` (`prepare_flash_attn` + `prepare_for_recurrence`, which adds `recurrent_slots`, a CPU int32 tensor with `_static_dev_cache`) → `prefill_ls`, which stops after the last TransformerBlock (`model_ls.py:298-314`; HyperHead, norm and lm_head are skipped) → `advance_recurrent_states` (position += n; `last_history = 0`). - Prefill is always batch 1. It interleaves with the decode forward of other jobs in the same `iterate()`. **3. Recurrent checkpoints** (`generator.py:585-587`, `job.py:1562-1620`). Each iterate, for every active job: - Stash when `(kv_position - cached_pages*256) % interval == 0` and the page is full. - interval = 2048 once `kv_position >= prompt_len - 2*8192`, otherwise 32768. - `RecurrentCache.put` (`cache/recurrent.py:55-95`) → `GDNState.stash` → per layer `recurrent_state[slot,:1].cpu()` and `conv_state[slot,:,:4].cpu()` (`gated_delta_net.py:290-295`). These are **synchronous D2H copies to pageable memory**, about 142 MiB per stash. Eviction is LRU, dropping stranded entries first. - When a requeue happens (`GLM53_MAX_RQ_TOKENS`, aligned to the 2048-token checkpoint boundary at `job.py:1110-1117`), the job stashes at a page boundary before releasing its pages (`generator.py:1339-1343`). The requeued job then resumes from KV pages + stash. **4. Decode, `iterate_gen`** (`generator.py:943-1353`). Assembles a compact batch of every prefill-complete job (bsz ≤ 4): - `block_index`: pinned int32 staging `(bsz, ceil(max_seq_len/256) rounded up to a multiple of 16)`, zero-filled, each row copied from `seq.block_index_tensor`. - `cache_seqlens`: pinned int32 `(bsz,)` = `seq.kv_position`. - `positions = None`: no mrope caps; the model is NoPE. - `batch_ids`: pinned int64 `(bsz, 1)` = each sequence's next token. - `recurrent_states`: a list of per-job `GDNState`. Slot indices become `recurrent_slots` via `prepare_for_recurrence`. - Params: `{attn_mode: "flash_attn", block_table, cache, cache_seqlens, recurrent_states, indexed_embeddings: [], positions: None, recurrent_history: False, pinned_staging: True}`. `pinned_staging` is not read anywhere. - `model.forward(batch_ids, params)` returns logits `(bsz, 1, vocab=154880)`. - Modules upload `block_table`/`cache_seqlens` with `get_for_device` (`util/tensor.py:115-165`). Pinned sources are copied `non_blocking=True`. This is safe only because the generator synchronizes before the staging buffers are refilled next iteration. - Sampling (`generator.py:1108-1151`): for each job, `job.receive_logits(batch_logits[a:b])` → `CustomSampler.forward(logits (1,1,V), current_device_ids, rand_u32, tokenizer, logit_mask=None)` → one `fused_sampler` call per job. - Before that, `job.prepare_sampling_past_ids` (`job.py:1531-1547`) stages all sequence ids into pinned memory and copies them H2D `non_blocking` every step for ComboSampler. `SS_RepP.reqs_past_ids()` returns True even when RepP collapses to a NoOp (`custom.py:796-797`, `1136`). This is wasted but harmless; ArgmaxSampler skips it. - On CUDA each 1-element token is copied into a pinned buffer, then one `torch.cuda.synchronize(device)` runs, then `receive_sample` does `next_token.cpu().item()` (`job.py:620-621`), decodes the text, and checks stop tokens and strings. - Completed or requeued jobs `deallocate_pages()` and `free()` their state slot. When the queue drains: `on_queue_drained()` → `recurrent_cache.prune_stranded()`, `pagetable.defrag()` (`cache_rotate`) and `malloc_trim()` (ctypes libc, not CUDA). **Warmup** (`model/model.py:190-345`, called by `model_init.init` before the generator exists): - Rectangular `batch_shape` mode with `past_len`. - Single-row families at 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 tokens. - One 8192-token chunk. - A bsz-4 family `[(32, False), (1, False)]` and a bsz-1 family with the same steps. There is no history step because `max_history = 0`. - A long-context family `[(8192), (64), (1)]` on 8448 cache rows. - Uses `cache.get_new_state()` / `release_state`. `-nw` skips warmup. ### (d) Non-ext CUDA dependencies in this part | file:line | dependency | reached? | XPU mapping | |---|---|---|---| | `generator/generator.py:1128` | `torch.cuda.synchronize(batch_logits.device)` | **D, every decode step** | **Must patch:** `torch.xpu.synchronize(dev)` (or `torch.accelerator.synchronize`). It is also the sync point that makes refilling the pinned staging buffers safe, so do not turn it into a no-op | | `generator/generator.py:1122` | `next_token.is_cuda` gates the pinned-readback batching | D | Use `next_token.device.type != "cpu"`. The generator's thread pool (`generator.py:188`) only runs grammar filters, which are not reached. ### Minimal set for this part (correct, not fast) 1. `ext.fused_sampler` in torch, modes 0-3 (mode 0 for greedy, 1 or 3 for typical requests), plus the attrs `FUSED_SAMPLER_MAX_BLOCKS` and `FUSED_SAMPLER_HIST_STRIDE`. 2. `ext.partial_strings_match` in Python (CPU), needed only when requests send stop strings. 3. `ext.cache_rotate` as a torch loop, or `enable_defrag=False`. 4. Python patches: - `generator.py:1122/1128` → device-agnostic synchronize. - `util/misc.py:cuda_sync_active` → XPU. - Check that `pin_memory=True` works on the XPU torch. - Optionally route `_dispatch_rewind_jobs` to the torch `GDNLayerState.rewind()`. Nothing else in the generator/cache/sampler path calls into `exllamav3_ext`. --- ## 6. Minimal op set for a correct (not fast) forward Configuration (section 3): - `EXL3_BC_GDN=0`, `EXL3_BC_ATTN=0`, `TRITON_F32_DEFAULT=ieee`; - `config.infer_params.no_reconstruct = True`, `load_method="python"`, single-device `xpu:0` load, `-nw`; - no `-cq`, no `--mtp`, `GLM53_*` unset; - the Python patches at `block_sparse_mlp.py:818`, `conv1d.py:245/279`, `vendor/fla/utils.py:117`, the MLA/DSA `torch.cuda.device` contexts and SM count, and `generator.py:1122/1128`. Shim implementations needed: | area | ops | |---|---| | **EXL3 / FP16 linear** (Part A) | `BC_LinearEXL3` (ctor fields + `run` + `run_alloc` = mul1 trellis decode → `diag(suh)·H·W_hat·H·diag(svh)` applied as `had128(x∘suh) @ W_hat → had128 ∘ svh`, fp32 accumulate, column-sliced for lm_head), `BC_LinearFP16`, `hgemm` | | **MLP / MoE** (Part B) | `BC_GatedMLP` (Python wrapper), `silu_mul`, `routing_ds3_nogroup`, `det_quant_weight` (no-op) | | **Hyper-connections / norms** (Part E) | `hc_mix`, `hc_mix_num_chunks`, `hc_apply`, `rms_norm` | | **KDA** (Part C) | `cuda_recurrent_gated_delta_rule` (channelwise; history not needed), `gated_rms_norm`, `BC_GatedRMSNorm` | | **MLA** (Part D) | `dsa_topk` | | **Generator** (Part F) | `fused_sampler` + `FUSED_SAMPLER_MAX_BLOCKS` / `FUSED_SAMPLER_HIST_STRIDE`, `partial_strings_match` (only with stop strings), `cache_rotate` (or `enable_defrag=False`) | | **Everything else in `bindings.cpp`** | name-only stubs that raise. `relu_mul` is read at import time | That is **16 ext functions/classes with real bodies** (most of them small elementwise or reduction ops), plus three groups of existing Triton kernels: 7 fla KDA kernels, 3 conv kernels, and about 12 MLA/DSA kernels. They should run on Intel Triton with the noted launch-config tweaks. The one heavy piece is the EXL3 linear, and the existing trellis-serve SYCL kernels (`linear`, `exl3_reconstruct`, `had_in_rm`, `had_out_h`) already match its layout bit for bit. Suggested order to bring it up: 1. EXL3 linear reference, checked against the Part A torch reference on one real tensor. 2. `rms_norm`, `hc_mix`, `hc_apply`. 3. Routing, `silu_mul`, `BC_GatedMLP`. 4. KDA (decode recurrent op, then the fla chunk kernels on XPU). 5. MLA dense, then sparse with `dsa_topk`. 6. `fused_sampler`. 7. Compare logits with a CUDA run using `EXL3_INT8_GEMV=0 EXL3_BC_GDN=0`. ## EXL3 conversion script ### Arguments #### Basic - **-i / --in_dir *directory***: The source model to convert, in unquantized HF format. The directory should contain at least a `config.json` file, a `tokenizer.json` file and one or more `.safetensors` files containing weights. - **-o / --out_dir *directory***: The destination directory for the converted **EXL3** model. Will be created if it doesn't exist, or overwritten if it does. - **-w / --work_dir *directory***: Working directory for temporary files. It should have enough free space to store an entire copy of the output model. This is also where checkpoints are stored and the only required argument if **-r / --resume** is specified. - **-ss / --shard_size *float***: Output shard size, in megabytes. Default is 8192. Set this to 0 to disable sharding. Note that writing very large `.safetensors` files can require a lot of system RAM. - **-b / --bits *float***: Target average number of bits per weight. - **-hb / --head_bits *int***: Number of bits per weight for the lm_head (output) layer of the model. Must be an integer from 1 to 8, default is 6. - **-mb / --mtp_bits *int***: Number of bits per weight for the MTP (multi-token prediction) layers, for models that have them. Must be an integer from 1 to 8, or 16 to store the MTP layers unquantized. Default is 4. MTP layers are quantized without calibration. - **-vb / --vision_bits *int***: Number of bits per weight for the vision component, for models that have one. Must be an integer from 1 to 8, or 16 to copy the vision model unquantized. The default depends on the architecture: vision towers validated for low-bitrate quantization default to 6, everything else to 16. Vision layers are quantized without calibration. - **-ngb / --ngram_bits *int***: Bits per weight for hashed n-gram embedding tables (PLE models, e.g. Qwen3.8-Flash-Next). Must be an integer from 1 to 8, default is `--bits` rounded to the nearest integer. The table is quantized (without calibration) before the layer-by-layer conversion and written as a standalone `ngram_embedding.safetensors` in the output directory; calibration forwards then run against the quantized table. Table quantization is resumable together with the rest of the job. - **-eb / --embed_bits *int***: Bits per weight for the token embedding table. Must be an integer from 1 to 8, or 16 (the default) to store the table unquantized. Each embedding is split into 256-wide groups that are rotated and trellis-coded without calibration, and the following layers are calibrated against the quantized table. A quantized table is decoded on the GPU at inference, and can be held in system RAM or streamed from disk (`--embed_disk` in `model_init`-based scripts). The table of an existing EXL3 model can be quantized with `util/recompile.py --embed_bits`. - **-ngf / --ngram_file *file***: Pre-quantized n-gram table (from `util/convert_ngram.py`) to copy into the output model instead of quantizing the table as part of the job. Overrides `--ngram_bits`. - **-hq / --hq**: Increase the bitrate of select layers, such as attention and shared-expert layers. Final model bitrate may be somewhat higher than requested by `--bits`, but for MoE models this is typically a very small increase in size (0.05 - 0.10 bpw) for a disproportionately large increase in model fidelity. - **-rcp / --recipe *file***: Per-tensor bitrate recipe (YAML, e.g. from `sc_optimize.py`), used in place of the budgeted allocation from `--bits`/`--head_bits`. More about optimized recipes [here](optimize.md). - **-cd / --cal_data *file***: Calibration data file (safetensors with packed token rows, e.g. from `sc_trace.py`) used instead of the bundled corpus mix. #### Advanced (generally disregard these options) - **--out_scales *str***: Output channel scales. Options are "auto" (default), "always", "never" and "yaqa". In auto mode every tensor gets output scales, except gate and up projections of gated MLPs (including MoE experts) where calibration statistics show that the channels the MLP's output is most sensitive to are also the ones with the largest weights. Scaling would concentrate the quantization error in exactly those channels. In yaqa mode the scales of each tensor follow how much the model's output depends on each of its output channels, taken from the diagonal of the tensor's output-side Hessian in `--hessians`, so the quantization error lands in the channels where it costs the least. Tensors without a Hessian file are treated as in auto mode. See `science/yaqa.md`. - **-hess / --hessians *directory***: Precomputed per-tensor Hessians from `util/yaqa_hessians.py`, one file per tensor. Files with an output-side factor (`hout`) switch the rounding of that tensor to two-sided LDLQ, files with an input-side factor (`hin`) replace the calibration passes, and `hout` or its diagonal (`hout_diag`) feeds `--out_scales yaqa`. An export made with `--diag` holds the diagonal only, which is enough for the output scales and leaves the rounding alone. - **-h1 / --hessians_one_sided**: Keep the regular one-sided LDLQ for tensors that have `hout`, so that `--hessians` only serves `--out_scales yaqa`. - **-hreg / --hessians_reg *float***: Diagonal regularization of the output-side Hessian in two-sided LDLQ, relative to its mean diagonal. Larger values blend toward one-sided LDLQ. Default is 0.025. - **-cb / --codebook *str***: Trellis codebook: "mul1" (default), "mcg" or "3inst". The mul1 codebook is required by some optimized inference paths (int8 GEMV, CPU expert offload); there is no reason to pick another codebook except for testing. #### Checkpoints - **-cpi / --checkpoint_interval *int***: Minimum interval (in seconds) between checkpoints. Default is 120. - **-r / --resume**: Resume an interrupted job pointed to by **-w / --work_dir** from the latest checkpoint. If resuming a job, all other arguments such as input and output directories, bitrate etc. are restored from the old job, though some can be overridden. Note that resuming is now explicit, reversing the behavior from ExLlamaV2. #### Performance - **-d / --devices *list***: Comma-separated list of GPU device IDs to use during quantization. By default only the first visible device (device 0) is used. Adding more devices can speed up quantization if there is sufficient PCIe bandwidth between them. This does not affect memory usage on the first GPU, and very little memory is used on the others, since only the most compute intensive operation (trellis encoding) is distributed. - **-dr / --device_ratios *list***: Ratio as comma-separated list. Determines how the encoding workload is distributed when using multiple devices. This is useful if using GPUs with dissimilar compute performance, to prevent slower GPUs from becoming bottlenecks. Ratios are relative, i.e. `1,1,3` is the same ratio as `3,3,9`. Recommendation is to omit this argument; by default, ratios are autotuned to maximize usage across GPUs. - **-cbs / --calib_batch_rows *N|auto***: Calibration rows per forward during capture and state advance. Default `auto` selects 32, capped at the calibration row count. Batched forwards automatically retry the same rows with half-sized batches after CUDA out-of-memory, down to one row. - **-cdn / --calib_devices *N|auto***: Calibration forward devices. Default `auto` uses one of the selected `-d` devices when the effective batch is larger than one, otherwise all selected devices. An integer selects the first N devices; `0` selects all. Quantization always uses all devices specified by `-d`. - **Restore old calibration behavior**: use `-cbs 1 -cdn 0`. Both options are momentary and may be changed when resuming with `-r`; they are not stored in `args.json`. - **Measured conversion speed**: On one Qwen3.5 35B-A3B MoE layer (256 experts, 256 calibration rows), capture took 114 s on the previous 2-device path, 36 s on the previous 1-device path, 27 s with `-cbs 16` on 2 devices, 21 s with `-cbs 16 -cdn 1`, and 16 s with `-cbs 32 -cdn 1`. On an 8x B300, 512-row run, capture per layer changed from 330 s to 23 s and total per-layer time from about 520 s to 219 s. An embedding/layer-0 A/B (64 rows, same recipe, `-sh -v -cb mul1`) compared 846 tensors: median proxy relative difference 0 at printed precision; mean proxy 1.02814e-4 vs 1.02826e-4; RFN 0.019624 vs 0.019572 and SQNR 36.380 vs 36.389. Differences were due to fp32 summation order. - **-pm / --parallel_mode**: Deprecated (no-op). Parallel mode is now the default: multi-GPU quantization distributes one linear layer to each GPU at a time whenever a layer has at least as many tensors as there are devices. Layers with fewer tensors than devices (e.g. the lm_head, alone in its layer) fall back to splitting the trellis encoding workload across devices, with small tensors capped to as many devices as they can feed (~1M weights per device). #### Debug stuff (ignore these) - **-lcpi / --last_checkpoint_index *int***: If specified, don't save checkpoints after this module index. - **-cr / --cal_rows *int***: Number of rows of calibration data. Default is 250. - **-cc / --cal_cols *int***: Number of columns of calibration data. Default is 2048. - **-v / --verbose**: Extra debug output while quantizing. - **--override_anyway**: Allow resuming even when overriding settings that will break the existing job. - **-img / --image_dump**: Save all tensors as images in the working directory. May require a large amount of system memory and disk space, can be slow. - **--max_module *int***: End quantization after this many modules, including embedding and norm layers. ### Examples #### Converting ```sh python convert.py -i /mnt/models/llama3.1-70b-instruct \ -o /mnt/models/llama3.1-70b-instruct-exl3-3.75bpw \ -w /mnt/temp/exl3 \ -b 3.75 ``` #### Resuming Resume the job started above if it was interrupted: ```sh python convert.py -w /mnt/temp/exl3 -r ``` #### Multi-GPU quant Convert a model on the first three devices: ```sh python convert.py -i /mnt/models/llama3.1-70b-instruct \ -o /mnt/models/llama3.1-70b-instruct-exl3-3.75bpw \ -w /mnt/temp/exl3 \ -b 3.75 \ -d 0,1,2 ``` Convert a model on the first three devices, using CUDA:2 as the primary device. Also keep attention etc. in higher precision with `-hq`. Final model size will be slightly larger than the 4.00 bpw requested in this example, but since this is an MoE model, the increase will be on the order of 0.05 - 0.1 bpw: ```sh python convert.py -i /mnt/models/qwen3.5-35b-a3b \ -o /mnt/models/qwen3.5-35b-a3b-4.00bpw-plus \ -w /mnt/temp/exl3 \ -b 4.00 \ -hq \ -d 2,0,1 ``` # Environment variables Runtime and build-time toggles recognized by ExLlamaV3. All of these have sensible defaults; they exist mainly for A/B testing, debugging and working around platform quirks. Boolean-ish variables treat `0` as off and any other value as on unless noted. C++-side variables are read once (on first use) and cached; Python-side variables are read at import time. Either way, set them before loading a model. A few defaults differ between the CUDA and ROCm builds. They are collected in `exllamav3/util/backend.py` (the C++ side holds the same values where it reads a variable itself): | Variable | CUDA | ROCm | Why | |---|---|---|---| | `EXL3_QKV_SLICE` | `1` | `0` | the RDNA multi-matrix GEMV has no sliced form | | `EXL3_INT8_GEMV` | `2` | `0` | the RDNA fdot2 GEMVs are faster at these shapes | | `EXL3_MOE_FUSED_ROWS` | `128` | `512` | the RDNA fused MoE kernel tiles the rows itself | | `EXL3_MOE_BATCH_RECON` | `1` | `0` | same | | `EXL3_MOE_MTILE` | `1` | `0` | same (no 32 / 64-row instances of the RDNA kernel) | | `EXL3_HC_FOLD` | `0` | `1` | the per-launch cost dominates the mHC decode kernels on RDNA | ## Attention ### `EXL3_GRAPHS` (default: `1` on CUDA, `0` on ROCm) Whether the decode paths capture their kernel sequences into device graphs and replay them. With `0` every graphed site (attention, MLA, GDN, the MLP and block-sparse decode kernels) runs the same C++ launch sequence eagerly on each call, kernels and order unchanged, so the switch changes only host submission. On CUDA replay is a few percent faster at decode. On ROCm the HIP runtime launches a graph node no faster than a plain kernel, the measured difference is within noise, and every instantiated graph exec reserves a 2 MB device-side kernel-argument pool that grows with each parameter update until the exec is destroyed (an open HIP runtime issue); a speculative-decoding server holds hundreds of execs, so disabling graphs there also frees over a gigabyte of VRAM. Set `EXL3_GRAPHS=1` on ROCm to test the graph path. ### `EXL3_BC_ATTN` (default: `1`) Graph-captured C++ decode attention. For decode steps (bsz ≤ 8, q_len ≤ 16) the whole attention block -- q/k/v projections, fused head norm + RoPE, cache append, flash-decoding attention and o_proj -- runs as a single C++ call, captured as one CUDA graph per (bsz, q_len) shape and replayed with only the input/output/position/block-table pointers patched. Removes effectively all Python host time from the attention block; the largest gains are on host-bound setups (small or hybrid models, fast GPUs, contended CPUs). The same flag covers the equivalent path for MLA layers (BC_MLAttention: q projections, latent projection and staging, partial RoPE, W_UK absorption, cache append, absorbed flash-decoding, W_UV unfold and o_proj as one graph). Module or cache configurations the path does not support (TP, headwise gates, LayerNorm or span-heads head norms, non-EXL3 projections, compander-enabled quant cache, ...) fall back to the regular dispatch path by design. Unexpected errors while building the path are raised, not swallowed. Set to `0` to disable the path entirely. ### `EXL3_BC_ATTN_TRACE` (default: `0`) Print one line per attention module/cache-layer pair when the graph-captured decode path is built or declined (module key, device). Activation check for A/B tests: a benchmark comparing `EXL3_BC_ATTN` settings is only meaningful if the enabled run actually built the path. ### `EXL3_BC_DSA` (default: `1`) DeepSeek-V4 counterpart of `EXL3_BC_ATTN`: for decode steps (bsz 1, q_len ≤ 16) the whole DSA attention step runs as one graph-captured C++ call: the batched x-side projection fan, fused head-norm RoPE, both compressor updates (compressed/indexer entry pools and rings), the lightning-indexer scoring and capture-safe top-k selection (long-context regime), the flash-decoding sparse attention with fused output de-rotation, the grouped o_proj and the sliding-window ring append. One graph per (cache layer, job slot, q_len, dense/top-k regime); position flows through shared device scalars (one 8-byte host write per step per job) plus a handful of patched scalar node parameters (live pool width, causal bounds), so replays never rebuild anything. Steps that need a host-side window ring shift or rebase (page-granular, rare) decline to the eager path for that step, as do ineligible layer configurations (non-EXL3 projections, ...) permanently. Set to `0` to force the eager path everywhere. ### `EXL3_BC_MLA` (default: `1`) MLA counterpart of `EXL3_BC_ATTN`: decode steps (q_len ≤ 16) of an MLA layer run as one graph-captured C++ block (projections, absorb, latent attention, unfold, o_proj). Set to `0` to force the eager dispatch path, for A/B testing. ### `EXL3_MOE_SHARED_COOP` (default: `1`) MoE layers with a shared expert run it, at decode batch sizes, as a one-expert launch of the fused expert kernels (at the shared expert's own bit width) instead of the three separate GEMV launches of its own graph; the routed launch merges the result as before. Applies to EXL3-quantized gated shared experts with 128-aligned widths and no post-norm. Under tensor parallelism such a shared expert is placed whole on one rank (its contribution enters the all-reduce from that rank only) rather than split across ranks. Set to `0` to keep the separate graph and the tensor split. ### `EXL3_HC_FOLD` (default: `0` on CUDA, `1` on ROCm) Launch-count folds for the mHC hyper-connection sites (DeepSeek V4, GLM 5.3) at decode row counts (up to 32 rows): a site's residual update (`hc_apply`) is deferred and run inside the next site's mix kernel, and the RMSNorm that follows a mix runs inside the mix's finalize kernel, two launches fewer per site. The folded kernels repeat the unfused kernels' arithmetic in the same order, so the outputs are bit-identical either way (`tests/test_hc_fold.py`). On by default on ROCm, where the per-launch cost is a large part of these small kernels; `1` enables it on CUDA as well. ### `EXL3_GR_MIX_TILED` (default: `1`) Prefill-sized mixes of the Qwen3.8-style gated residual (`GatedResidual`, the low-rank hyper-connection) run a tiled CUDA kernel whose GEMMs use exact int8 tensor-core accumulation with a fixed fp32 combination, so the result is bit-identical on every GPU architecture and tensor-parallel ranks compute identical streams (a prerequisite for replicating decisions such as MoE routing across ranks instead of broadcasting them). Precision matches the fp16 path and it is faster than the cuBLAS path it replaces. Set to `0` to fall back to the cuBLAS GEMM path (device-dependent kernel choice, not rank-consistent). Decode-sized mixes use the fused `gr_mix` kernel either way. Both kernels read the one resident fp16 table set; the tiled path derives its int8 operands from it per call (a deterministic per-row split, so the same bytes every call), and the decode kernel takes the norm weight on the stream side, written by the preceding site's residual update. The same int8 scheme covers the MoE router projection for batched rows (`routing_gemm.cu`), which has no switch. On ROCm both kernels run on RDNA's int8 WMMA instructions (`rocm/det_gemm_rocm.cuh`). The per-chunk integer sums are exact and the fp32 combination keeps the same fixed order, so ranks agree across RDNA generations as well. ### `EXL3_GR_INT8` (fork default: `1`) Use the fork's derived int8 gated-residual projection tables for decode-sized mixes (up to eight rows) on supported CUDA shapes. This preserves the established GB10 decode path. It quantizes the norm-folded projection and repacked up table; this is a numerical approximation, so compare model logits and task quality when changing it. Set to `0` to use upstream's fp16 decode representation. Both settings retain upstream's **unfolded fp16** tables for deterministic tiled prefill. The int8 decode copies are additional resident memory, and never replace or fold the tables used by prefill. The weighted-stream handoff between adjacent sites is used only when the receiving site takes the fp16 decode path. Targeted merge checks are in `tests/test_spark_merge_gpu.py`; run the upstream fp16 parity tests with `EXL3_GR_INT8=0`. ### `EXL3_BC_GDN` (default: `1`) Gated-delta-net (Qwen3-Next/3.5, KDA in GLM-5.3/Kimi Linear) counterpart of `EXL3_BC_ATTN`: the decode step of a linear-attention layer runs as one graph-captured C++ call. Set to `0` to force the torch path, for A/B testing. ### `EXL3_GDN_SUB_CHUNK` (default: `2048`) Prefill of a gated-delta-net / KDA layer runs the fla chunked scan over consecutive sub-ranges of this many tokens, carrying the fp32 recurrent state between them. The projections and the convolution still run at the full chunk size; only the scan's temporaries (about 112 KB per token at 48 value heads of 128) shrink from the chunk to the sub-range, e.g. 1.5 GB to 0.7 GB per layer at a 16k chunk. The result is bit-identical to a single pass, and 2048 is also the fastest setting measured (1024 costs about 40% more on the scan). Set to `0` to run the whole chunk in one pass. ### `EXL3_GDN_PROJ_FP32` (default: `0`) Prefill of a gated-delta-net / KDA layer takes its q/k/v projection out of the GEMM in fp16 instead of fp32; the values are converted to bf16 for the recurrence either way, and the projections stay below |100| on natural text. Set to `1` for the fp32 projection (about 0.5-2.5e-3 relative difference in the layer's output, 1.5 GB less transient per 16k rows on GLM-5.3). ### `EXL3_GDN_GATE_FP32` (default: `1`) KDA's forget-gate and beta projections (the inputs to the per-channel decay) stay fp32. Set to `0` for fp16: 256 MiB less per 16k rows on GLM-5.3, about 1e-3 relative difference in the layer's output. ### `EXL3_GDN_CONV_TOKEN_MAJOR` (default: `1`) The prefill short convolution reads the projection output in place (token-major, any float dtype) instead of a transposed bf16 copy of it. Same kernel arithmetic; a bf16 projection is bit-identical to the copy, an fp16 one skips its bf16 rounding. Set to `0` for the copy. ### `EXL3_PLE_SUB_CHUNK` (default: `1024`) Prefill of the PLE (n-gram) layer runs its fused stream pass in row slabs of this many tokens, carrying the short-conv state between slabs and adding each slab's result into the stream in place. The pass holds several full-width fp32 working tensors of the stream stack so the slab bounds that. 1024 is the fastest slab measured. Set to `0` to run the chunk whole. ### `EXL3_BC_DSA_DEBUG` (default: `0`) Raise errors encountered while building the graphed DSA path instead of silently declining to the eager path. Diagnostic for why a configuration falls back. ### `EXL3_DSV4_NO_XFAN` (default: `0`) Set to disable the eager DSA path's projection fans. With the fans, the x-side projections that share the block input (q_a, wkv, and the compressor/indexer kv/gate pairs) run as a single per-matrix-N batched MGEMM, and q_b pairs with the indexer query projection over q_res the same way in the top-k regime: six to eight GEMV launches collapse into two. A/B switch for the eager path only; the graphed path (`EXL3_BC_DSA`) builds its own fan and is not affected. Layers whose projections mix quantization formats or bitrates decline the fan by themselves. ### `EXL3_QC_STAGING` (default: `1`) How quantized K/V caches feed the attention kernels (replaces the former `EXL3_QC_ATTN`). Only affects quantized caches. - `0` - no staging: packed cache tensors feed the prefill and decode kernels directly, with dequantization fused into the kernel loads. Lowest memory: no staging scratch is ever allocated, and nothing extra is reserved during autosplit loading. Prefill pays for the in-kernel expansion (roughly 5–25% on the attention kernel depending on bitrate and GPU), which every kv tile repeats once per query block and sibling query head. - `1` - prefill staging (default): prefill chunks of 256+ tokens dequantize the referenced cache window once into a shared fp16 scratch and run the fp16 kernel over it, putting quantized-cache prefill within ~1–3% of fp16. Decode stays on the direct path. The scratch is a per-call transient of one fixed size, the full cache at batch size 1 (`2 * max_num_tokens * num_kv_heads * head_dim` fp16 elements; QSA layers use their bounded dense window instead), so the allocator sees the same request on every prefill chunk rather than a size that grows with the context. The autosplit measuring pass budgets it, so the space is accounted for at load time; loads with an explicit split map it at the first long prefill. For very large caches this is the tradeoff to weigh against `0` (e.g. ~4 GB at 1M tokens with 8 kv heads of dim 128). - `2` - full staging: legacy dequantize-then-attend path; whole cache layers are expanded into full-size fp16 temporaries before attention. Debug/A-B mode (same effect as the former `EXL3_QC_ATTN=0`); only affects decode if `EXL3_BC_ATTN` is also disabled, since the graphed decode path reads the packed cache directly. ### `EXL3_QC_PF_TWO_PASS_MIN_Q` (default: `256`, `9` on Turing) Query-length threshold for the prefill staging pass at `EXL3_QC_STAGING=1`. Chunks shorter than this keep the direct path, which reads less global memory (relevant for short trailing chunks over long contexts at low cache bitrates). Where the staged window feeds SDPA (`EXL3_SDPA_PREFILL` below) every prefill chunk stages. Tuning/testing knob. ### `EXL3_SDPA_PREFILL` (default: on for sm_75, off elsewhere) Prefill chunks (more than 8 query rows, causal, no sliding window, softcap or sinks) attend over a dense fp16 copy of their window instead of running the Triton paged prefill kernel: the staged dequantization of a quantized cache, or the fp16 cache's own pages, viewed where they are consecutive and gathered otherwise, then PyTorch's memory-efficient SDPA per KV group (its cutlass kernel refuses `enable_gqa`). Triton emits no tensor-core instructions for sm_75, so the Triton kernel runs on scalar FMA there and prefill time grows steeply with context; the SDPA kernel keeps Turing's HMMA units busy. Decode and draft verification stay on the Triton kernels. `1` forces the path on any CUDA device (it is exact, so this is a testing switch), `0` forces it off on Turing. ### `EXL3_FA75` (default: on for sm_75, off elsewhere) On the path above, head_dim 256 attention runs the `fa75` kernel (`exllamav3_ext/turing/fa75.cu`, `mma.sync.m16n8k8`) instead of SDPA, whose cutlass kernel handles 256-wide heads at a fraction of its 128-wide rate. Plain PTX for sm_75 and later; `1` forces it on any CUDA device. ### `EXL3_QC_PREFILL_NS` (default: `0` = measure) Pipeline stage count for the direct quantized-cache prefill kernel. Unset/`0`, the best of {1, 2} is measured once per (shape family, device) at first use; a nonzero value pins it, skipping the measurement. Only relevant where the direct path still runs (`EXL3_QC_STAGING=0`, or short chunks below the threshold above). ### `EXL3_TRITON_SMEM_LIMIT` (default: unset) Caps the dynamic shared memory per block that the Triton attention kernels believe the device grants. The kernels' tile configs are sized for Ampere-class budgets; each launch site lists a ladder of smaller configs and takes the first whose compiled footprint fits the device (Turing: 64 KB). Setting a limit below the real one walks the ladders on any GPU, which is how the stepped-down configs are exercised without the smaller hardware. Note the picks then reflect this device's footprints, which differ from the target architecture's. Debugging only. ### `EXL3_TRITON_SMEM_DEBUG` (default: `0`) Prints every ladder probe (kernel, config, measured footprint, fits or over) and every graph kernel that declines to the eager path for lack of shared memory. ### `EXL3_MLA_PREFILL` (default: `mha`) Prefill strategy for MLA layers: `mha` up-projects past latent tiles from the compressed cache and attends in MHA form (~2.8× fewer FLOPs per query-past pair); `absorbed` restores the single-kernel absorbed-form prefill for A/B testing. ### `EXL3_PREFER_FA2` (default: `0`) Put the flash-attn-2 backends ahead of the built-in Triton attention kernels in the dispatch order. flash-attn is an optional dependency; when it is not installed, this switch is ignored (with a warning) and the built-in kernels serve everything.
